Ruth was not to be comforted. “That’s only a story. This is a bad dream that keeps coming back.”

===Chinese===

“你们一个个的，叫我怎么办才好？”汉娜愁苦地问。

“咱们本来过了一个多快乐的晚上，”露丝接着说。“快乐没有用。还是一直苦着倒好些。”

“比这更好的，还有勇敢。想想我和那个窃贼！”

露丝却怎么也哄不过来。“那不过是故事罢了。这个却是噩梦，一直缠着回来。”

===Original===

“My poor lamb,” Hannah said, “you shall sleep in my room if I have to sleep on the floor.”

“Can I?” It was pitiable to see how the sharp face softened.

“And you’d better be quick about it,” Wilfrid said through the open door. “I can hear the uncle marching up the path.”

Hannah paused to give him a nod of gratitude as she hurried Ruth up the stairs. There was nothing seriously wrong with that boy: he had the kindest heart in the house, but it was not the house he ought to have been in.

===Chinese===

“我可怜的小羊羔，”汉娜说，“你就睡到我屋里去，哪怕我自己睡地板。”

“我能吗？”那张尖脸软和下来，看得人心里发疼。

“你最好快着点儿，”威尔弗里德隔着敞开的门说。“我听见舅舅大踏步上小径来了。”

汉娜领着露丝匆匆上楼，中途停了停，朝他感激地点了点头。那孩子并没有什么大不了的毛病：全家数他的心最良善，只是这一户人家，本不该是他安身的地方。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===
Leaving a defiant maidservant and a nervous child on the top landing, Hannah descended to the one below, where she found Wilfrid waiting for her in his bedroom doorway and listening, between apprehension and amusement, to the sounds in Ethel’s room. These were the banging of drawers and the rattling of their handles under the shock, and Ethel’s footsteps thudding stubbornly across the floor, and Wilfrid made a gesture in their direction, and whispered, “Come in for a minute.”

“I can’t. I must get your uncle’s tea.”

“Let him wait! He’s been to the Spenser-Smiths’ and had a jolly good supper. He came back in their car. I heard it at the gate. He’ll be purring like a well-fed cat. As for you, Mona Lisa, you look like a stray one.”

“I do feel rather lost. I think I’ll ask Mr. Samson if he’s got room for me among the others.”

===Chinese===
汉娜把一个倔强不驯的女仆和一个惶惶不安的孩子留在顶层楼梯口，自己往下一层走去；威尔弗里德正等在自己卧室门口，听着一墙之隔埃塞尔房里的动静，又是心惊，又觉得有趣。那动静是抽屉摔得砰砰响、拉手震得咯咯响，还有埃塞尔执拗地踏过地板的脚步声；威尔弗里德朝那边做了个手势，悄声说：“进来待一会儿。”

“不行，我得去给你舅舅备茶。”

“让他等着去！他上斯宾塞-史密斯家吃了一顿好不痛快的晚饭，还是坐他们家的汽车回来的；我在大门口听见车响了。这会儿他准像一只喂饱了的猫，呜噜呜噜直打呼噜呢。至于你呀，蒙娜丽莎，你倒像一只走失的猫。”

“我也真觉得自己成了没着没落的了。我想我去问问桑普森先生，看他那一伙儿里还容得下容不下我。”

===Original===
“And it’s all my fault,” Wilfrid sighed, dramatically running his fingers through his hair. “But why the deuce shouldn’t I walk home with a girl? It’s no more than common courtesy. And if I held her hand a bit longer than was necessary, what’s that to Ethel?”

“Nothing at all I should think. Don’t distress yourself,” Hannah said coldly. “There’s been trouble with Doris.”

“All right, Mona Lisa, all right! I can see you’re thinking I’m a conceited puppy. Have it your own way. But that doesn’t account for her running like a hare when she saw me. And what’s Doris been doing?”

“Walking out with a young man, I believe.”

===Chinese===
“这就全怪我了，”威尔弗里德叹了口气，戏剧式地把手指插进头发里拢了拢。“可我究竟为什么不能送一位姑娘回家，见鬼？这不外乎寻常的礼貌罢了。就算我握她的手握得比必要久了那么一点儿，与埃塞尔何干？”

“依我看，毫不相干。别自己苦恼自己了，”汉娜冷冷地说。“是多丽丝出了乱子。”

“行行行，蒙娜丽莎，行！看得出，你在心里说我是只自命不凡的小蠢狗。随你怎么想。可这解释不了她一见我就逃得像只野兔呀。多丽丝又干了什么啦？”

“我听说，是跟一个小伙子相好上了。”

===Original===
“And I caught you flirting with old Samson! My word, we have been going it! Wouldn’t the uncle be pleased! He’s a bit morbid about intercourse between the sexes. Of course he approves of marriage, but the preliminaries make him sick. I call it an objectionable characteristic. How did he get married himself? But then, anything the uncle does is on a higher plane! I suppose,” he said slyly, “you haven’t noticed that?”

Hannah’s face lost all expression. That was her answer to Wilfrid and her own resistance to temptation. “And what about your own preliminaries, as you call them?” she asked. “Are you engaged to the young woman?”

“Engaged! Don’t be simple!”

“Well, in my young days, if we held hands we meant something by it.”

===Chinese===
“我还撞见你跟老桑普森调情呢！我的老天，咱们这一阵子可真够出格的！舅舅知道了该多高兴啊！他对男女之间的来往本就有点儿病态。婚姻他当然是赞成的，可婚前那点儿铺垫就叫他恶心。要我说，这毛病实在要不得。他自己当年是怎么结的婚？不过话又说回来，舅舅做什么都高人一等！我猜，”他狡黠地说，“这一点你还没留意到吧？”

汉娜的脸上霎时敛尽了表情。这就是她对威尔弗里德的回答，也是她自己抵御诱惑的法子。“那么你自己那套——照你的说法——铺垫呢？”她问。“你同那位小姐订婚了吗？”

“订婚！别犯傻了！”

“哼，在我年轻那会儿，我们若是握了手，总是有点意思在里头的。”

===Original===
“Oh, we meant something, I assure you. And how dull your young days must have been, poor Mona Lisa.”

“No,” said Hannah, “they weren’t, because I wasn’t. It isn’t the days that are dull, it’s the people who can’t see them properly. What on earth do you think I should do in this house if I couldn’t make amusement for myself?”

“Oh, come, I do my best, but you’ve got a professional conscience. I admire you for it, but it doesn’t deceive me. Not in the least. Let’s exchange views about the uncle, Mona Lisa. It would do us both all the good in the world.”

“I can’t stay long enough,” Hannah said. “But I’ll tell you one thing you’ll like.” She smiled at him very sweetly. “There’s a remarkably strong family likeness between you and him. Not in the face. In the character,” and with that, and a spiteful little grimace across her shoulder, she left him, and forgot her mendacious triumph as she heard Ethel shut her wardrobe door with a vicious bang.

===Chinese===
“哦，我向你担保，我们也有意思来着。再说，你年轻的日子想必闷得慌，可怜的蒙娜丽莎。”

“不，”汉娜说，“并不闷，因为我不闷。日子本身从不闷，闷的是那些不懂得看日子的人。你倒说说，我要是不会给自己找乐子，在这个家里还能干什么？”

“哦，得了，我也尽了力，可你带着一副职业良心。这个我佩服，只是它骗不了我，一点儿也骗不了。咱们来交换交换对舅舅的看法吧，蒙娜丽莎，这对咱们俩都大有好处。”

“我待不了这么久，”汉娜说。“不过有件事说出来你会爱听。”她朝他甜甜一笑。“你和他两个，家族相像得出奇。不在脸上。在性情上。”说完这句，又朝肩后扮了个恶毒的小鬼脸，她便离开了他；听见埃塞尔把衣柜门砰地狠命一摔，她那点靠撒谎得来的得意也忘了个干净。

===Original===
What was she to do with this family? she asked herself, as she ran down the stairs. And why should she stay? She was not so old or so useless that she could not get another post, but she remembered Ruth, snuggling under the bedclothes and saying childishly, “Whatever did I do without you?” and she remembered her compact with Mrs. Corder.

“I’ll make a job of it,” she said, rapidly preparing Mr. Corder’s tray, and she carried it into his study with secret pomp, as though, in this ritual, she dedicated herself to the family’s service.

Robert Corder made a handsome figure, standing on the hearthrug, with his head thrown back and some of the urbanity, fit for a visit to the Spenser-Smiths’, still beaming from him, and at the sight of him, Hannah’s faith in her resolutions failed her. She could deal with hysteria, she could help those she pitied, but, in the presence of this man, could she sustain her character of industrious nonentity? Something alive seemed to turn in her breast. It was the demon of mischief who lay there; he was stretching himself in lazy preparation for action and, if she was not careful, he would presently express himself in speech. Perhaps, she thought, a little, a very little, liberty would be good for him: if she kept him too quiet, he would suddenly get out of control, and there would be an end to her high endeavours for the family. The best thing, she decided swiftly, was to be natural: that would satisfy the demon and it was the golden rule for manners, but if she obeyed it at this moment, she would throw the teapot at Mr. Corder’s head. She had had a tiring day and he stood there, like a large, healthy animal waiting to be fed, and made no movement to relieve her of the tray.

===Chinese===
她奔下楼梯，一面自问：这一家人，教她拿他们怎么办？她又何必留下？她还没老到、也没无用到另谋一个位置都谋不到的地步；可她想起露丝往被窝里偎着、孩子气地说“没有你，我可怎么办呢”，又想起她对科德太太许下的盟约。

“那我就把它当作一桩正经事来办。”她说着，麻利地备好科德先生的茶盘，端进书房，怀着一股暗藏的堂皇，仿佛借着这个仪式，她把自己献给了这个家族的差事。

罗伯特·科德立在炉前地毯上，头往后仰着，为拜会斯宾塞-史密斯家备下的那点温文尔雅还在他周身焕发着光，好一副轩昂的气派；汉娜一见他这副模样，对自己决心的信心先自泄了气。歇斯底里她应付得了，她怜悯的人她帮得了；可在这个男人面前，她那副勤恳庸碌的角色还撑得下去么？一样活物仿佛在她胸口翻了个身。那是伏在那儿的促狭魔鬼；它正懒洋洋地舒展筋骨，预备动作，她一不留神，它马上就要借她的嘴冒出声来。她想，或许给它一点儿自由——很少的一点儿——倒对它有益：管得它太安静，它会突然脱缰，她为这个家立下的宏愿也就完了。她旋即拿定主意：最好自然些——这样既喂饱了魔鬼，也是礼貌的黄金法则；可这一刻若真依了它，她就该把茶壶照着科德先生的脑袋摔过去了。她累了一整天，他却站在那儿，像一头等着喂食的肥壮牲口，一动也不动，没打算替她卸下茶盘。

===Original===
“If you would kindly take those books off the table,” she said politely, “I shall be able to put this down.”

He looked at his watch before he obliged her. “Half-past ten,” he said.

“Is that all?” Hannah said pleasantly. “I thought it was about eleven,” and before he had time to make one of the several retorts that must have occurred to him, she exclaimed, “And now I’ve forgotten the biscuits!”

===Chinese===
“劳驾把桌上那些书挪一挪，”她客客气气地说，“我好用地方搁这个。”

他先看了看表，才给她让开地方。“十点半，”他说。

“就这么晚？”汉娜愉快地说。“我还当有十一点了呢。”还没等他从肚里备下的好几句回敬里挑出一句来，她就嚷起来：“哎呀，我把饼干给忘了！”

===Original===
“Don’t trouble about them, Miss Mole. I had supper with Mr. and Mrs. Spenser-Smith.”

She recognised the cue for a murmur of congratulation or envy, but she chose to miss it. “Then, if you don’t want them, I’ll say good night, Mr. Corder.”

“Just a moment, Miss Mole. Mrs. Spenser-Smith expressed some surprise that you had not been to see her. I think it would be courteous to pay her that attention.”

“Then I’ll try to pop in some afternoon, when I’m having a walk.”

===Chinese===
“饼干就不必费心了，莫尔小姐。我在斯宾塞-史密斯夫妇家用的晚饭。”

她听出了这句提词——照着戏文，此处该低声道贺或者表示艳羡——她偏要错过。“那么，您既用过了，我就道晚安了，科德先生。”

“稍待片刻，莫尔小姐。斯宾塞-史密斯太太说，你还没去拜望过她，颇感诧异。依我看，这份礼数你还是尽到的好。”

“那我往后散步的哪个下午，顺道进去坐坐吧。”

===Original===
Robert Corder’s quick little frown, like Ruth’s, but different in its causes, came and went. “Her At Home day is the first Friday in the month.”

“Does she have an At Home day?” Hannah asked with a wide smile. “I thought that was unfashionable. Then I can’t go till December.”

“You misunderstand me,” he said gently. “It might be better for you to avoid that day.”

“Yes, they’re dreary occasions, aren’t they? Thank you for telling me. Good night.”

===Chinese===
罗伯特·科德的眉头轻轻一皱——那眉样同小露丝的一般无二，缘由却不一样——旋即舒展开来。“她家的会客日（At Home day）是每月第一个星期五。”

“她家还设会客日？”汉娜咧开大嘴一笑。“我还当这早就不时兴了。这么说，我得等到十二月才能去了。”

“你误会了我的意思，”他温和地说。“你还是避开那一天为好。”

“是啊，那种场面闷煞人，不是么？多谢你指点。晚安。”

===Original===
She knew she would be called back when she reached the door.

“Another thing, Miss Mole. I shall be asking a gentleman to supper next week. I think Thursday would be the best day. He has just taken up the ministry of Highfield Chapel﻿—a small place, but I suppose he considers it promotion﻿—and I feel we ought to do what we can to welcome him. You will bear that in mind, won’t you?”

“Yes,” Hannah said. “Do you want anything special to eat?”

===Chinese===
她知道，等她走到门口，自己还会被叫回来的。

“还有一件事，莫尔小姐。下星期我要请一位先生来吃晚饭，我想星期四最合适。他刚接了海菲尔德礼拜堂（Highfield Chapel）的牧师职——地方不大，不过我猜在他看来总算是升迁了——我觉得咱们理应尽力欢迎他。这一层你会记在心上，是不是？”

“好，”汉娜说。“吃的东西可要特别预备些什么？”

===Original===
“I can safely leave that to you, I’m sure. In fact, I think I ought to say how much I appreciate the care you have given to our meals.”

“I’m glad you’re pleased,” she said sincerely. If he was not obtuse, he was generous, and she smiled as she spoke; then her eyes, leaving his face, fell on that of Mrs. Corder who was listening attentively to all they said, and Hannah persuaded herself that Mrs. Corder was glad to think of Ruth, upstairs in the dovecot, and trusted Hannah to do what she could for Ethel. “And, by the way,” Hannah said, “is the minister married?”

Mr. Corder’s annoyance expressed itself in a somewhat sickly smile. “Always the first question!” he exclaimed. “But is it of any real importance to you, Miss Mole?”

===Chinese===
“这桩事交给你，我尽管放心。说实在的，我觉得我该说一句：你替咱们的饭菜费了多少心思，我心里很是领情。”

“您满意，我就高兴。”她说得诚心诚意。他若不是迟钝，便是慷慨，说这话时她面带笑意；随后她的目光离开他的脸，落到那帧科德太太的照片上，照片里的人仿佛正凝神细听他们说的每一个字。汉娜便说服自己相信：科德太太想到露丝正在楼上鸽舍里安睡，又想到汉娜必会尽力照拂埃塞尔，心里一定是欢喜的。“对了，顺便问一句，”汉娜说，“那位牧师结了婚没有？”

科德先生的恼怒化作一抹有几分僵苦的笑。“开口头一句准是这个问题！”他喊道。“可这对你当真要紧吗，莫尔小姐？”

===Original===
“Of great importance,” she replied, “because I suppose, if he is, he will bring his wife.”

Robert Corder turned away quickly. “No, no, he’s not married,” he said.

She looked at his back almost tenderly. The poor man could not open his mouth without betraying himself and though she had said nothing at which he could reasonably take offence, perhaps she had given him something to think about, and her demon had had his little outing and she had a soothing draught to offer Ethel when she carried up a cup of hot milk and knocked at her door.

===Chinese===
“要紧得很，”她答道，“因为我想，他要是结了婚，总要把太太一并带来的。”

罗伯特·科德赶紧扭身背对着她。“没有，没有，他没结婚，”他说。

她望着他的背影，几乎怀着怜爱。这可怜人一开口就藏不住自己；虽然她没说过一句他可以理直气壮动怒的话，却也许给了他一点可供琢磨的东西；而她那促狭魔鬼也出去小小游玩了一遭；待她端着一杯热牛奶上楼去敲埃塞尔的房门时，她正有一服镇定的汤剂可以奉上。

===Original===
It was a little while before she gained admittance. She had the impression that everything Ethel possessed was being hurriedly concealed in drawers and cupboards and, when she entered, there were still signs of disorder in the room.

“Don’t worry about Doris,” she said at once. “I’ll have a few words with her in the morning. It will be easier for me than for you. And drink this while it’s hot.” She tried to avoid looking at Ethel’s scarred face, but Ethel showed no more shame than she had shown control. “I’m all in favour of walking out, but I should like to know something about the young man.”

“She ought to have told me!” Ethel exclaimed. “I’ve been so good to her!”

“Yes,” Hannah said, “it’s a mistake to be good to people, if you’re hoping for reward, because you won’t get it. It would have been better for both of you if you’d tried to train her. You’ll feel ashamed of her, tomorrow week, when she slams the dishes on the table. Did you know your father is asking a minister to supper?”

===Chinese===
敲了好一会儿才蒙准进门。她的感觉是，埃塞尔的每一样东西都正被慌慌张张塞进抽屉和柜橱；待她进了屋，房里仍然处处留着零乱的痕迹。

“多丽丝的事你不必发愁，”她立刻说。“明儿早晨我找她说几句话。这事由我出面，总比由你出面来得方便。把这个趁热喝了。”她竭力不去看埃塞尔那张带疤的脸；可埃塞尔不曾在方才发作时有半点克制，此刻也不见有半点羞惭。“出门相好，我举双手赞成；只是那个年轻人是个什么样的人，我也想知道些。”

“她早该告诉我！”埃塞尔嚷道。“我一直待她那么好！”

“是啊，”汉娜说，“对人好是个错，若你指望报答，因为你是得不到的。你要是当初想法子调教调教她，对你们俩反倒都好些。等到下个星期，她把菜盘往桌上砰砰一摔，你就该替她害臊了。你可知道，你爸爸要请一位牧师来吃晚饭？”

===Original===
Ethel, who had been restive under reproof, rolling her eyes and threatening to bolt, steadied herself as Hannah produced this carrot. “A minister! Who is it?”

“I don’t know,” Hannah said carelessly. “Some young man who’s got a new cure of souls﻿—if you have such things in your denomination. Anyhow, I hope he’ll cure them.”

“Then it must be the new minister at Highfield Chapel.”

“That’s the man. We shall have to kill the fatted calf, I suppose,” Hannah said, and she wished it was possible to put a love potion into the ginger-beer which was the Corders’ festal beverage. Ethel loved, and married to a minister, would be a useful member of society, and he must have his fatted calf, so he must think Ethel had cooked it and, in the meantime, a week of tranquillity for the family was assured.

===Chinese===
埃塞尔方才挨了训斥，正不服气，翻着白眼，一副要夺门而逃的架势；汉娜抛出这根胡萝卜，她便定住了。“一位牧师！是哪一位？”

“我不知道，”汉娜满不在乎地说。“一位年轻先生，新得了医治灵魂的圣职——你们那一派里若有这样的名目的话。反正，我盼着他真能把它们医治好。”

“那准是海菲尔德礼拜堂新来的牧师。”

“正是此人。咱们恐怕得宰一头肥牛犊了，”汉娜说。她心想，若能往那姜汁啤酒——科德家的节庆饮料——里掺进一剂媚药，该有多好。埃塞尔有了爱情，再嫁给一位牧师，便是社会上一名有用的成员；牧师必须吃到他的肥牛犊，那就得让他相信牛犊是埃塞尔亲手烧的；而与此同时，这一家一星期的太平也就保定了。

===Original===
An hour later she lay in Ruth’s bed, considering the events of the past day. She thought of them, one by one, extracting from them all their savour, whether sweet or bitter. There was her walk on the hill overlooking the water, with the bright tree showing through a grey mist which seemed to darken when the wings of a swooping gull flashed through it: there was the sound of unseen ships hooting or booming at the turn of the river and, at her will, she had been able to imagine them as huge amphibians, calling to each other as they floundered in the water and sought the hidden banks, or she could acknowledge them as the sirens of ships which were coming home from distant places or setting out on fresh voyages, and standing up there with the soft rain on her face, she had marvelled at the richness of human life in which imagination could create strange beasts though facts were sufficient in themselves, while she, who had the privilege of these experiences, had no ache or pain in the whole of her lithe body and no more troubles than were good for her.

She had a feeling of sovereignty while she stood there; she could make what she liked of her world. She was more than a sovereign; she was a magician, changing ships into leviathans with some tiny adjustment of her brain, and, in addition, she had a freedom such as, surely, no one else in all Radstowe could claim, for she was in possession of herself and did not set too great a value on it.

In this high mood, she had swung down from her perch above the rocks and kept her fine content until she came upon Ruth, near Regent Square, and remembered her old ulster and realized, with a pang, that a part of her belonged to Ruth. She had given it willingly and could not withdraw it and she had increased her gift before the day was over.

It had been the most eventful day of her sojourn with the Corders and, in an existence like hers, where excitements outside herself came seldom, it seemed wasteful to have such a walk, to seal her friendship with Ruth, interview Mr. Blenkinsop, witness Ethel’s abandonment and Doris’s impudence, talk to Mr. Samson over the hedge and get a compliment from Robert Corder and news about a minister, in one day.

===Chinese===
一个钟头以后，她躺在露丝的床上，回味这一天的事。她把它们一件一件想过，把它们其中的滋味，不管是甜是苦，一样一样咂摸出来。有她在山冈上那趟俯瞰水面的散步：灰蒙蒙的雾里透出一棵亮闪闪的树，一只疾扑而下的海鸥掠雾而过，翅膀一闪，雾仿佛随之转暗；有看不见的轮船在河流转弯处鸣笛、低吼的声音。她随自己的心意，把它们想象成巨大的两栖巨兽，在水中扑腾挣扎、彼此呼唤着寻觅隐蔽的河岸；她也不妨承认，那不过是轮船的汽笛，那些船正从远方归家，或是启程远航。她站在那里，柔雨拂面，惊叹人生的丰盈：事实本身原已足够，想象偏还能造出怪兽来；而享有这些体验的她，轻盈的身体从头到脚没有一丝酸痛，烦恼也恰恰只有于人有益的那一点点。

她站在那儿的时候，怀有一种君临之感；她的世界，她爱怎么造就怎么造。她不止是君王，她还是魔术师，脑子里轻轻一拨，就能把轮船变作庞然大物；不但如此，她还有一份自由，全拉德斯托怕是再没有第二个人敢夸口也有——因为她拥有自己，并且并不把这拥有看得太重。

怀着这股豪兴，她从岩顶上方的立足处一路轻快地荡下来，那份圆满的快意一直保持到在摄政广场附近撞见露丝；她想起自己那件旧阿尔斯特长大衣，心头一酸，这才明白自己有一部分已经归了露丝。那件大衣她是甘心情愿给的，收不回来了；而且不等这一天过完，她的赠与又添了码。

这是她客居科德家以来最多事的一天。像她这样的一生，外来的兴奋难得光临，一天之内竟摊上这么一趟散步，同露丝缔结了友情，拜会了布伦金索普先生，目睹了埃塞尔的失态与多丽丝的放肆，隔着树篱同桑普森先生交谈，还从罗伯特·科德那里得了一句夸奖、得了一位牧师的消息——未免太奢侈了。

===Original===
“This is extravagance,” she murmured.

There were no more sounds from Ethel’s room, and as Hannah turned on her other side to sleep, she saw that the door communicating with Robert Corder’s room was framed in gold. Then that border slowly changed its shape, widening at the top and side, and his figure was silhouetted against the light. She lay still and stiff and shut her eyes. She heard him advance a step and felt his silent, swift retreat. He shut the door as quietly as he had opened it and the gold band was round it, as though it had never stirred.

“What will he make of this?” she wondered, pressing her mouth against the pillow. There would be trouble in the morning, but it would be something to tell Lilla when she paid her call, avoiding the first Friday of the month. Yes, with some amplifications, it would make a very good story, and while she amplified it and planned a fit and reasonable reply to any complaint Robert Corder might make, she felt a new kindness for a man who could steal into a room so gently to look at his little daughter.

===Chinese===
“这可真是挥霍，”她低声说。

埃塞尔房里再没有声响。汉娜翻了个身侧躺欲睡，看见通罗伯特·科德房间的那扇门四周镶着一圈金光。随后那道金边缓缓变了形状，上边和旁边都展宽了，他的身影衬着灯光显现出来。她直挺挺躺着，一动不动，闭上了眼睛。她听见他向前迈了一步，又觉出他悄然迅疾地退了回去。他把门带上，同开时一样轻；金边仍旧围着门，仿佛从来不曾动过。

“这个他会怎么看呢？”她纳闷着，嘴抵住枕头。明儿早晨免不了一场风波，可这正好是去看莉拉时讲给她听的一件好材料——登门时避开每月第一个星期五便是。是啊，稍加渲染，它就是一个顶好的故事。她一面渲染着，一面预备好一套又合宜又在理的答话，来应对罗伯特·科德可能发出的任何抱怨；心里却对那个男人生出一种新的柔情——为了看一眼他的小女儿，他竟能把溜进房来的动作放得这样轻。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===

The shadow that fell on Hannah the next morning was not the one of Robert Corder’s displeasure. It was a darker one that hung over her all the week and, on the evening of the supper-party, she slipped out of the house and walked swiftly up the road. At the top of it, she stood still and, drawing a deep breath, looked back. The road was empty. There was nothing to be seen, and she had not expected to see anything, except the lighted windows of the houses and the street lamps which stood like sentinels who were tired of keeping unnecessary watch and did not recognise a fugitive in Miss Mole, and there was no one to notice her as she paused with her back against the railings of a garden and a wry smile on her lips. She was thinking that, until this moment, she had not run away from anything since the days when she had believed in the clever bear and pretended a wolf was coming after her.

“Drat the man!” she said, recovering her jauntiness.

She had shut her door in his face, ten years ago, and that was a heartening memory, and if she avoided him now it was for reasons which he would be the last person to understand. And, if she had stayed, what a pleasantly-shocking little talk he and Robert Corder, closeted in the study, would have had about her! She could imagine the shakings of heads over Mr. Pilgrim’s revelations, the pursings of lips, and Robert Corder’s sudden, angry realisation that he would have to face an awkward situation. She had spared him that, for the present, and it had been easier than she dared to hope when the name of Mr. Pilgrim fell blastingly on her ears. It was Mr. Pilgrim for whom she had been so ready to kill the fatted calf﻿—but it was she who was the prodigal! It was he who was the eligible bachelor of her hopes and, characteristically, she had time to be sorry for Ethel before she began to consider herself. Even Ethel could hardly be enthusiastic about Mr. Pilgrim after she had seen him, and Ruth and Wilfrid were already in dismay at the prospect of an evening with a strange minister. They enraged Ethel with their prophecies of how he would look and what he would say and, in spite of Hannah’s distress, she longed to prompt them. They wondered if he would be content to discuss ministerial affairs with Robert Corder, a possibility at which Ethel rolled anxious eyes, or whether they would be expected to help in his entertainment, and it was then that Wilfrid opened the door for Hannah by thanking his God they had her to rely on.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨罩到汉娜头上的阴影，并不是罗伯特·科德不悦的阴影。那是更暗的一团，整整一个礼拜悬在她头顶；到了宴客的那晚，她溜出宅门，脚下生风地沿街而上。走到坡顶，她站住了，深深吸一口气，回头望去。街上空无一人。什么也看不见，她也本没指望看见什么，无非是各家亮着灯的窗，和那一盏盏街灯——它们立在那儿像哨兵，却早已厌倦了这桩多余的守望，并不曾在莫尔小姐身上认出个逃犯来；她背靠着一家园子的栅栏、嘴角挂着一丝苦笑驻足的那一会儿，也没有谁来留意她。她心里正想着：直到此刻为止，她还不曾打什么跟前逃开过——自打那些她相信聪明的熊、假装有狼追在身后的童年日子以来。

“呕，这该死的男人！”她说，那股满不在乎的欢腾劲儿又回到身上。

十年前，她曾当面砰地关上门，把这个男人拒在门外，这段回忆至今想来提神；如今她躲着他，自有其中的道理，而他正是天底下最不可能懂得这些道理的人。再说，她要是留下来，他和罗伯特·科德关起书房门来，关于她，还不知要有一番多么惊险有趣的小谈话呢！她想象得出：听着皮尔格林先生（Mr. Pilgrim）的种种披露，两颗脑袋如何摇个不停，两张嘴如何抿得铁紧，罗伯特·科德又如何在蓦然之间又惊又怒地醒悟：他得面对一桩尴尬局面了。这一层的难堪，她暂时替他免了；自打皮尔格林先生的名字轰然砸进她的耳朵，事情竟一路顺当，顺当得超出了她敢盼望的地步。她预备宰了肥牛犊去款待的，原是皮尔格林先生——谁知道浪子竟是她自己！他才是她指望中的合意单身汉。而她一副老脾气，先来得及替埃塞尔难过，才轮到盘算自己。便是埃塞尔，亲眼见过皮尔格林先生之后，也难说对他热心得起来；露丝和威尔弗里德一想到要陪一位陌生牧师过一晚上，早已叫苦不迭。他们预言他会生什么模样、会说什么话，把埃塞尔惹恼了；汉娜虽则自身烦恼，却听得心痒难搔，恨不得从旁提词。他们猜他肯不肯只同罗伯特·科德谈谈教务便心满意足——埃塞尔一想到这种可能，急得眼珠直转——又猜会不会还要他们帮着张罗款待；恰在此时，威尔弗里德感谢他的上帝，说他们还有她可以倚仗——这就替汉娜把门打开了。

===Original===

“And if he’s not the man I take him for, he’ll think she’s a rare bird to find in Beresford Road. But if he is, and I’m afraid he is, then it will be she who’ll get all the fun. So you’ll enjoy yourself, Mona Lisa, if no one else does, and you must do your best for us.”

Ethel was affronted as, no doubt, Wilfrid meant her to be, and her anger against him, assuaged by the imminence of Mr. Pilgrim, returned in force. She had been keeping her father’s house, she reminded him, for the last two years and this was not the first time they had received a guest. He was trying to make Miss Mole think she did not know how to behave﻿—he was always praising someone to annoy somebody else﻿—and, as a matter of fact, it would be easier for her without Miss Mole. It was very difficult to be the hostess when there was another woman there, older than oneself. It made her nervous. They had had the Spenser-Smiths to supper and Mrs. Spenser-Smith had said how nice everything had been.

“That was because she talked all the time. This Pilgrim fellow may stammer, or something, and then the uncle will have to do the talking and that’ll be the last visit Pilgrim pays here. You mark my words! It’s better to have Miss Mole in the hand than to drive Pilgrim into the bush.”

===Chinese===

“就算他不是我所想的那种人，他也会觉得她是贝雷斯福德路上难得撞见的一只稀罕鸟。可他要是呢——我怕他就是——那所有乐子便都归了她。所以，蒙娜丽莎，就算旁人都快活不成，你横竖快活得成，你得替我们卖力啊。”

埃塞尔着了恼——不用说，这正是威尔弗里德存心的效果——她对他的怒气，先前因皮尔格林先生大驾将临才勉强按住，这会儿大张旗鼓地卷土重来。两年来一直是她在操持父亲的家，她提醒他道；招待客人，这也不是头一回了。他这是存心做给莫尔小姐看，显得她不懂规矩似的——他惯会夸一个来气另一个——何况说实在的，没有莫尔小姐，她反倒省力。屋里另有一个人，又比自己年长，女主人的角儿就难唱了。这叫她心慌。他们请斯宾塞-史密斯家吃过晚饭，斯宾塞-史密斯太太还夸样样都好。

“那是因为她一个人说个没完。这个姓皮尔格林的家伙，兴许要结巴，或者来点别的毛病，那就得我舅舅一个人撑着说话，皮尔格林下一回也就不上这个门了。你们记着我的话！莫尔小姐攥在手里，强似把皮尔格林轰进林子。”

===Original===

“Oh, how horrid you are!” Ethel cried. “Of course he doesn’t stammer. How could he preach?”

“Perhaps he can’t.”

“And you’re disgusting about Father, and perfectly ridiculous about Miss Mole.” Ethel’s voice was getting beyond control. “Why should you think she’s such a brilliant conversationalist? I’ve never noticed it.”

“Ah, she’s like old Samson’s parrot. She can do it when she chooses. But then, I know I’m prejudiced about her.”

===Chinese===

“哦，你真可恶！”埃塞尔嚷道。“他当然不结巴。不然他还怎么讲道？”

“也许讲不成呢。”

“你说爸爸的话叫人恶心，说莫尔小姐的话更是荒唐。”埃塞尔的声音快管不住了。“你凭什么认定她是个谈锋那么健的人？我从没瞧出来。”

“咳，她像老桑普森那只鹦鹉，高兴起来这门本事是有的。不过话说回来，我知道我对她偏心。”

===Original===

Hannah raised her head from her darning and looked coolly from one to the other. “I don’t know what your manners will be like on Thursday,” she said quietly, “but I hope they won’t be what they are now. You needn’t continue the discussion because I happen to be engaged that evening.”

“Oh, I say, what luck! Can’t you be engaged to me?”

“But, Miss Mole﻿—” Ethel began and her face was stilled by her rapid calculations.

===Chinese===

汉娜从织补的活计上抬起头，冷冷地看看这个，又看看那个。“我不知道你们到了星期四会是什么规矩样，”她不动声色地说，“只盼别是眼下这副样子。这场讨论不必再往下进行了，因为我碰巧那天晚上另有约定。”

“哦，我说，好运气！那你就不能跟我订婚吗？”

“可是，莫尔小姐——”埃塞尔刚一开口，飞快的盘算便把她脸上的神色凝住了。

===Original===

“If you’re thinking about the cooking, you needn’t worry about that. I shall leave the supper prepared and, as you say, it will be easier for you without me, and for everybody else.”

“It won’t. It’ll be much worse,” Ruth muttered, and Ethel, preoccupied with a new thought, could only dart a glance at this second ally of Miss Mole’s before she asked﻿—“But what will Father think?”

“I don’t know,” Hannah replied simply.

===Chinese===

“你若是在想做饭的事，那不必操心。晚饭我会先备齐；再说，就像你讲的，没有我，你省力，大家也都省力。”

“省不了。只会糟得多，”露丝咕哝道。埃塞尔正被一个新念头占住了心神，只朝莫尔小姐这第二个同盟军飞快瞟了一眼，便问道——“可爸爸会怎么想？”

“我不知道，”汉娜老老实实地答。

===Original===

“And I don’t mean to be rude, Miss Mole﻿—”

“But you succeeded,” Hannah said.

“I’m sorry, Miss Mole. It was only because Wilfrid made me so angry. And I don’t think Father will like it. And suppose Doris makes mistakes?”

“She will,” Hannah said pleasantly, and she could have added the assurance that Mr. Pilgrim would not know.

===Chinese===

“我不是存心失礼，莫尔小姐——”

“可你成功了，”汉娜说。

“对不起，莫尔小姐。都怪威尔弗里德把我气成那样。再说，爸爸不会乐意的。再说，多丽丝要是出了错怎么办？”

“她会的，”汉娜和颜悦色地说。她本还可以添一句保票：皮尔格林先生瞧不出来。

===Original===

It was clear that her mind was made up and though Robert Corder resented Miss Mole’s engagement, in a place unnamed, and let her know it, he also let her know, but without intention, that he was secretly relieved, for a housekeeper, like a son at Oxford, was a good thing to mention casually, but an irritation in the flesh, when she happened to have a trick of misinterpreting the most obvious remarks. It was this which had obliged him to ignore his discovery of her in Ruth’s bedroom and he felt mentally securer when she was out of the house.

She could divine all this with the acuteness which was partly natural and partly an acquired habit of self-defence, she could see him on the brink of asking what her engagement might be and retiring, as usual, under cover of some unnecessary orders, and now, just before the guest was due, she had run up the street to find a temporary refuge with Mrs. Gibson.

As she turned into Prince’s Road she reflected sagely on the sequence of events and the difficulty of deciding that this one was good and that one evil. If she had not deceived Mrs. Widdows and gone out to buy the reel of silk, she could not have saved Mr. Ridding’s life, and while Mr. Blenkinsop seemed to regret this preservation, it had been the means of providing Hannah with a shelter in her time of need. It was impossible to please everybody, and even the menace of Mr. Pilgrim, which had been darkening her life for the past week, might prove to be one of those clouds with a silver lining, but no one could really know until the end of time, when each little action and its consequence would be balanced in the scales, and she was sure the adjudicator would not be aggrieved, though he might be astonished, at the result. She was conscious in herself of a tolerance which must be a dim reflection of a greater one; she refused to be harsher with herself than she was with other people or than her vague but tender God was with all the world, and she had recovered her spirits when she saw the lighted window of Mrs. Ridding’s basement kitchen and rang the bell of Mrs. Gibson’s front door.

===Chinese===

明摆着，她主意已定。罗伯特·科德对莫尔小姐那个地点不明的约满心不悦，也让她知道了他的不悦；可他同样让她知道了——虽说并非有意——他暗地里松了一口气：一个管家妇，好比一个在牛津念书的儿子，是闲谈里提起来够体面的名目，却也是肉里的一根刺，倘若她偏偏惯会把最明白不过的话听出岔来的话。正是为了这个，他只得装作没发现她在露丝卧房里的那桩事；她不在家里，他心神便安稳几分。

这一切她都猜得出，凭的是那份机敏——一半出自天性，一半是自卫防身练出来的积习。她看得见他话已到嘴边、想问问她约的究竟是什么，又照例缩了回去，拿几道多余的吩咐遮掩过去；而此刻，客人将到未到，她已沿街奔上来，投奔吉布森太太，寻一个临时的避难所。

转进王子路时，她一副贤哲气派，把这一连串事情的因果想了一遍，想着要断定此事为善、彼事为恶是何等之难。她若不曾哄骗威多斯太太、出去买那绞丝线，便救不得里丁先生的命；布伦金索普先生对这条命得以保全，瞧着倒像颇以为憾，可正是这件事，在汉娜急难之际给了她一处庇身之所。要叫人人都称心，是办不到的；就连皮尔格林先生这桩威胁——它把她整整一个礼拜的天色都压暗了——没准儿也是一朵镶了银边的云，只是谁也说不真切，非得等到时间的尽头，那时每桩小小的行止连同它的后果都要摆上天平去称；她确信那位裁判官见了结果不至于恼怒，虽说说不定要吃一惊。她自觉胸中有一副宽容，那想必是更大的宽容漏下的一点微光；她不肯待自己比待旁人更苛刻，也不肯比她那位模糊然而温柔、庇着普天下的上帝更苛刻。想到这里，她的兴致已经回来：她望见里丁太太家地下厨房那扇亮着灯的窗，伸手按响了吉布森太太的门铃。

===Original===

There was always a strangely muffled feeling in that house. If there was still trouble in the basement, it did not penetrate into Mrs. Gibson’s comfortably-furnished rooms, and as Hannah ate her supper and listened to Mrs. Gibson’s gentle and contented talk, she felt as though she were under the influence of some mild narcotic. Mrs. Gibson’s voice rose only when she pressed Hannah to eat. She thought Miss Mole was looking tired. She had been out to choose the chicken herself and Miss Mole must eat as much as she could.

“You’re a lady, Mrs. Gibson, if ever there was one,” Hannah said. “I don’t know anybody else who would have taken so much trouble for me.”

“Oh, my dear!” Mrs. Gibson exclaimed. It saddened and flattered her to think this was true.

===Chinese===

那幢房子里总有一种奇异的、捂得严严实实的发闷气息。就算底下那层另有风波，也渗不进吉布森太太这些陈设得舒舒服服的屋子。汉娜吃着晚饭，听吉布森太太温吞而餍足地絮语，只觉得自己像给一种温和的麻醉剂熏着了。吉布森太太的嗓门，只有在劝汉娜多吃的时候才提起来。她说莫尔小姐瞧着累了。鸡是她亲自出去挑的，莫尔小姐务必能吃多少就吃多少。

“您真算得上一位太太，吉布森太太，天底下要是真有太太的话，”汉娜说。“肯为我费这么大心思的，我认得的人里再挑不出第二个。”

“哎哟，我的乖！”吉布森太太嚷了一声。这话是真情，想起来又叫她难过，又叫她受用。

===Original===

“Yes,” Hannah went on, “if Mrs. Spenser-Smith had asked me out to supper, she would have given me yesterday’s mutton hashed. Quite good enough for Miss Mole! She’d keep her chickens for the people who could afford them. It’s the way of the world, but you don’t belong to it. You ought to be in Heaven, Mrs. Gibson, and I hope you won’t go yet. You had all this to see to and Mr. Blenkinsop’s dinner as well.”

Mrs. Gibson nodded her head in satisfaction. “Mr. Blenkinsop was very obliging when I told him you were coming. He said he’d have his dinner early and then it would be out of the way.”

“Ah,” said Hannah, “he was afraid I’d take it up to him!”

“I don’t know, dear. He’s got a kind heart, really. What do you think he did on Sunday? Took Mr. Ridding off for a walk in the country!”

===Chinese===

“可不是，”汉娜接着说，“换作斯宾塞-史密斯太太请我出去吃晚饭，端出来的准是昨儿剩的羊肉，剁碎了回锅。给莫尔小姐吃，很够好了！她的鸡要留给出得起的人家。世道就是如此，可您不属于这个世道。您本该待在天堂里，吉布森太太，不过我盼着您先别动身。您这儿一大摊子要张罗，还得管布伦金索普先生的饭呢。”

吉布森太太满意地点点头。“我说您要来，布伦金索普先生可随和了。他说他把饭吃早点，就不碍事了。”

“嘿，”汉娜说，“他是怕我把饭给他端上楼去呢！”

“哪儿的话，乖。他心肠其实好得很。你猜他星期天做了什么？带里丁先生下乡散步去了！”

===Original===

“And lost him?” Hannah suggested.

“No, dear. Mr. Blenkinsop isn’t the man to lose things. He’s very careful. If there’s a collar missing, he knows it, and he’ll get Sarah to tighten up his trouser-buttons when they’re nowhere near coming off.”

“I call that very delicate,” Hannah said.

“Yes, but it vexes the girl sometimes, though I must say he makes it worth her while. Well, off they went with a packet of bread and cheese apiece, and they didn’t come back till dark. It would do Mr. Ridding good, he said, and give that poor little thing a bit of a rest.”

===Chinese===

“把人弄丢了？”汉娜猜道。

“丢不了，乖。布伦金索普先生不是丢三落四的人，他仔细着呢。短一副衣领他都点得出来，裤扣离松还差着十万八千里，他就叫萨拉（Sarah）给紧一紧了。”

“我看这功夫可真够细腻的，”汉娜说。

“是呢，不过有时也把姑娘惹恼，虽则我得说，他给的工钱对得起这份麻烦。喏，他们一人揣一包面包和干酪就动身了，天黑透了才回来。他说这于里丁先生身子有益，也好让那可怜的小东西喘口气。”

===Original===

“Is that what he called her?”

“It’s what I call her myself.”

“And how did he know she needed a rest?”

“Anybody that looks at her can see that,” Mrs. Gibson said. “She’s always putting the baby in his pram when Mr. Blenkinsop goes off to business. I thought there might be trouble about that. ‘Leave him out at the back,’ I said to her, but she said how was she to hear him crying when she was in the kitchen, so we risked it﻿—behind the bushes﻿—and Mr. Blenkinsop hasn’t made any complaint, though prams weren’t what he expected when he came here.” She sighed gently. “And I didn’t expect them myself. But things are going on very comfortably and we must hope for the best. Now, Sarah’s going to clear away, and we’ll have a nice cosy time by the fire.”

===Chinese===

“他是这么称呼她的？”

“那是我自己叫惯的。”

“那他怎么知道她需要歇一歇？”

“谁看她一眼都看得出来，”吉布森太太说。“布伦金索普先生一出门办事去，她就总把娃娃搁进童车。我还当这要惹出麻烦来。‘把他搁在后头就是了，’我跟她说，可她说自己人在厨房，娃娃哭起来怎么听得见，于是我们赌了一回——搁在树丛后头——布伦金索普先生倒一句没抱怨，虽说当初他搬进来的时候，指望的并不是童车。”她轻轻叹了口气。“我自己也没指望有这些。可日子过得满安稳，咱们总得往好处盼。好了，萨拉要来撤桌子了，咱们舒舒服服靠着火坐一会儿。”

===Original===

At half-past nine Mrs. Gibson had begun to nod and Hannah knew it was time for her to go. She went upstairs to fetch her coat and hat, wondering how she should spend the hour before it was safe to return to Beresford Road. She decided to walk round the hill and down the Avenue and see how many leaves were hanging on the trees and, if Mr. Pilgrim still lingered, she might be able to get upstairs and into her nightgown before she was seen.

She felt rather desolate and she felt angry. She was sacrificing some of her independence to that man whom she ought to have outfaced, but she could not have him defiling the poor little remains of her romance, and she did not want to be separated from Ruth. Which of these motives was the stronger, she did not know. She kept the memory of her short-lived happiness in a place of its own, which was all she could do for it; she rarely looked at it, but she would keep prying eyes from it, if she could, and the memory of Ruth’s thin face, at once so childish and so mature, seemed to encourage and commend her. Nevertheless, she was conscious of the loneliness, in which she pretended to rejoice, when, through his open door, she saw the warm glow of Mr. Blenkinsop’s shaded lamps cast on the dark landing. He was not the man to sit in a room with the door open and, before he came back, there would be time, she thought, for a peep. It would suit her acid humour to see in what comfort Mr. Blenkinsop passed his evenings, while Hannah Mole, threatened by her past, had to wander in the streets. In the unlikely possibility of Mr. Blenkinsop’s having a past he need not be afraid of it. He had, according to Mrs. Gibson, a nice little income from his mother, and he was a man, and to men a past could be forgiven, even, if repentance followed, by a Nonconformist minister, while Hannah was a woman for whom repentance had no practical results. In this unfairness, she found what consolation she needed, for though what she had done was folly, it had been done fearlessly and she was too proud to feel regret.

She was advancing for her peep when Mr. Blenkinsop appeared in the doorway. “I thought that was your step on the stairs,” he said.

===Chinese===

九点半，吉布森太太打起了瞌睡，汉娜知道该走了。她上楼去取大衣帽子，心里盘算：离开能放心回贝雷斯福德路的时辰还有一个钟头，这一小时怎么打发。她决定绕着山冈兜一圈，再顺林荫大道走下去，数一数树上还挂着多少叶子；倘若皮尔格林先生还赖着没走，她说不定能赶在被瞧见之前溜上楼、换上睡衣。

她觉出几分凄凉，也觉出几分恼火。她正把几分独立牺牲给那个男人，其实她本该拿眼风把他逼退的；可她不能容他来玷污她那段罗曼史可怜巴巴的残余，她也不愿同露丝分开。这几样动机哪一样更重，她自己也说不清。她把那段短命的幸福收在一个专属的去处，这已是她能为它做的全部；她轻易不去看它，但凡她能够，她绝不容窥探的眼睛沾它一沾；而露丝那张瘦脸的记忆——那样稚气，又那样老成——似乎在给她打气，也在替她说好话。话虽如此，当她透过布伦金索普先生敞开的房门，望见他那几盏罩灯的暖光泼在昏暗的楼梯平台上时，她仍旧觉出了那份她佯称乐在其中的孤单。他不是那种肯开着门待在屋里的人；趁他还没回来，她想，偷看一眼是来得及的。看看布伦金索普先生在何等安逸里消磨夜晚，而汉娜·莫尔叫自己的过去追逼着，只能在街上流浪——这正合她那点酸溜溜的脾气。布伦金索普先生有一个过去的可能固然渺茫，退一万步说，就算有，他也无须怕它。据吉布森太太说，他从母亲名下领着一份优渥的小进项；何况他是男人，男人有过去是饶恕得起的，只要悔改跟上，连非国教牧师也饶恕；汉娜却是女人，悔改到女人身上，是不结实效的。在这桩不公里，她捡到了所需的安慰，因为纵然她干下的事是桩蠢事，干的时候却无所畏惧，而她的骄傲也不容她后悔。

她正蹑步上前要偷看，布伦金索普先生已在门口现身。“我就觉得楼梯上那是你的脚步声，”他说。

===Original===

“And I did my best not to make a sound! I know you don’t like being disturbed.”

“It’s quicker than other people’s,” he said, “and, as a matter of fact, I was just going to have a little walk. I generally have one at this time of night, so perhaps you’ll allow me to see you home.”

“I wasn’t going home, as you call it, yet,” she said. “When I have a night out I make the most of it. I’m going round the hill and down the Avenue.”

“I don’t think you ought to do that alone.”

===Chinese===

“我还特意不弄出一点响动呢！我知道你不喜欢人搅扰。”

“你这脚步比旁人的快，”他说，“其实呢，我正要出去溜达一小圈。这个时辰我照例要走走的，或许容我送你回家？”

“我还没打算回你所谓的那个家呢，”她说。“难得夜里出门，总要把本钱捞足。我要绕山冈兜一圈，再顺林荫大道下去。”

“我看你不该一个人去。”

===Original===

“I shan’t be alone if I’m with you. But no!” she cried repentantly, “I won’t spoil your walk. I’ll go by myself. Let’s start at opposite ends and I’ll meet you at the top of Beresford Road, to show you I’m not murdered, and you can deliver me at the door.”

“That would be a very silly thing to do,” he said.

“But I like doing silly things.”

“And I don’t,” he said firmly, following her down the stairs.

===Chinese===

“有你在，我就不算一个人。可不行！”她悔悟似的嚷道，“我不能搅了你的散步。我自己走。咱们打街两头动身，我在贝雷斯福德路坡顶同你会合，好叫你瞧瞧我没遭人谋害，然后你再把我送到门口，当面点交。”

“那可是桩顶傻的事，”他说。

“可爱做傻事的是我呀。”

“可我不，”他说得斩钉截铁，跟在她身后下了楼。

===Original===

“Ah, you ought to learn,” she said, seeing him plainly now, in the light of the hall, and she thought he looked too set and stolid to learn anything she could teach him. Spectacled and grave, he waited while she said good night to Mrs. Gibson, and they set off together without a word.

Hannah found it difficult to talk to Mr. Blenkinsop when she could not see his face. The sight of it made her feel merry and ready to be absurd; his mere bulk, keeping pace with hers, deadened her faculties, and he seemed to have nothing to say himself. In silence they crossed Regent Square and went through the little alley to the street where stately Georgian houses began when the shops ended, and so reached the Green, and the lamps lighting the little paths.

“I think this is much sillier than walking separately,” Hannah said and, looking up at him, she had the gratification of seeing him smile unwillingly. The smile only lasted for a moment.

===Chinese===

“咳，这个你该学一学，”她说。这时门厅的灯光正把他照了个通透，她看他那副又板又钝的样子，只怕凡她能教的，他一概学不进去。他架着眼镜、一脸庄重，候着她同吉布森太太道过晚安，两人便一言不发地上了路。

汉娜发现，只要看不见布伦金索普先生的脸，她就难以同他说话。那张脸一见就叫她心头发快，浑想胡闹；光是他那副身量，亦步亦趋地陪着她走，就把她的才具压得迟钝了，而他自己似乎也无话可说。两人默不作声地穿过摄政广场，钻过那条小巷，到了店铺到头、乔治朝代的气派宅邸开始排开的那条街，就这样走到了绿地，走到照亮一条条小径的路灯下。

“我看这样比分头走还傻，”汉娜说，一面仰头望他，颇为受用地看见他不情愿地笑了。那笑只撑了一瞬。

===Original===

“But you are safer,” he said.

“If you want to be safe, you’d better be dead.”

“I don’t agree with you,” Mr. Blenkinsop said.

“Good! Let’s argue about it.”

===Chinese===

“可这样你安全些，”他说。

“要想保险，除非死了。”

“这个我不同意，”布伦金索普先生说。

“好啊！那咱们就来辩一辩。”

===Original===

“I don’t see anything to argue about.”

“Then tell me about the Riddings.”

“You’re very curious about the Riddings.”

“Of course I am. Are you teaching him to play chess?”

Mr. Blenkinsop cleared his throat. “Yes, I’m trying,” he said bashfully, and then, angrily, as though it were Hannah’s fault, he exclaimed, “That girl will break down herself, if she doesn’t get some relief!”

===Chinese===

“我看没什么可辩的。”

“那就给我讲讲里丁一家吧。”

“你对里丁一家倒是很好奇。”

“我自然好奇。你在教他下棋吗？”

布伦金索普先生清了清嗓子。“在教，我试着呢，”他赧然说，随即又恼了——倒好像这该怨汉娜似的——嚷道：“那姑娘要是再得不到一点喘息，自己先要垮了！”

===Original===

Hannah was content to be silent for the rest of the walk. She had plenty to think about and so, apparently, had Mr. Blenkinsop, and she believed they were both thinking of Mrs. Ridding; and though, apart from her interesting thoughts, it was, as she had said, a silly walk, she enjoyed the feeling of his unnecessary protection and she was touched by his courtesy.

When she parted from him at the gate, she saw that the doorstep was illumined as it could not be unless the door was open, and in the hall she found Mr. Corder. She had feared to find Mr. Pilgrim too, and her smile of relief was a new thing to the minister.

“I have just been out to look for you,” he said.

===Chinese===

剩下的路，汉娜甘愿不作声了。她心里有的是可想，布伦金索普先生显然也有，她相信两人想的是同一位里丁太太；虽说撇开那些有趣的念头不谈，这趟散步正如她自己说的，是桩傻事，她却享受着他那份多余的护卫之感，他的殷勤有礼也叫她心头一热。

到大门口分手时，她看见门阶上亮着灯光，那光若非大门开着，是照不到的；果然，门厅里立着科德先生。她方才还怕皮尔格林先生也在里头，此刻这如释重负的微笑，落在那位牧师眼里是桩新鲜事。

“我刚出去找过你，”他说。

===Original===

“How kind of you! Then I suppose you saw me coming down the road with Mr. Blenkinsop.” He had not expected this frankness and she felt that he was disappointed.

“Mr. Blenkinsop?” he repeated.

“Yes. I’ve spent the evening with Mrs. Gibson and Mr. Blenkinsop saw me home.”

“Ah, Mrs. Gibson. I hope you had a pleasant time. Don’t trouble about my tea, Miss Mole. I have had to make it myself.”

===Chinese===

“您可真好！那么想必您瞧见我同布伦金索普先生一道打路上下来了。”他没料到她这般坦率，她也觉出他失望了。

“布伦金索普先生？”他重复了一遍。

“是啊。我一晚上都在吉布森太太那儿，是布伦金索普先生送我回来的。”

“哦，吉布森太太。但愿你们过得愉快。不必麻烦你张罗我的茶了，莫尔小姐，我只好自己动手泡了。”

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
A fortnight later Hannah walked across the downs to pay her call on Lilla. Robert Corder had again reminded her of this duty and she was willing enough to perform it. The shadow cast by Mr. Pilgrim had receded, and though she could still see it like a storm-cloud that might, or might not, break, the sky immediately above her was clear and she felt lighthearted. She had found something very whimsical in the comparative indifference of the family towards a visit which, to her, had been so portentous. At breakfast, the next morning, Robert Corder had made some of those kindly, disparaging remarks of which he was a master. He hoped Mr. Pilgrim would not find city life too much for him after the less exacting demands of the country: fortunately for him, the chapel was a small one, with a congregation of simple-minded people and no intellectual influence there, or in the wider interests of Radstowe, would be expected of him. In other words, though he did not use them, Mr. Pilgrim was not likely to sit on any committees with Robert Corder.

Wilfrid’s glance at Hannah was a comment on these bland remarks and a description of the evening’s entertainment. Ethel looked thoughtful and subdued and Ruth was occupied with a letter which was spread out on her knee and sheltered by the table.

When she looked up her face was radiant. “Uncle Jim’s coming for Christmas!” she cried.

===Chinese===
半个月后，汉娜（Hannah）穿过丘陵草原（the Downs），去拜会莉拉（Lilla）。罗伯特·科德（Robert Corder）又一次向她提起了这份礼数，她也心甘情愿要去尽一尽。皮尔格林先生（Mr. Pilgrim）投下的阴影已经退去；虽说她仍望得见它，像一朵不知崩不崩得下来的雷雨云，可她头顶上那一片天是晴的，她心境轻快。那一回拜会，在她眼里原是桩天大的事，一家人却相当地漫不经心，她觉得这里头古怪得好笑。次日早餐桌上，罗伯特·科德搬出了他顶拿手的那套和和气气的贬人话。他希望皮尔格林先生在乡下那些不费神的日子里待惯了，别嫌城市生活太吃不消：好在他那座礼拜堂不大，会众都是些心思简单的老实人，无论在堂内，还是在拉德斯托（Radstowe）更广大的地面上，都不至于有人指望他拿出什么思想上的影响。换句话说，尽管这些话他一句没有出口，皮尔格林先生多半不会同罗伯特·科德同坐哪一个委员会。

威尔弗里德（Wilfrid）朝汉娜丢来的那一瞥，既是给这番温吞话作的注脚，也是对前晚那场余兴的写照。埃塞尔（Ethel）若有所思，蔫蔫的；露丝（Ruth）专心对付着一封信，信摊在膝头，拿桌子遮着。

她抬起头来，满脸放光。“吉姆舅舅（Uncle Jim）要来我们家过圣诞节了！”她嚷道。

===Original===
“Indeed?” Robert Corder said coldly.

“Good man,” Wilfrid muttered, and Ethel, whose pleasure was spoilt by the fact that Ruth conveyed it, turned to him sharply, saying, “He’s no relation of yours!”

“That’s why I like him,” Wilfrid retorted.

===Chinese===
“是么？”罗伯特·科德冷冷地说。

“好人哪，”威尔弗里德咕哝了一句。埃塞尔原也欢喜，只是这欢喜打露丝嘴里说出来，就不是滋味了，她陡地转向他：“他跟你可没有半点亲戚关系！”

“所以我才喜欢他，”威尔弗里德回敬道。

===Original===
This little flurry passed unnoticed by Robert Corder. He was looking hurt. “I have heard nothing about this visit,” he said.

“Oh, but you will. He’s going to write to you.”

“Is that a letter from him?”

===Chinese===
这点小小的风波，罗伯特·科德全没留意。他一脸受了委屈的样子。“这趟来访，我可是什么也没听说，”他说。

“哦，你就会听说的。他要写信给你呀。”

“你手里这封就是他写的？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Ruth, ready to protect it.

“I never ask to see your letters, as you know,” her father said, and waited unsuccessfully for a moment. “But I think it would have been better if he had written to me first, and I don’t know that it will be convenient to have him. You must remember that we have a room less than we had last time he came, and there will be Howard at home, too. There will be extra mouths to cook for and I think Miss Mole ought to be considered in this matter.”

“Oh, Moley﻿—!” Ruth exclaimed, and turned scarlet.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”露丝说，摆出一副护信的架势。

“你的信，我从来不讨着看，这你是知道的，”她父亲说，说着白等了一会儿，并无下文。“不过依我看，他要是先来信知会我一声，才合规矩；至于他来，方不方便还两说。你们该记得，他上回来的那一回，我们如今少了一间屋，霍华德（Howard）届时也在家。多两张嘴要做饭，依我看，这件事也该替莫尔小姐着想着想。”

“哦，莫莉——！”露丝失声叫了出来，脸一直红到耳根。

===Original===
Here was another annoyance for Robert Corder. “That is not the way to address Miss Mole,” he said. “Miss Mole, I would rather you did not allow it.”

“But she doesn’t! I mean﻿—it just slipped out. Miss Mole, two people wouldn’t make such a terrible lot of difference, would they?”

“With due notice, I can feed a regiment,” Hannah said grandly.

===Chinese===
这又给了罗伯特·科德一桩烦恼。“这不是称呼莫尔小姐的规矩，”他说。“莫尔小姐，我宁可你别容她这么叫。”

“可我没容！我是说——话是自己溜出来的。莫尔小姐，多两个人，也不至于就是天大的难处，是不是？”

“只要预先招呼一声，我能喂饱一团人，”汉娜口气阔大地说。

===Original===
“There!” said Ruth, looking at her father boldly.

“Of course she can,” said Ethel, less in support of Ruth than in depreciation of Miss Mole. “I’ve done it myself and nobody thought anything of it.”

“Yes, we did. We thought a lot, because we couldn’t eat the Christmas puddings. You didn’t fill the basins and the water got in. Don’t you remember?”

===Chinese===
“听见没有！”露丝说，大胆地望着她父亲。

“她当然能，”埃塞尔说，与其说是给露丝帮腔，不如说是贬低莫尔小姐。“我自己就办过，也没见谁觉得了不得。”

“怎么没有！我们觉得可了不得呢，圣诞节布丁根本没法吃。盆你没装满，水全灌了进去。你不记得啦？”

===Original===
“Ruthie, Ruthie, that isn’t kind. Ethel did her best. Now don’t get excited, but run off to school or you’ll be late.”

“But I am excited and there’s plenty of time. And Wilfrid will be away for the Christmas days and you know you always make him and Howard share a room when they’re here together, and what difference will Uncle Jim make? You can’t not have him, when it’s the first Christmas he’s had at home for years. But it won’t be the last! He’s left the sea!”

“Left the sea?” Robert Corder repeated, and he looked towards the letter which Ruth was putting in her pocket.

===Chinese===
“小露丝，小露丝，这话说得可不善。埃塞尔是尽了力的。好啦，别兴奋个没完，上学去吧，不然要迟到了。”

“可我偏偏兴奋，工夫又富余得很。再说圣诞节那几天威尔弗里德不在家；你明明白白知道，他们俩同时在家的时候，你一向叫威尔弗里德同霍华德合住一间屋，添个吉姆舅舅能添出什么来？你不能不请他，这是他这么多年头一回在家过圣诞。往后也决不会是最后一回！他离了海啦！”

“离了海？”罗伯特·科德跟着念了一遍，眼睛瞟向露丝正往口袋里揣的那封信。

===Original===
“Retired,” said Ruth, enjoying her private information. “He says he thinks he’ll buy a little farm,” and she disappeared before her father could make it difficult for her to keep her letter to herself.

“Well, well,” he said, tolerantly, “I suppose we must forgive a bluff sailor for his rough and ready manners. No doubt I shall hear from him before long.”

“And you’ll let him come, won’t you?” Ethel begged.

===Chinese===
“退休啦，”露丝说，独占的消息正咂摸得有滋有味。“他说他想买一座小农场呢。”话音未落，人已没了影，没给她父亲来得及设法把那封信弄到自己手里。

“罢了，罢了，”他宽容地说，“爽利的水手，礼数粗率，想必可以担待。不用多久，他自然会来函知会我的。”

“那你会让他来的，对不对？”埃塞尔央求道。

===Original===
Robert Corder decided to become the indulgent father. “I see I shall have an unhappy Christmas if I don’t,” he said playfully and Hannah made a mental note of this weakness which, in anyone else, she might have called amiability.

It was plain that he did not care for Uncle Jim, who was Mrs. Corder’s brother and, with Mr. Blenkinsop’s affairs already developing in her mind, Hannah began busying herself with Uncle Jim, his sister and her husband, and seeking any information she could get, though she could manage very well without it.

While she crossed the downs, keeping to the paths for the sake of Lilla’s carpets, thinking that a grey sky was lovelier than a blue one, that the bare trees were exquisite against it and that the proper place for leaves was the ground, she was imagining little past scenes between Uncle Jim and Robert Corder, and clumsily tender ones between Mr. Blenkinsop and Mrs. Ridding. She did not trouble about the construction of her dramas: she saw pictures and framed sentences; she saw Uncle Jim with a protective arm round his sister’s waist and heard Mr. Blenkinsop saying solemnly, “Yes, since that very first evening﻿—” She imagined Uncle Jim as a modern buccaneer, bronzed and bearded, and though she feared he would not actually have rings in his ears, it would be surprising not to see them there, and suddenly, the actors in her little scenes became Uncle Jim and herself. Those flashing eyes of his pierced below her plain exterior and recognised a kindred spirit, and he would carry her off to sea, for really, it was absurd for a sailor to think he could turn farmer, but, if he persisted, she would be there to help him, and they would adopt Ruth and live happy ever after.

===Chinese===
罗伯特·科德决计来扮一位姑息的父亲。“我知道，我不答应，我这个圣诞节就过不痛快了，”他打趣道。汉娜心里把这点软肋记上了一笔——这毛病要是搁在旁人身上，她原可以管它叫和气。

他不喜欢吉姆舅舅，这是明摆着的——吉姆舅舅是科德太太（Mrs. Corder）的兄弟。布伦金索普先生（Mr. Blenkinsop）那桩事正在她心里酝酿发酵，她便顺势把心思挪到吉姆舅舅、他的妹妹和她丈夫这三口人身上，能搜罗一点消息是一点；其实没有这些消息，她也照样应付得了。

她一路穿过丘陵草原，为着莉拉家的地毯，专拣小径走；一面心里想着：灰色的天比蓝色的可爱，光秃秃的树衬着它精致极了，叶子待在本该待的地上；一面编排着吉姆舅舅同罗伯特·科德旧日相见的种种小戏，还有布伦金索普先生同里丁太太（Mrs. Ridding）之间那些笨手笨脚的温存戏。

===Original===
“H’m,” Hannah said in self-derision. She had arrived at Lilla’s chains and posts, and the windows of the red and white house were looking at her with coldly-practical eyes. Her fancies could not live under that gaze. The bold buccaneer would choose a buxom wench for partner; Miss Mole must continue to trust in the rich old gentleman, and she was wondering whether Lilla knew anything about Mr. Samson, when the severe parlourmaid opened the door and, making no mistake this time, took Hannah to the drawing-room.

“You’re looking very well, Hannah,” Lilla said, taking the credit to herself.

“And you’re looking more like a robin than ever, dear,” Hannah said, making the usual peck at her cousin’s cheek. “It’s nice to see you in your natural habitat, or whatever they call it. You have such an exalted expression in chapel that I hardly know you, though of course, I’m proud of our slight acquaintance.”

===Chinese===
这些戏，她并不费心去搭骨架：只见一幅幅画面，只听一句句框好的台词；她看见吉姆舅舅伸出一条护持的胳膊搂着妹妹的腰，听见布伦金索普先生一本正经地说，是啊，就打头一天晚上起——她把吉姆舅舅想成一个现代的海盗，古铜色脸膛，满脸胡须；她料定他耳朵上未必真戴得有环，可要是竟看不见那两只环，倒教人意外。忽然之间，她那些小戏里的角色，变成了吉姆舅舅和她自己。他那双炯炯的眼睛穿透她寻常的外表，认出了一颗同类的心；他要带她出海远走——说实在的，水手痴心妄想改行当农场主，本就是桩荒唐事；可他执意要试，她就在一旁帮他；他们还要收养露丝，从此过着幸福的日子。

“哼，”汉娜自嘲地哼了一声。她已经走到莉拉家门口的铁链和柱子跟前，那座红白两色的房子，窗口正用一双双冷冷的、只讲实际的眼睛看她。这样的注视之下，她的幻想活不成。那个大胆的海盗，准要挑个丰腴的姑娘来做搭档；莫尔小姐还是得继续指望那位阔气的老绅士。她正琢磨着莉拉晓不晓得桑普森先生（Mr. Samson）的底细，那位板着面孔的客厅女仆开了门，这一回没有认错，把汉娜领进了客厅。

“你的气色好得很哪，汉娜，”莉拉说，把这份功劳记到了自己账上。

===Original===
“Now, don’t begin your nonsense, but tell me how you’re getting on.”

“I shouldn’t like to boast,” Hannah said, “until I hear what Mr. Corder has told you.”

“Very little. Naturally, as I recommended you, he wouldn’t like to make any complaints to me. And, perhaps, there are none to make,” she added generously. “How do you think you are managing yourself?”

===Chinese===
“你呢，亲爱的，比哪一回都更像只知更鸟了，”汉娜说，照例在她表姐的脸颊上啄了一下。“看你待在自己的自然栖息地里——或者随他们怎么个叫法——真是高兴。你在礼拜堂里那副神情超凡入圣，我简直认不得你了；当然了，咱们这点浅浅的交情，我是引以为荣的。”

“行了，别开你的玩笑，说说你过得怎么样吧。”

“我可不敢自夸，”汉娜说，“得先听听科德先生跟你说过些什么。”

===Original===
“Splendidly! Almost too well, I’m sometimes afraid. I’m glad to have you to confide in, Lilla. I’m rather worried and I want your advice.”

“Then it’s the first time you’ve wanted anybody’s,” Lilla said drily, and her bright eyes hardened with suspicion.

“Thank God, I’ve never had the same need to!” Hannah exclaimed. “You’ll hardly believe me, Lilla﻿—”

===Chinese===
“他说得少得很。自然喽，你是我荐去的，他不乐意向我诉什么苦。不过，说不定也没有苦可诉，”她宽宏大量地添了一句。“你自己觉得撑持得怎么样？”

“好极了！有时我倒怕好得过了头。有你这个人可以交心，我真高兴，莉拉。我正愁着呢，想讨你个主意。”

“那可是破天荒头一遭，你用得着别人的主意了，”莉拉干巴巴地说，一双亮眼睛疑心地上了冻。

===Original===
“You may be sure of that, but, whatever it is, just talk about something else till the tea has come in.”

“Yes,” said Hannah, “that’s the kind of thing it is. And I hope it’s a good tea, because I’ve been rather mean with ours lately. Saving up for Christmas. We’re going to have Mrs. Corder’s brother with us. Do you know anything about him?”

“I believe he’s a sailor of some sort. I think he calls himself a captain, but not, of course, in the Navy.”

===Chinese===
“谢天谢地，这样的事我还从来用不着求人！”汉娜扬声说。“莉拉，你怕是万万不肯信——”

“这你可以放一百二十个心。不过不管是什么事，茶端进来之前，先说点别的。”

“瞧，”汉娜说，“正是这么一档子事。但愿你这茶备得像样，我们家近来茶饭上抠得很，攒着过圣诞呢。科德太太的兄弟要上我们家来住。你了解他吗？”

===Original===
“No, if he had been, I should have heard of it. I don’t much mind about his profession. It’s his character, his impressionability, his income, I’m after. And his age. That’s rather important, but he can hardly be a stripling.”

“Really, Hannah﻿—Now, let me show you these little bags I’m making for Christmas presents,” she said brightly as the maid came in. “Pretty, aren’t they?”

“Very pretty,” Hannah said, wondering which one she would get. Lilla’s presents to people like herself would be of the hashed-mutton variety, and Hannah was glad. A handsome present from Lilla would have been difficult to accept and, so far, she had not been put to that inconvenience.

===Chinese===
“我记得他是个跑船的。好像自封了个船长——当然，绝不是海军的。”

“可不是，他要真是海军的，我总该有所耳闻。他的行当我倒不大在意，我要打探的是他的品性、他的耳根子、他的进项。还有他的岁数。这一条顶要紧，不过他总不至于还是个毛头小子。”

“瞧你，汉娜——来，看看我做的这些小袋袋，预备送圣诞礼物的，”女仆进来时，她喜洋洋地说。“好看吧？”

===Original===
“I thought one of these would do very well for Ethel Corder.”

“She’d love it, but give her a gaudy one, and put a modest little cheque inside.”

“I don’t think I need reminding of that,” Lilla said coldly. “First and last, I do a good deal for the Corders.”

===Chinese===
“好看极了，”汉娜说，心里盘算着自己会分到哪一只。莉拉送给像她这等人的礼物，向来是碎烩羊肉一路的货色，汉娜为此高兴。莉拉要是送出一份厚礼，接也不是，不接也不是；好在这一层不便，至今还没有轮到她头上。

“我看这里头挑一只送给埃塞尔·科德，正合适。”

“她准喜欢。可要拣只花哨的给她，里头再搁一张小小不言的支票。”

===Original===
“And Ethel’s devoted to you. If I wasn’t devoted to you myself, I should get rather tired of hearing your name. And little does she suspect that you and I were practically fed at the same bosom. Figuratively speaking, Lilla, figuratively speaking! And I’ve heard Mr. Corder use that expression himself.”

“Not about human beings,” Lilla said, “and I don’t like it.”

“But between us girls!” Hannah protested mildly. “You must give me a little licence, and that reminds me of another thing I want to ask you. Do you know an old man called Samson, who lives next door to us in Beresford Road? Keeps a parrot and dozens of cats.”

===Chinese===
“这可不用你来提醒我，”莉拉冷冷地说。“里里外外，我为科德家办的还少吗？”

“埃塞尔又对你那样敬爱。我自己要不是也敬爱你，光听你的名字就该听腻了。她还万万想不到，你我两个，当年差不多是在同一个怀抱里吃奶长大的。打个比方说，莉拉，打个比方说！这话还是我亲耳听科德先生用过的呢。”

“他可不是拿来说人的，”莉拉说，“我也不爱听这话。”

===Original===
“I’ve heard the parrot and a very objectionable noise it makes. Isn’t he a common old man with a red face?”

“Yes, gloriously common,” Hannah said. “That’s why I like him. I’m common myself. There’s no need to think before you speak, with him. It does me good. I’ve made friends with him, Lilla, you won’t be surprised to hear. He’s had bronchitis lately, and I’ve been doing his shopping for him, and if there are any coppers in his change he lets me keep them for my trouble, on condition that I don’t put them in the plate on Sunday.”

“Then, if that’s the truth, and I don’t suppose it is, I think you’re very unwise. Isn’t there anybody else to do it for him?”

===Chinese===
“可咱们姐妹们私下说话嘛！”汉娜温温和和地抗议。“总得容我一点放肆。这么一说，我又想起一件事要请教。你认不认得一位姓桑普森的老先生，住在我们贝雷斯福德路（Beresford Road）隔壁？养着一只鹦鹉，还有几十只猫。”

“鹦鹉我可是领教过的，那副嗓子要多讨厌有多讨厌。他不就是个红脸膛的俗气老头儿吗？”

“是啊，俗气得堂堂正正，”汉娜说。“所以我就喜欢他。我自己也俗气。跟他说话，用不着先在心里掂量，这对我大有好处。我同他交上朋友了，莉拉，你听了准不吃惊。他近来害了支气管炎，跑腿买东西的事一直是我替他办的；找头里要是有几个铜板，他就赏我当辛苦钱，只有一样条件：星期天不许投进奉献盘里去。”

===Original===
“Not a soul, poor old dear. He looks after the house himself, and very neat and clean it is, in spite of the cats.”

“Then it’s still more unwise, but, of course, I don’t really believe you’d take the man’s money.”

“That the truest part of a perfectly true tale. Funny, isn’t it? It pleases him and I don’t mind a bit. It doesn’t often come to more than twopence halfpenny, because I do some juggling with sixpences and threepenny-bits.”

===Chinese===
“要是真有其事——我看未必——那你可就太不谨慎了。就没有旁人替他跑腿吗？”

“一个人也没有，可怜的老宝贝儿。家务是他自己张罗的，屋里收拾得干干净净，猫再多也不乱。”

“那就更不谨慎了。当然了，你拿他的钱这话，我才不信呢。”

===Original===
“Well, you’d better be careful. You were not engaged to look after the neighbours and, from what I remember of the man, I don’t think Mr. Corder would approve of the acquaintance.”

“Ah,” said Hannah, “that’s where I score. I happen to have a trump card up my sleeve and out it will come when it’s wanted, I confess I’m looking forward to the moment.”

“Oh, well,” Lilla sighed, “it’s no good talking to you. You’ll go your own way and you’ll come to grief and it will be most unpleasant for me.”

===Chinese===
“这桩千真万确的事里，偏偏数这一节最真。好笑吧？他高兴，我也一点儿不别扭。数目难得超过两个半便士，因为我会拿六便士和三便士的小银币变变戏法。”

“那你可得当心着点儿。雇你又不是叫你照应街坊。照我记忆里那人的样子，科德先生不会赞成你们这门来往。”

“啊，”汉娜说，“这正是我得分的所在。我袖子里恰好藏着一张王牌，到了用得着的时候，它自然会出来。不瞒你说，我正盼着那一刻呢。”

===Original===
“We have to bear each other’s burdens, dear,” Hannah said quietly. “And I’ll have another crumpet, if I may. They’re so beautifully buttery.”

“Very indigestible, I’m afraid. I daren’t touch them.”

“And a crumpet that isn’t swimming in butter is about as much use as a ship in dry dock. I’ll try that little aphorism on Uncle Jim﻿—if it is an aphorism. I’ll look it up. If you want to give me a Christmas present, Lilla, give me a dictionary.”

===Chinese===
“唉，”莉拉叹了口气，“跟你说也是白说。你由着性子走你的路，早晚栽个大跟头，到时候难堪的是我。”

“咱们总得彼此担当重担呀，亲爱的，”汉娜轻轻地说。“劳驾，再给我一块烤饼。这黄油抹得真叫漂亮。”

“只怕顶不消化。我可不敢碰。”

===Original===
“Mr. Corder must have dozens of dictionaries.”

“Well, they’re not mine﻿—yet,” Hannah said.

Lilla pretended not to hear this remark. “I’m going to give you one of these little bags. And now,” Hannah was waiting for this, “what do you want my advice about?”

===Chinese===
“再说，一块不泡在黄油里的烤饼，就跟旱船坞里的船一样，派不上半点用场。这句小格言我要拿去在吉姆舅舅面前试试——要是它算得上格言的话。我要去查一查。莉拉，你要想送我圣诞礼物，就送我一册辞典吧。”

“科德先生家里的辞典，少说也有一打。”

“可那些还不是我的——暂时还不是，”汉娜说。

===Original===
“No, no, I’m not going to worry you, but,” she leaned forward eagerly, “I must just ask you one question. Have you ever heard that Mr. Corder walks in his sleep? That’s my one hope, Lilla. Did Mrs. Corder ever complain of that, among other things?”

“Mrs. Corder never complained of anything. Why should she?”

“There’s a skeleton in every cupboard. Now I wonder how that saying arose. We go on, using these expressions, part of our common heritage, Lilla﻿—”

===Chinese===
莉拉只当没听见这句话。“我打算送你一只这样的小袋袋。好了，”汉娜等的正是这一句，“你要我拿主意的是什么事？”

“不不，我不拿这个烦你了。不过，”她急切地探过身来，“我只求你答一个问题。你可曾听说，科德先生有梦游的毛病？莉拉，这是我唯一的指望。科德太太那一肚子的抱怨里，可曾有过这一条？”

“科德太太从来什么也不抱怨。她有什么可抱怨的？”

===Original===
“I wish you wouldn’t harp so much on being common, but just keep the fact to yourself.”

Hannah put down her cup with a shaking hand. “You shouldn’t make me laugh when I’m drinking. Oh, Lilla, what a treasure you are! All right, I won’t. But it’s part of our heritage, all the same, and neither you nor I know where it comes from. Now, the kind of dictionary I want is the kind that will tell me things like that. I’m afraid it will be rather expensive.”

“I don’t see what you want with a dictionary, and I haven’t a skeleton in my cupboard and I don’t believe there’s one in Robert Corder’s. You’d better speak plainly, Hannah.”

===Chinese===
“哪一口壁橱里都藏着一具骷髅。我倒想琢磨琢磨，这句俗话是怎么兴起来的。我们至今还说这些话，都是咱们共有的家底，莉拉——”

“我希望你别老把俗字挂在嘴边，这一节，自己心里收着就好。”

汉娜端着茶杯的手一抖，把杯子放下了。“你也不该趁我喝着茶逗我笑哇。哦，莉拉，你可真是个宝贝！好，我不挂了。可它终归是咱们家底的一部分，你我谁也说不清它的来路。对了，我想要的那种辞典，就是能把这些来历讲给你听的那种。只怕价钱不便宜呢。”

===Original===
“I’ll speak as plainly as I can without being improper, though, really it wasn’t so very improper because I pretended to be asleep, as any nice woman would, but imagine my horror when Mr. Corder opened my bedroom door and came and had a look at me. It wasn’t a long look, and I don’t wonder, but a look it was! Now, how can you explain that?”

“I can’t,” Lilla said slowly, “but I’m sure you can. It’s no good trying those tricks on me, Hannah, and if it had really happened you wouldn’t have told me. I hope you won’t try them on anyone else. In the first place, they’re vulgar and, in the second, they’re not funny.”

Hannah looked disappointed. “I thought it was very funny at the time. I must have told it badly. I haven’t been very successful with my stories lately. And it would have been funnier still if I’d just opened an eye and winked at him. I meant to put that in when I told you and I forgot. Well, I must go back and call on Mr. Samson. He would have seen the joke. The good jokes, Lilla, are the ones in which character and circumstances conflict humorously and Mr. Samson would appreciate this one.”

===Chinese===
“我不明白你要辞典做什么。我的壁橱里没有骷髅，我也不信罗伯特·科德的壁橱里有。汉娜，你有话还是直说吧。”

“只要不失体统，我能说多明白就说多明白。不过说真的，这事也谈不上怎么失体统，因为我一直装睡来着——凡是正经女人都会这么办——你倒想象想象我的惊骇吧：科德先生推开我的卧房门，走进来端详了我一眼。那一眼并不长，这也难怪，可总归是看了我一眼！你说，这可怎么解释？”

“我解释不了，”莉拉慢慢地说，“可我敢保你解释得了。汉娜，跟我耍这些花招没有用；真有其事的话，你也决不会告诉我。我希望你别的谁也别耍。一来，这些把戏粗俗；二来，它们并不可笑。”

===Original===
“For goodness sake, don’t tell him!” Lilla exclaimed. “If he thinks you’re that kind of woman﻿—”

“He knows exactly what kind of woman I am, which is more than you do, Lilla dear.”

“But what’s the real explanation?” Lilla asked, almost wistfully.

===Chinese===
汉娜一脸失望。“我当时觉得可笑极了。一定是我讲砸了。近来我讲笑话老不走运。再说，我当时要是睁开一只眼，冲他挤一挤，还要更好笑呢。本来打算讲给你的时候补上这一笔，偏偏忘了。唉，我该回去拜访桑普森先生了，换了他，准能领会这个笑话。顶好的笑话，莉拉，是性格同境况可笑地相冲突的那种，这一个，桑普森先生一定赏识。”

“老天爷，千万别告诉他！”莉拉嚷了起来。“他要拿你当了那种女人——”

“我一向是个什么样的女人，他一清二楚——这一点可比你强，亲爱的莉拉。”

===Original===
“Family secrets, family secrets!” Hannah said. “I’ve kept yours and I’m going to keep the Corders’.”

===Chinese===
“家里的秘密，家里的秘密！”汉娜说。“你的秘密我守住了，科德家的，我也要守住。”

## XX / 第二十章

===Original===
Mr. Blenkinsop would have been more tactful and more chivalrous if he had explained his presence by pretending he had been waiting for this arrival. He did nothing of the kind and, after shaking hands with Robert Corder, he went away and Robert Corder, looking hurt, retired to his study. It was his habit to go there at once, when he came home, unless he heard sounds which roused his curiosity, and he was very curious, from some other part of the house, and Hannah wished she had instructed Mr. Blenkinsop to talk in whispers. She was sorry his visit had been cut short just as he was beginning to be interesting and though it was a little puzzling that he should seek her out, Hannah, who was not modest, could make it natural enough. Mr. Blenkinsop, bewildered, and troubled by a new situation, had instinctively turned to the person who could understand it. And she could understand everything, she thought, in a kind of ecstasy. She knew that Robert Corder was now trying to decide whether he should ask questions and risk the evasions which he could not believe were snubs, or find the outlet he wanted in a reprimand, and he would not know what he was going to do until he saw her. That she would presently witness the solution to this problem was some compensation for Mr. Blenkinsop’s departure, and at a suspicion of rebellion against the necessity for making her visits to her old friend on the sly and receiving those of her young one under the shadow of Robert Corder’s displeasure, she reminded herself that these conditions prevented dullness. There had been peace between her and Robert Corder for the last two weeks and she told herself that she had no wish to break the truce, but she was not going to turn the other cheek: that was part of his profession, not hers, and when she took his tea into the study and saw him writing at his desk, she thought he was obeying the command laid on him, for he raised his head and thanked her, and she felt disappointed, but until she was actually through the door, she could not be sure he had nothing to say to her and it was when she was about to turn the handle that she heard him speak in a high, questioning tone.

===Chinese===
布伦金索普先生本可以更圆通些、更有骑士风度些：只消一口咬定他早就在等候科德先生回来，此行的由头也就交代过去了。他偏不这样做；同罗伯特·科德握过手，他就走了，罗伯特·科德一脸委屈，退回书房。他惯常一进家门就径直上书房，除非宅子里别处传来勾起他好奇的动静——而他的好奇心原是很重的。汉娜心里直念叨：早该嘱咐布伦金索普先生压着嗓子说话才是。他的来访正讲到有味儿的地方，就叫拦腰斩断，她深以为憾；至于他为什么特地寻到她门上，虽说有点费解，汉娜又不是个谦虚的人，自能把它解释得妥妥帖帖。布伦金索普先生叫这新局面弄得又慌又乱，出于本能，便转向那个能懂这局面的人。而她无所不懂——她有点陶然欲醉地想。她知道罗伯特·科德此刻正拿不定主意：是开口盘问、冒着碰上支吾搪塞的风险——他决不肯承认那是对自己的怠慢——还是借一顿训斥，找着他需要的发泄口；究竟走哪一步，他得见着她才有分晓。眼看就能亲眼看到这道难题如何了结，总算把布伦金索普先生早退的亏补回来几分；而心里那点造反的苗头——凭什么探望老朋友得偷偷摸摸，接待小一辈的朋友得罩在罗伯特·科德的不悦阴影底下——刚一冒头，她就提醒自己：这些个格局，恰好杜绝了沉闷。近两个星期以来，她同罗伯特·科德一直相安无事，她对自己说并不想撕毁停战协定；可要把另一边脸也送上去，她才不干：那是他那一行的本分，不是她的。等她把茶端进书房，见他伏案写字，只当他正奉行那条加在他身上的诫命——他抬起头道了声谢——她心里反倒失望；然而人没真正跨出门去，就不能断定他准没话要说；果然，她的手刚要旋动门把，就听得他扬起声调、带着考问的意味开了腔。

===Original===
“And Ruth?”

“Ruth?” Hannah repeated.

“Have you forgotten that she is at the school concert tonight? Or did you remember it very well?”

“Very well,” Hannah replied.

“I see. Well, Miss Mole, there is, of course, no objection to your having visitors, as long as they don’t interfere with your duties, but I thought it was understood that you were to fetch Ruth.”

To be attacked on the subject of her duties which were always punctiliously fulfilled, for, as Wilfrid said, she had a professional conscience, to receive suggestions that she was neglecting Ruth, was more than Miss Mole could bear, and she made no effort to keep the anger out of her voice as she replied quickly, “Then you made a mistake. Wilfrid offered to fetch her for me.”

===Chinese===
“那么，露丝呢？”

“露丝？”汉娜重复道。

“你忘了她今晚要去参加学校音乐会？还是说，你记得一清二楚？”

“记得一清二楚，”汉娜答道。

“原来如此。好吧，莫尔小姐，当然喽，你招待客人，只要不耽误你的差事，谁也无话可说；不过我原以为，咱们是讲定了的：露丝该由你去接。”

拿她的差事来做文章——她的差事从来一丝不苟，用威尔弗里德的话说，她有一颗职业良心——又暗示她怠慢了露丝，这可超出了莫尔小姐的忍受限度。她全不想把怒气从嗓音里筛出去，立刻回敬道：“那就是你弄错了。是威尔弗里德主动提出替我去接她的。”

===Original===
“That was very obliging of Wilfrid,” Robert Corder said smoothly, “but I don’t care to have my daughter’s safety dependent on a young man who isn’t scrupulous to keep his promises.”

“It isn’t too late to start now,” Hannah said, with a glance at the clock.

“Then, Miss Mole, I shall be glad if you will go.”

“I’m sorry to refuse, Mr. Corder, but I couldn’t insult Wilfrid like that.”

“Wilfrid?” he said coldly.

“That is what he has asked me to call him. I don’t distrust him and I won’t behave as if I do.”

===Chinese===
“威尔弗里德倒是殷勤，”罗伯特·科德把话说得四平八稳，“可我不愿意把女儿的安全，押在一个不把守约当回事的年轻人身上。”

“现在动身也还来得及，”汉娜说着，朝时钟瞟了一眼。

“那么，莫尔小姐，就请你走一趟吧。”

“抱歉得很，我不能从命，科德先生，我不能那样折辱威尔弗里德。”

“威尔弗里德？”他冷冷地说。

“是他要我这么称呼他的。我不疑心他，也不肯装出疑心他的样子。”

===Original===
“But, at my request﻿—”

Miss Mole shook her head, smiling as she would have smiled at a child who could not be blamed for his stupidity. “I wouldn’t kick a puppy at anyone’s request.”

“Puppy is a good word in this connection,” he said, and Hannah realised that his temper was very bad and that though he seemed impervious to anything but petty slights to his own dignity, he might have troubles worthy of sympathy.

“It was an unfortunate word to use,” she said lightly. “I meant any young thing. Wilfrid will keep his promise, Mr. Corder, and Ruth will like being fetched by him. It isn’t every girl who has such a handsome cousin. And,” she went on quickly, as he frowned, “your daughter was anxious that the house should not be left.”

“And you were anxious to see Mr. Blenkinsop.”

Miss Mole was deaf to this interruption. “She didn’t want Doris and her young man to be waiting about in the street. I should have thought the atmosphere of Beresford Road would have a sobering effect, much more sobering than the Downs, where I suppose they wander.”

===Chinese===
“可是，看在我的面上——”

莫尔小姐摇了摇头，笑吟吟的，像看一个傻得叫人怪不着的孩子。“任凭谁开口，我也不去踢一只小狗。”

“就着这个话头，小狗两个字，倒选得妙，”他说。汉娜这才明白，他的火气有多旺；他看着百事不侵，只把损伤他颜面的鸡毛小事放在心上，骨子里未必没有值得一掬同情的烦恼。

“可惜这个词用得不巧，”她轻描淡写地说，“我指的是随便哪样小东西。威尔弗里德会守约的，科德先生，露丝也乐意让他去接。有这样一个漂亮的表哥，不是哪个姑娘都有的福气。再说，”见他皱眉，她赶忙抢着说，“你女儿还惦记着家里不能没人照看呢。”

“而你惦记的是会见布伦金索普先生。”

莫尔小姐对这句插嘴置若罔闻。“她是不忍叫多丽丝和她那位小伙子在街上干等。我倒本以为，贝雷斯福德路的空气自有一种清醒的功效，比丘陵草原清醒得多——我猜他们是在那边游荡来着。”

===Original===
“Miss Mole, I dislike this sort of talk.”

“But they do it, you know.”

“And I was not aware that Doris,” he overcame his repugnance with difficulty, “that Doris had what you call a young man.”

“Yes, he’s quite young,” Hannah said simply. “He’s the grocer’s assistant. You see, when he called for orders﻿—”

“I don’t wish to hear about it. I dislike the idea very much.”

“Yes, Doris isn’t particularly attractive, is she? I shouldn’t have chosen her myself, but there’s no accounting for tastes, as they say, and I think she has improved a little under the influence of the grocer’s assistant’s love. And he’s highly respectable﻿—at least, they’ve both told me so, and the grocer himself corroborates. I’ve made enquiries. He’s rather a friend of mine.”

===Chinese===
“莫尔小姐，这种话我不爱听。”

“可他们照逛不误，这您也知道。”

“而我压根不知道，多丽丝，”他好容易压下满心的嫌恶，“压根不知道多丽丝有你所谓的什么小伙子。”

“是啊，年纪轻轻的，”汉娜老老实实地说，“他是杂货铺的伙计。是这样的，他来接订单的时候——”

“我不想知道这些。这念头我讨厌得很。”

“可不是，多丽丝算不上怎么标致，对吧？换了我，也不会挑中她；不过人各有所好，俗话说得好。我想她在这位杂货铺伙计的爱情熏陶之下，倒真出息了一点儿。他人也极其正派——至少他们俩都这么对我讲，杂货铺老板本人也肯作证。我打听过。他还算得上我的一位朋友呢。”

===Original===
“You seem to make friends rather easily, Miss Mole.”

“Yes, isn’t it lucky for me?” she said brightly, and then, as though she noticed his clouded brow for the first time, she asked in her softest voice. “Have I done anything to annoy you?” and immediately perceived that the frontal attack was the one for Robert Corder. A patch of red showed on each cheek and he fidgeted with the papers on his desk.

“I did hear something tonight which disturbed me a little,” he confessed, and Hannah, glad that he was not looking at her, braced herself for the shock. “And then,” he went on, “I am disappointed in Samuel Blenkinsop. He neglects the chapel, but he visits my house when I am out of it and goes off without a word. I don’t understand it.”

Hannah’s body slackened suddenly and she found that all her muscles ached. “Do you mind if I sit down?” she asked.

“I shan’t keep you a moment, Miss Mole, but yes, sit down, of course. Can you tell me, as you seem so intimate with Blenkinsop, whether I have offended him? I know,” he said, smiling faintly, “that my views sometimes alarm the more timid spirits in my congregation, but I always welcome candid criticism.”

===Chinese===
“你交朋友，似乎也太容易了些，莫尔小姐。”

“是啊，这不正是我的运气吗？”她笑逐颜开地说。随后，仿佛才头一回留意到他眉间的阴云，她拿最软和的嗓音问：“我做了什么惹您恼火的事了吗？”话一出口她就看出，正面进击的一路，用在罗伯特·科德身上最灵。他两颊各泛起一小块红，手在桌上的文件里拨弄个不停。

“今天晚上，我确实听到一点风声，心里颇不安生，”他招认道。汉娜庆幸他没朝自己看，硬起心肠等着那一下震动。“再说，”他接着说，“塞缪尔·布伦金索普也叫人失望。礼拜堂他疏远了，倒会拣我不在家的时候上我家里来，一言不发就走。我弄不懂。”

汉娜周身蓦地一松，只觉条条肌肉都在发酸。“我坐一坐，您不介意吧？”她问。

“耽搁不了你多大工夫，莫尔小姐。好，坐，当然可以。你同布伦金索普既然相熟到这般地步，能不能告诉我，我是不是什么地方开罪了他？我知道，”他淡淡一笑，“我的见解有时会惊着会众里胆气小的几位，可我一向欢迎直言不讳的批评。”

===Original===
Gazing at Robert Corder, Hannah forgot Mr. Blenkinsop. This self-deception which, she could well believe, was sincere, had startled her into the fear that her opinion of herself was just as fond, and the world she had made for herself, in which she was wise and witty, of wide sympathies and an understanding heart, would have slipped silently into ruins if she had not found a desperate strength with which to prop it up. If it did not stand, she would go under with it; she would have to admit that Robert Corder was wrong in his dislike of her and one of her pleasures was the conviction that he was right, that she was too subtle for his comfort and too clear-sighted. It would be terrible to have to own that, however much they might differ, they had this bond of seeing themselves as no other person could.

===Chinese===
汉娜定定望着罗伯特·科德，把布伦金索普先生忘了个干净。这一份自欺——她满可以相信它出于真诚——却把她惊出一身冷汗：她对自己的那点估量，会不会同样是一厢情愿？她亲手为自己筑起的那方天地——在那里头，她睿智而风趣，同情广博，善解人意——若不是拼着孤注一掷的力气把它死死撑住，早已无声无息地塌作一堆瓦砾。这天地若立不住，她也将随之没顶；她将不得不承认，罗伯特·科德厌恶她，原是厌错了；而她平生一乐，正在于深信他没有厌错——深信自己精微得叫他坐立不安，明察得叫他无所遁形。若还要她招认：两人无论怎样南辕北辙，竟共着这样一条纽带——各自把自己看得那样透彻，旁人谁也办不到——那才真是可怕。

===Original===
“What is the matter, Miss Mole?” he asked suavely.

“I was﻿—thinking,” Hannah said.

“You are doubting,” he said helpfully, “whether you are justified in telling me.”

“No,” said Hannah, “I’m afraid I’d forgotten all about Mr. Blenkinsop.”

“In other words, you were not listening to what I said.”

“Indeed I was, but it made me think of other things.” She looked at him with the bright intelligence she kept for him. “Isn’t that what conversation is for?”

===Chinese===
“怎么回事，莫尔小姐？”他慢条斯理地问。

“我在——想事情，”汉娜说。

“你是在踌躇，”他好心地替她接上，“不知道把话讲给我听，该是不该。”

“不是，”汉娜说，“我怕的是，我早把布伦金索普先生忘了个一干二净。”

“换句话说，我方才的话，你没有在听。”

“明明听着呢，只是听着听着，叫我想起了别的。”她望住他，眼里闪着她专为他留着的那点颖悟的光。“谈话不正是为了这个吗？”

===Original===
“Not as I understand it,” he replied, and Hannah was smilingly silent.

“And now,” she said, feeling that this was her moment, while he was angry, “I hope you’ll tell me what you’ve heard about me. For I can’t bear suspense,” she added truthfully.

“No,” he said coldly, “I prefer to try to forget it.”

“Oh, well, if you can forget it,” she said, rising as the door bell rang, “I shan’t worry about it either,” but she was not so easy as she appeared. She fancied that Robert Corder’s way of forgetting things was to put them in some safe place until he wanted them, and she knew that while she watched for an obvious danger, another, unsuspected, might creep up behind her. The obvious danger was Mr. Pilgrim. He was not the only person who knew Hannah Mole outside her professional capacity, but he was close at hand and that very night she was to feel that he was coming nearer, to hear his feet as she had heard them, ten years ago, padding up her little garden path. She had been angry then, but amused because she had been happy: she was angrier now because that happiness was only a little cherished dust, and it would need all her skill and energy to keep it from being blown away by the indignant breath of the sinless. It seemed hard that she should not be allowed to keep it without fighting for it; it was so frail a possession that, in the turmoil, she might lose it, and in its frailty, if she had been given to pity of herself, she would have seen her tragedy. She had no memories which sustained her with their own strength; it was she who had to be tender with the weak. She could have faced the world with a glorious failure, but she must hide this one, which had ended meanly, and she was ten years older now and sometimes she was tired. She did not want to be sent roving again, just yet: she wished, as she had said, to make a good job of her undertaking, and the happiness of these people was becoming important to her.

When she opened the door to Doris, she looked at her sharply to see whether the course of her love was running smooth, and the sight of her face, rosy with the kisses of the grocer’s assistant, was a pleasant one.

“Good girl,” Hannah said. “It’s just striking ten. Have you had a nice walk?”

===Chinese===
“至少我不这样理解，”他答道。汉娜含笑不语。

“那么此刻，”她说——趁他动气，正是她的时机——“我希望您把听来的关于我的话，讲给我听。悬心吊胆地等，我可爱不了。”这半句倒是实话。

“不，”他冷冷地说，“我宁可想个法子把它忘了。”

“哦，好啊，你既忘得掉，”门铃一响，她起身说道，“我也不必再为它操心了。”可她心里并不像外表这么松快。她猜想，罗伯特·科德忘事的诀窍，是把东西妥妥帖帖收在什么地方，等到要用时再取出来；她也明白，她一边提防明处的危险，另一桩没提防的，兴许正从背后悄悄爬近。明处的危险，是皮尔格林先生（Mr. Pilgrim）。知道汉娜·莫尔其人、而不限在她职业身份以内的，他并非独一个；可他近在咫尺。就在今天夜里，她果然觉出他又挨近了一步——听见他的脚步声，同十年前一模一样，噗嗒噗嗒踏上她那条小小的园径。那时她也发怒，却恼中带着笑意，因为她那时是幸福的；如今她恼得更凶，因为那份幸福只剩下一小捧珍藏的尘灰，要护它不被自命无罪之人义愤的鼻息吹散，得使出她全副的手段与气力。不许她安安稳稳守住这点东西、偏要她拼力一争，似乎太苛刻；这产业又那样娇脆，一阵乱局里兴许就保不住；而正因为它娇脆，倘若她素性惯于自怜，早该从中照见自己的悲剧了。她没有哪段回忆能凭自身的力气把她撑住；倒是她，得反过来疼惜这些脆弱的东西。她本可以顶着一场壮丽的失败去直面世人，可这一场收场得寒碜，只能藏起；何况她如今又长了十岁，有时也觉得累。她眼下还不想再被打发去四方漂泊：她说过，要把手里这桩差事办得漂亮；这些人的幸福，在她心里正一日重似一日。

她开门放多丽丝进来，目光犀利地一扫，看她那桩恋情行船行得可平稳；那张脸叫杂货铺伙计的亲吻烘得红喷喷的，看着着实教人欢喜。

“好姑娘，”汉娜说，“钟正敲十点。散步散得可痛快？”

===Original===
“I’ve been to see his mother, Miss,” Doris said proudly. “She was a bit stiff, but he says he thinks she’ll get used to me.”

“Well, there!” Hannah said in congratulation, and watched one happy person go up to bed.

The voices of Wilfrid and Ruth, who came in soon afterwards, sounded happy, too, and Ethel, following close on their heels, showed an excitement which roused Hannah’s misgivings. Something had happened on which she would raise expectations not to be fulfilled, and the joyousness meant future trouble, but not until she began her confidences, when Wilfrid and Ruth had gone to bed, did Hannah understand that the chief part of the trouble might be for herself.

Mr. Pilgrim had been to the Girls’ Club. The one in connection with his own chapel was ill-attended and badly managed and he had gone to see how Miss Corder managed hers. It appeared that he was delighted with everything, he had given the girls a little address, and he hoped Ethel would allow him to consult her again.

===Chinese===
“我上他母亲那儿去了，小姐，”多丽丝得意地说，“她起先有点儿端着架子，可他说，他想她会慢慢惯了我的。”

“哟，了不得！”汉娜贺了一声，眼看着这个有福的人儿上楼睡觉去了。

不多时，威尔弗里德和露丝也进了门，两人的话音同样透着高兴；紧跟着他们脚后进来的埃塞尔，一脸兴奋，倒把汉娜心头的疑虑勾了起来。准是出了什么事，而埃塞尔要拿它生出些兑现不了的指望，这一团喜气里埋着日后的麻烦；可是直到威尔弗里德和露丝睡下、埃塞尔开始向她倾吐心事，汉娜才明白：这麻烦的大头，多半要落在她自己身上。

原来皮尔格林先生到少女俱乐部去过一趟。他自己那座礼拜堂名下附设的一处，问津者寥寥，又办得不成章法；他此去，是要看看科德小姐把她的俱乐部经营成什么样。据说他样样中意，还给姑娘们作了一小篇训话，并盼着埃塞尔肯让他改日再来请教。

===Original===
“So I must help him if I can, mustn’t I, Miss Mole?”

“Practical experience is what he wants.” Hannah said briskly. “Let him go to the club and watch you do it.”

“Yes,” Ethel said, a little doubtfully. “But you see, Patsy Withers is generally there and she’s so interfering. She wasn’t there tonight, or she would have made Mr. Pilgrim think she runs the Club. And she’s so silly with men. I thought I might ask him to tea one day.”

Hannah kept a silence which made Ethel anxious.

“Don’t you think I might do that, Miss Mole?”

“Well,” Hannah said, “you know what family teas are.”

===Chinese===
“所以我只要帮得上，就得帮他，是不是，莫尔小姐？”

“他要的是实际经验，”汉娜脆生生地说，“让他上俱乐部去，看你怎么办事好了。”

“是啊，”埃塞尔说，口气里有几分迟疑，“可是您瞧，佩茜·威瑟斯常在那儿，又顶爱插手管事。今晚她不在，要在的话，准会叫皮尔格林先生认定俱乐部是她开的。她一见男人就犯糊涂。我想着，哪天兴许可以请他来吃茶。”

汉娜偏不作声，这份沉默叫埃塞尔心里没了底。

“您觉得这事做不得吗，莫尔小姐？”

“这个嘛，”汉娜说，“一家子围着喝茶是什么滋味，你是知道的。”

===Original===
“Yes,” Ethel agreed again.

“And your father and he would have so much to say to each other that I’m afraid you wouldn’t be able to help him very much. And you wouldn’t be at your best.”

“How do you mean﻿—my best?”

“You wouldn’t speak with the same authority in the bosom of your family.”

“Oh,” Ethel said, and she looked and sounded disappointed.

“But,” said Hannah quickly, easing her own conscience, “you mustn’t take my advice. You must do as you think best.”

===Chinese===
“知道，”埃塞尔又应了一声。

“再说你父亲同他见了面，有的是话谈，只怕你帮不上他多少忙。何况你也拿不出最好的样子来。”

“这话怎么讲——最好的样子？”

“人在自家人怀里，说话可就没了那股权威。”

“哦，”埃塞尔应了一声，神色之间、语气之间，尽是失望。

“不过，”汉娜赶紧接上，顺手替自己的良心松了松绑，“我的话你可不能全当真，只管照你自己认定的最好的去办。”

===Original===
“But I don’t know what that is!” Ethel cried. “I thought you’d help me. You know, Miss Mole, it’s dreadful, sometimes, not to have a mother.”

There were tears in her eyes and Hannah thought of Mrs. Corder, who trusted Miss Mole to do what she could. “Then I’ll tell you exactly what I think,” she said quietly. “Do nothing until he speaks to you about it again.”

“But, perhaps, he never will!”

“I know,” Hannah said, thinking now of all the women who waited for the words they would not hear. “But if he’s not in earnest about his work, it’s not for you to remind him.”

“Isn’t it? But that’s just one of the ways where women help.”

“Not at this stage of your acquaintance.”

===Chinese===
“可我根本不知道那才最好呀！”埃塞尔嚷道，“我还指着你帮我呢。莫尔小姐，您知道的，有时候没有母亲，真是不堪设想。”

她眼里汪着泪。汉娜想起了科德太太——她信得过莫尔小姐，肯由她尽力而为。“那么我就把心里的话一五一十讲给你，”她平平静静地说，“他不同你再提这事，你就按兵不动。”

“可是，他兴许再也不提了呢！”

“我知道，”汉娜说。这一刻她心里想着的是天下多少女人，苦等着一句永远等不来的话。“可他若不是真心把工作当回事，那就轮不到你来提醒他。”

“轮不到吗？可女人帮忙，帮的不正是这一种吗？”

“照你们眼下这点交情，还使不得。”

===Original===
“I feel as if I’ve known him for a long time. You do feel like that with some people, don’t you? He was so friendly tonight. And I was wondering if we should have a Christmas party, for Howard, you know.”

“You’ll have to speak to your father about that,” Hannah said.

She went very slowly up to bed and her feet felt heavy. It was no use running from Mr. Pilgrim: dodge and double as she might, he was bound to catch her in the end, but if he caught Ethel at the same time, the loss to one might be balanced by the gain to another, and that, after all, was the only way to reckon. The world would gain something from a happy Ethel﻿—if such a man as Mr. Pilgrim could make her happy﻿—and Hannah Mole would get something out of it, in spite of him.

Her thoughts had carried her past Ruth’s half open door, but Ruth’s voice arrested them.

“Is that you, Miss Mole?” she called cautiously. “I thought it might be Father,” she explained. “That’s why I spoke like that. It didn’t sound a bit like you, Moley. You generally run upstairs so much quicker than anybody else.”

“So I’ve been told before,” Hannah said.

===Chinese===
“我觉得好像已经认识他好久了。有的人就是叫人一见如故，对不对？今晚他那么亲热。我还在琢磨，咱们要不要办一场圣诞聚会——为了霍华德，您知道的。”

“这件事，你得同你父亲去讲，”汉娜说。
