她上楼去睡，走得极慢，两条腿沉甸甸的。躲皮尔格林先生是躲不掉的：任凭她闪转腾挪，他迟早要把她逮住；可倘若他逮住她的同一刻，也把埃塞尔逮住了，那么一人之失，或可拿另一人之得来相抵——说到底，这笔账也只能这么算。一个幸福的埃塞尔，总归叫世界添点什么——倘若皮尔格林先生这路人当真能叫她幸福——而汉娜·莫尔也照样能从中得着点什么，管他愿意不愿意。

她一路想着心事，脚步已过了露丝那扇半掩的房门，露丝的声音却把心事截住了。

“是你吗，莫尔小姐？”她小心翼翼地问。“我还当是父亲呢，”她解释说，“所以才那么问。那脚步声一点儿也不像你，莫莉（Moley）。你上楼向来跑得比谁都快。”

“这话我不是头一回听人说了，”汉娜说。

===Original===
“You’re not tired, are you?”

“Yes I am, rather.”

“Oh, dear! And I’m afraid you had a dull evening. I enjoyed the concert and Wilfrid was so nice, coming home, not trying to show off, or anything silly. He’s like that when you’re alone with him. And Ethel’s in a very good temper and Howard will be here in a few days, but I don’t like you to be tired. I do hope we’re not going to be too much for you.”

“I hope not,” Hannah said, with a wry smile. “I shall be all right in the morning,” she added cheerfully, but she stayed up for a long time, in the cold of her bedroom, working at Ruth’s dress, repaying the child, whom she was afraid to kiss, for her first sign of thoughtfulness.

===Chinese===
“你不累吧？”

“累的，还累得不轻。”

“哎呀，瞧我！今晚你准是闷坏了。音乐会我可开心了，威尔弗里德回来的路上好得很，一点儿不逞能，也没讲半句傻话。同他单独在一处的时候，他就是这副样子。还有，埃塞尔脾气好极了，霍华德几天之内也就到了。可是我不喜欢你累着。我真巴望，咱们不至于叫你受不了。”

“我也巴望不会，”汉娜说，嘴角牵起一丝苦笑。“挨到明早，我就又精神百倍了，”她高高兴兴地补上一句。可她在卧室的清寒里坐了许久许久，赶缝露丝的裙子，偿还这孩子头一回露出的一点儿体贴——那孩子，她是连亲一亲都不敢的。

## XXI / 第二十一章

===Original===

The arrival of Howard Corder unfortunately coincided with the arrival of Mr. Blenkinsop’s letter, and what should have been a glad little family gathering had Robert Corder’s views of the younger generation for its entertainment. Mr. Blenkinsop was the text of the discourse, but everyone felt that illustrations from the home circle might be supplied, and Wilfrid sat in lively, and Howard in patient, expectation of reproach. Mr. Corder spared them this, however, and, breaking off suddenly, he remarked with a smile, that he did not want to spoil Howard’s first evening at home.

Wilfrid twisted an eyebrow in Miss Mole’s direction and Howard looked steadily at his plate. He seemed to have all the patience the other Corders lacked and a capacity for being contented if he were given the chance.

“Howard must tell us all the news of Oxford,” his father said, generously resigning his position of spokesman.

“Oh﻿—it’s just the same as usual. We’ve had a lot of fog,” Howard said, and suddenly, at a slight movement from the head of the table, Wilfrid and Ethel both began to speak at once.

Wilfrid waved a hand courteously. “Go on. Your turn.”

“Oh, it’s nothing. I was just going to say it’s funny you should have had that letter from Mr. Blenkinsop﻿—”

===Chinese===

霍华德·科德来得不巧，恰与布伦金索普先生的来信撞了个正着；一场本该欢欢喜喜的小小家庭团聚，拿来当余兴节目的，竟是罗伯特·科德对年轻一代的一番高论。布伦金索普先生是这篇布道的经文，可人人都觉得，例证尽可以从自家圈子里现取现用；威尔弗里德坐立不安地等着挨训，霍华德则耐着性子等。然而科德先生饶过了他们：话讲到半截忽然一收，含笑说，他不愿扫了霍华德回家头一晚的兴致。

威尔弗里德朝莫尔小姐这边拧了拧眉毛；霍华德目不转睛地盯着自己的盘子。他仿佛把其余科德家人欠缺的耐性全数集于一身，而且只要有机会，便能安于现状、自得其乐。

“霍华德一定得把牛津的种种新闻讲给我们听听，”他父亲说，慷慨地让出了发言人的位子。

“噢——还是老样子。雾倒碰上了一大批，”霍华德说。就在这时，餐桌主位上起了一丝轻微的动静，威尔弗里德和埃塞尔顿时同时开了口。

威尔弗里德客气地挥了挥手。“你说吧，轮到你了。”

“噢，没什么。我只是想说，好笑，你怎么会收到布伦金索普先生那封信——”

===Original===

“Funny!” Robert Corder exclaimed.

“I mean﻿—queer, because I’ve had one from Patsy Withers. She doesn’t want to help me with the Club any longer.”

“Indeed? Well, no doubt Miss Withers has very good reasons for her decision and I can see no likeness between her and Mr. Blenkinsop.”

“Oh, no!” Ethel agreed eagerly, “but she did promise to help, and now she says Wednesday evening doesn’t suit her. Oh! Perhaps she likes going to the weeknight service.”

“That would be very strange,” her father said bitingly. “As a matter of fact, she was there last time. I told you it was a mistake to have Wednesday for the Club.”

“But the girls wanted it then, and the Club’s for the girls.”

===Chinese===

“好笑！”罗伯特·科德喝了一声。

“我是说——古怪，因为我也收到了佩茜·威瑟斯的信。她不愿意再帮我操办少女俱乐部了。”

“是吗？唔，毫无疑问，威瑟斯小姐这么做自有她十分充足的道理；我看不出她同布伦金索普先生之间有任何相似之处。”

“哦，可不是！”埃塞尔急急地附和，“可她先前是答应过要帮忙的，如今她说星期三晚上不方便。哦！说不定她是爱去听周间晚礼拜呢。”

“那可就太奇怪了，”她父亲话里带刺地说。“其实呢，上一回她就在场。我早对你说过，把俱乐部定在星期三是个失策。”

“可姑娘们要的就是那个晚上，再说俱乐部原是为姑娘们办的。”

===Original===

“Then you must be prepared to lose your helpers.”

“I should think you’d be glad,” said Ruth. “I hate Patsy Withers.”

“Ruthie, Ruthie!”

“I do,” Ruth said stubbornly. “She’s like barley sugar, yellow and squirmy, and her voice is like it too, all sweet and waggly, and she talks to me as if I’m about six.”

“If you were six I should send you to bed,” said her father. “I won’t have such remarks made at my table.”

“But you were making them yourself about Mr. Blenkinsop,” Ruth said sullenly.

===Chinese===

“那你就得准备好失去你的帮手。”

“要我说你还高兴才是，”露丝说。“我恨佩茜·威瑟斯。”

“小露丝，小露丝！”

“我就是恨，”露丝倔头倔脑地说。“她活像根大麦糖，黄澄澄、软塌塌、扭扭曲曲的；嗓音也像那糖，又甜又黏，颤颤晃晃；她跟我说话，那口气就跟我顶多六岁似的。”

“你要真有六岁，我就打发你上床去，”她父亲说。“我的饭桌上，容不得这种话。”

“可你自己方才不也在说布伦金索普先生吗，”露丝闷声闷气地说。

===Original===

“That’s a very different matter,” he said, and he went out of the room. This was his usual way of emphasising a disapproval which he forebore to express in words, and possibly he believed it left his family depressed. Ethel, indeed, looked frightened, and Ruth looked glum until her next speech sent Wilfrid into a roar of laughter.

“I don’t think it’s good for people to be ministers,” she said, and Ethel cried, “Oh, hush, Wilfrid, hush! Father will hear you. And it’s very wrong of you, Ruth, to say such things. It’s a noble profession. Isn’t it, Miss Mole?”

“All professions can be noble,” Hannah replied gravely. It was only a little while since Ethel had extolled the doctor’s calling in the same words, only a little longer since Ruth had flushed in loyalty to her father when Miss Mole had disconcerted him, and in these changes she measured the profundity of Ethel’s feelings and the extent of Ruth’s trust. It was Howard, to whom Miss Mole was a stranger, who looked uncomfortable, and Ethel, glancing from one face to another, looking for support and thinking of Mr. Pilgrim, assuring herself she was right but wishing they would all agree with her, saw her brother’s look and misinterpreted it.

“And when your own brother’s a minister, you’ll change your mind,” she said.

“Oh, shut up,” said Howard. “Let’s go into the other room and play at something.”

“Coming, Mona Lisa?” Wilfrid asked.

===Chinese===

“那完全是两码事，”他说，说着便走出了房间。这是他的老办法：心里不以为然而又不肯见诸言辞，便用这一着来加重分量；他多半还相信，这一走，全家人该垂头丧气了。埃塞尔果然一脸惊惶；露丝沉着脸，直到她下一句话把威尔弗里德逗得放声大笑。

“我看人当牧师没好处，”她说。埃塞尔嚷了起来：“哦，别说了，威尔弗里德，别说了！爸爸要听见的。露丝，说这种话可是很不应该的。牧师是高尚的职业。莫尔小姐，你说是不是？”

“什么职业都可以高尚，”汉娜一板一眼地回答。就在不久之前，埃塞尔还用一模一样的字眼颂扬过医生那一行；再往前不久，莫尔小姐弄得科德先生下不来台的时候，露丝还怀着对父亲的忠心涨红了脸。从这些变来变去里，汉娜量出了埃塞尔的感情有多深、露丝的信任有多广。倒是霍华德——莫尔小姐于他是生人——显得不自在；埃塞尔一张脸一张脸地看过去，四处讨援兵，心里转着皮尔格林先生（Mr. Pilgrim），一面拿准自己没错，一面又巴望人人都附和她；她瞧见了哥哥的眼色，却把它会错了意。

“等你亲哥哥当上了牧师，你就得改口了，”她说。

“哦，闭嘴吧，”霍华德说。“咱们上另一间屋玩点什么去。”

“来吗，蒙娜丽莎？”威尔弗里德问。

===Original===

Hannah shook her head and remained by the fire, under the hissing gas. As soon as Robert Corder heard the young people in the drawing-room, he would look into the dining-room to see if Miss Mole was doing her duty; in his present mood he would be more disappointed than pleased to see her there, and Miss Mole was willing to disappoint him, but what a pity it was, she thought, that he and she could not be companionable. Did it never occur to him, that she might crave for maturer society sometimes and need more relaxation than she got? From seven o’clock in the morning until half-past ten at night, she was busy in his service, dusting, cooking carefully, making beds, shopping economically, darning socks and stockings and his under garments, superintending Doris, who was slow and stupid, and she got her little periods of leisure by her own speed and contrivance. If he had been a different kind of man, they could have had good talks by the fire when she took in his tea at ten o’clock. And, perhaps, he was thinking the very same thing, and wishing she were a different kind of woman and more like Miss Patsy Withers, and it seemed to Hannah that Lilla would have been cleverer if she had chosen her watchdog of the breed Mr. Corder admired. The angularity and asperity of Miss Mole simply served to show up the softness and sweetness of the other, but Lilla’s thrifty turn of mind had seized on the opportunity of doing two good deeds in one, supplying Mr. Corder with a housekeeper and sparing herself the inconvenience of having a penniless cousin on her hands. She would have done better to have introduced Patsy herself into the family and let her see what she could make of it. Patsy would probably have found that a hero is more easily worshipped at a distance and Robert Corder would have learnt that flattery does not flavour food, and Hannah saw that the result of this combined ignorance might be as dangerous to her as Mr. Pilgrim, though not so disagreeable, and might be disastrous to Ruth. And Patsy had been at the weeknight service and Mr. Corder had heard something which disturbed him. Now what could that be? Hannah asked herself, tapping her lips with her scissors.

===Chinese===

汉娜摇了摇头，独自守在炉火边，头顶上煤气灯嘶嘶作响。罗伯特·科德一听见年轻人们进了客厅，就会踱到餐室门口张一眼，看莫尔小姐是否恪尽职守；照他此刻的心绪，见她在那儿，他失望要多于欢喜，而莫尔小姐也乐意叫他失望。只是她想，他和她竟不能亲亲热热地相处，多么可惜。难道就从来没想过，她有时也渴求些成熟些的伴侣，需要的宽舒也比她得到的更多？从早上七点到夜里十点半，她一直为他的差遣忙碌：掸尘，细心做饭，铺床，精打细算地采买，织补短袜、长袜和他的贴身衣物，还要督着多丽丝干活——那孩子又慢又笨；而她那一点一滴的闲暇，全是靠自己的麻利和心计抠出来的。倘若他是另一种样子的男人，十点钟她端茶进去的时候，两人本可以守着炉火好好谈谈。而说不定，他心里转的正是同一个念头，正巴望她是另一种样子的女人，更像佩茜·威瑟斯小姐那一路；在汉娜看来，莉拉当初挑这条看门狗的时候，若是挑中科德先生看得上眼的品种，倒还更聪明些。莫尔小姐的棱角与泼辣，恰恰把另一位那份温软甜美衬得毕现；不过莉拉那副精打细算的性子，抓住的正是这一举两得的机会：既给科德先生供应了一名管家，又甩脱了落在自己手里这个身无分文的远房表亲的累赘。她原本更有道理亲自把佩茜引荐进这个家，让她自己去掂量，看能折腾出什么名堂。佩茜多半会发现，英雄隔得远才好崇拜；罗伯特·科德也会领教到，奉承话给饭菜提不了味。汉娜看得分明：这两下里的懵懂凑在一处，落到她头上，结局可能同皮尔格林先生一般危险，只是没那么叫人厌烦罢了，而对露丝则可能是灭顶之灾。何况佩茜又去过了周间晚礼拜，科德先生听到了什么，叫他心绪不宁。究竟会是什么呢？汉娜用剪刀尖轻点着嘴唇，问自己。

===Original===

Mr. Corder put his head round the door and Hannah smiled at him as charmingly as she could. When he had disappeared, Ethel came in and fidgeted with the ornaments on the mantelpiece.

“Finished the game?” Hannah asked.

“No, but I have to stay out till they call me in. Miss Mole, don’t you think it’s rather funny about Patsy?”

“Is it? You’re really glad to get rid of her, aren’t you?”

“Yes. But still, I think it’s funny.”

This was as near as Ethel would go to what was on her mind and Hannah would not give her the little push she wanted. “Is she a great friend of yours?” she asked, instead.

===Chinese===

科德先生把头从门边探进来张了张，汉娜竭尽所能，朝他妩媚地一笑。他人一没影，埃塞尔就进来了，把壁炉台上的摆设件件拨弄了个遍。

“游戏玩完了？”汉娜问。

“没有，可我得待在外头，等他们来叫我。莫尔小姐，佩茜这件事，你不觉得有点好笑吗？”

“好笑吗？甩掉她，你心里正乐意呢，是不是？”

“是。可尽管这样，我还是觉得这事好笑。”

埃塞尔的话到这儿就打住了，再不肯往心里那件事上多走一步；而汉娜也不肯推她一把，把她想要的那点促狭替她说破。“她是你的知己好友吗？”她只这样问。

===Original===

“Well, sometimes I think she is and sometimes I think she isn’t. That’s what she’s like.”

“I know. You tell her something and then you wish you hadn’t. Have you ever told her anything about me?”

“Oh, Miss Mole, yes!” Ethel cried, for she was truthful and her deceptions were only for herself. “But only about those mattresses.”

“Well, what else was there to tell?” Hannah asked grimly. “You did all you could, it seems to me. Never mind, never mind! Don’t cry. You cry far too easily and it’s not becoming and they’ll know you’ve been doing it when you go into the other room. Stop it!” Hannah cried.

“But you’ll think I’m a sneak and you’ve been so kind to me lately.”

“I’ll always be kind to you if you’ll let me,” Hannah said.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，有时候我觉得她是，有时候又觉得她不是。她就是这么个人。”

“我知道。你告诉了她一桩事，回头就巴不得自己没说过。关于我的事，你同她说过什么没有？”

“哦，莫尔小姐，说过！”埃塞尔嚷道——她天性诚实，自欺还使得，骗人的话却从来不会说。“可只说过那几床垫子的事。”

“唔，除了那个，还有什么可讲的？”汉娜冷冷地问。“依我看，凡是能说的你都替我说尽了。不要紧，不要紧！别哭。你也太爱哭了，怪不好看的；再进那间屋，人家一眼就看出你哭过。别哭了！”汉娜喝道。

“可你会当我是个打小报告的小人，再说你近来待我这么好。”

“只要你肯，我什么时候都待你好，”汉娜说。

===Original===

“It wasn’t that I wanted to tell tales, but I did want to talk to somebody.”

“Then talk to me in future.”

“Did she tell Father?” Ethel asked in a strangled whisper.

“I don’t know.”

“Because, if she did﻿—Oh, there! they’re calling me. I’ll come back next time I’m out.”

Wilfrid changed places with Ethel. “There’s trouble brewing,” he said.

===Chinese===

“我不是存心搬弄是非，可我实在想找个人说说话。”

“那往后就说给我听。”

“她告诉爸爸了吗？”埃塞尔憋着嗓子、气声儿地问。

“我不知道。”

“因为，要是她说了——哦，糟了！他们叫我了。下回我出来，再来找你。”

威尔弗里德同埃塞尔换班来了。“麻烦正在酿着呢，”他说。

===Original===

“Man is born to trouble﻿—And poke the fire for me, please.”

“What I like about you, Mona Lisa﻿—”

“Yes, yes, I should love to hear it, but what’s the trouble?”

“What I like about you is your allusive and elusive mind. There you are! The sparks are flying upward. And, of course, there are lots of other things I like.”

“What’s the trouble?” Hannah repeated self-denyingly.

“Revolt’s the trouble. Howard says he’s chucking the ministry and all Mrs. Spenser-Smith’s good gold will be chucked away too. What do you think of that? She sent him to the University to make a high-class little minister of him and he says he won’t be a little minister. He’s just broken the news, and they’re arguing about it now instead of getting on with the game. So we’re going to have a happy Christmas, and I shall go as early as possible to my poor dear mother and stay with her as long as she’ll keep me. He’ll have a hell of a time, but it’s better than having it for life.”

===Chinese===

“人生在世必遇患难——劳驾，替我把火拨一拨。”

“我喜欢你的一点，蒙娜丽莎——”

“是是是，我爱听极了，可到底是什么麻烦？”

“我喜欢你的，是你那颗既爱引经据典、又叫人捉摸不透的心。瞧，应验了！火星飞腾上去了。当然啦，我喜欢你的地方还多着呢。”

“到底是什么麻烦？”汉娜强自克己，又问了一遍。

“麻烦就是反叛。霍华德说，他不当牧师了，斯宾塞-史密斯太太那些实打实的金子也就跟着一起扔掉了。你看这叫什么事？她送他上大学，要把他造成一个上等的小牧师，他却说他偏不要做小牧师。他刚把消息捅出来，这会儿他们正吵呢，游戏也顾不得玩了。这么说，我们要过一个快快活活的圣诞节了；我要尽早动身去我那可怜的好妈妈那儿，她肯留我多久，我就住多久。他要过一阵地狱似的日子，可总比过一辈子强。”

===Original===

Hannah sighed. “Why do people want to give each other hell?”

“Because it makes them feel like God. And it’s so easy. Now the uncle﻿—”

“Be careful,” Hannah said. “He’s in the hall, getting the letters.”

“Yes, he likes turning over the letters. God, again! He has to know everything. And yet, Mona Lisa, to do the man justice, he’s a benevolent deity in the chapel. But that’s easy, too, when you come to think of it. His people get the reward of their obedience, and so would his children if they’d be what he considers good, which means admiring and believing in papa. Ruth’s quite right. It isn’t good for people to be ministers and there are times when I’m sorry for the poor devil. If you see yourself as the centre of the universe﻿—”

“I should have thought that’s what you do yourself.”

“Yes, but I know I’m doing it. That makes all the difference. Now the uncle﻿—”

===Chinese===

汉娜叹了口气。“人们干吗偏要叫彼此受地狱的罪呢？”

“因为这样让他们觉得自己像上帝，况且又那么容易。就说那位舅舅——”

“小心，”汉娜说。“他在门厅里取信呢。”

“是啊，他喜欢翻检信件。瞧，又当上上帝了！他什么都得知道。不过，蒙娜丽莎，说句公道话，此人在礼拜堂里倒是一位仁慈的神明。可你细想想，那也容易：他的子民顺服，便得奖赏；他的儿女只要肯做他眼里的好人，也一样得赏——所谓好，无非是崇拜爸爸、相信爸爸。露丝说得顶对。人当牧师没好处，有时候我也替那可怜人难过。要是你把自己看作宇宙的中心——”

“我还当你自己正是这么干的呢。”

“是啊，可我知道自己是在干。这就全然两样了。就说那位舅舅——”

===Original===

“I ought not to be listening to all this,” Hannah said, but she liked having the boy there, sitting on the hearthrug with his back against a chair and his arms hugging his knees. She could pretend that thus her own son would have dealt with her and she with him, while she knew that the very lack of those demands which a mother and son make of each other was what constituted the charm of her relationship with Wilfrid.

“Oh, nonsense,” he said, “you and I are the only reasonable human beings in the house. Howard’s all right, but he’s dull, and depressed, poor lad. Ethel’s trying to make him change his mind and Ruth’s persuading him to keep the bomb until her Uncle Jim arrives.”

“Ruth seems to think her Uncle Jim’s omnipotent,” Hannah said, with the suspicion of a sniff.

“Well, he’ll take part of the shock. It was silly of Howard to tell Ethel. She’s bound to blurt it out. She can’t see a difficulty without bumping into it, or giving such a jump that everybody else begins looking for it.”

He stopped speaking as Robert Corder came into the room, with a letter in his hand. “This is for you, Miss Mole,” he said, giving it to her slowly, and he looked at her curiously and then at Wilfrid impatiently. “I thought you were playing with the others.”

“So I am, sir. I’m waiting till they call me in. It’s one of those games where there’s more waiting than playing.”

===Chinese===

“这些话我本不该听，”汉娜说。可她喜欢这孩子待在这儿：坐在炉前地毯上，背靠一把椅子，双臂搂住膝盖。她尽可以假想，倘若她自己有个儿子，他同她相处便是这般光景，她也这般待他；但她心里明白，她同威尔弗里德这份情谊的迷人之处，恰恰在于母子之间那种彼此相求的东西，在这里一样也没有。

“哦，胡说，”他说，“这满屋子的人，你我是仅有的两个明白人。霍华德人不坏，就是乏味，还蔫头蔫脑的，可怜的家伙。埃塞尔正劝他回心转意，露丝则在撺掇他把这颗炸弹留到她吉姆舅舅来了再引爆。”

“露丝好像当她吉姆舅舅无所不能似的，”汉娜说，鼻子里似有若无地哼了一声。

“唔，他好歹能分担一份惊吓。霍华德先去告诉埃塞尔，真是傻。她迟早会脱口说漏。她见了难处，没有不一头撞上去的；不然就猛地一跳，惊得旁人都跟着去找那难处在哪儿。”

罗伯特·科德拿着一封信走进屋来，他住了口。“这是你的信，莫尔小姐，”他说，慢吞吞地递给她，一面好奇地端详她，又转向威尔弗里德，满脸的不耐烦。“我还当你在跟他们玩呢。”

“是在玩呢，先生。我等着他们来叫我进场。这种游戏，等的工夫比玩的多。”

===Original===

Without examining it, Hannah had dropped the letter on to her knee, with the address turned upwards. “And they begin talking about something else,” she said.

“Oh yes,” Wilfrid returned her smile, “they’re doing that.”

“Well,” said Robert Corder, “I hope you are not disturbing Miss Mole.”

“No, we’re talking about something else, too,” she said pleasantly. “And I can sew at the same time. A woman can always do two things at once. If she couldn’t, she’d have a dreary time of it.”

“I often envy women,” Robert Corder said. “They have useful and not exacting occupation for their hands, and no labour need be dreary.”

To this, neither Wilfrid nor Miss Mole ventured a reply, and Robert Corder retired after another look at the letter on Hannah’s knee.

===Chinese===

汉娜看也没看，就把信搁在了膝头，有地址的那一面朝上。“他们还会聊起别的事情来，”她说。

“可不是嘛，”威尔弗里德回她一笑，“他们正是在聊呢。”

“呃，”罗伯特·科德说，“但愿你们没有搅扰莫尔小姐。”

“没有，我们也在聊别的事呢，”她和颜悦色地说。“再说我还一边做着针线。女人向来能同时做两件事。若是不能，这日子可就闷透了。”

“我常常羡慕女人，”罗伯特·科德说。“她们的手上有顶用又不费神的活计可做，再怎样的劳作也不至于沉闷。”

对这话，威尔弗里德和莫尔小姐都没敢答腔；罗伯特·科德又看了一眼汉娜膝上的那封信，便退了出去。

===Original===

“He wants to know who your letter’s from, he wants to know what we’re talking about, he wants to talk to somebody himself. You ought to have encouraged him. Mona Lisa.”

“Ought I?”

“Yes.” He nodded his head sagely. “Just for the good of the community.”

“Why didn’t you do it, then?”

“He hates the sight of me,” Wilfrid said. “Too much like my father. But you want to read your letter.”

“I’m not sure that I do. I don’t know who it’s from.”

When she had read it, she realized that Robert Corder must have recognised the clerkly hand of Mr. Blenkinsop who asked her to tea with him early the next week. “I haven’t a hat fit to wear,” was Hannah’s first thought, and the second was one of impatience that Mr. Blenkinsop could not manage his affairs without support, but there was something flattering in his desire to see her, and something touching, and, as she thought of him, who looked so self-sufficient, she was bound to wonder if Robert Corder, also, was not as much a baby as the rest of them. Mr. Blenkinsop was a solemn infant who asked for what he wanted and Robert Corder was a spoilt one who expected his needs to be divined.

===Chinese===

“他想知道你的信是谁写来的，想知道我们在聊什么，他自己也想找个人说话。你本该鼓动鼓动他的，蒙娜丽莎。”

“是吗？”

“是。”他煞有介事地点点头。“就算是为了大家的福利嘛。”

“那你怎么不这么做呢？”

“他一见我就头疼，”威尔弗里德说。“我太像我父亲了。不过，你想看你的信了。”

“我也说不准想不想看。我不知道是谁写来的。”

信读完了，她才明白过来：罗伯特·科德必定认出了布伦金索普先生那一手账房先生似的工整字迹——布伦金索普先生请她下星期早些时候到他那儿去吃茶。汉娜的头一个念头是：她没有一顶见得了人的帽子；第二个念头是一阵不耐烦：这位布伦金索普先生，办自己的事竟少不得有人撑腰。可他想见她这件事里，有几分抬举人的意味，也有几分惹人心软的地方；想着他那样一个看上去万事自足的人，她不由得要琢磨：罗伯特·科德是不是也同旁人一样，是个不折不扣的婴孩。布伦金索普先生是个一本正经的小娃娃，要什么，自己开口讨；罗伯特·科德则是个娇宠坏了的小娃娃，只等着旁人把他的需求猜出来、伺候周到。

## XXII / 第二十二章

===Original===

Robert Corder’s work took him out of the house for a great part of the day, but it also brought him back at times when a business man is in his office and knows nothing of domestic affairs until he returns to find a meal awaiting him and the work of the day apparently done. Robert Corder was conscious perforce of the doings of the household. He would swing down the garden path in the morning and meet the butcher’s boy carrying a recognisable joint, and if the joint did not appear in a cooked condition that evening, he would wonder where it was and why Miss Mole had ordered it a day before it was needed, or he would come back and see half the drawing-room furniture in the hall and get a glimpse of Miss Mole doing something with a duster or a feather broom. His study was never disturbed in this manner. The fire was lighted before breakfast, and the room, presumably, dusted and swept, and when he was in the house he chose to sit there, but, even in that sanctuary, he could not be unaware of sounds and movements. He could hear the front door and the back door bells when they were rung and the thumping feet of Doris as she ran up the three stairs from the kitchen, and if she did not introduce a visitor into the study, he would, as likely as not, hear her voice, with its Radstowe burr, calling Miss Mole to settle some question with which she could not deal herself. He could distinguish Ethel’s prancing step from Miss Mole’s quick, even one, and sometimes, when he heard the front door shut, he would stroll to his window to see whether it was his housekeeper or his daughter who had gone out. When it was his daughter, he would have a slight feeling of discomfort: when it was Miss Mole he felt an active irritation. There was something wrong with Ethel’s appearance and he could not give it a name, but he knew she did not look definitely feminine, like Miss Patsy Withers, in spite of her bright colours, or completely unconscious of herself, as her mother had been, and she certainly was not pretty. That, perhaps, was a good thing. It would have been a great and highly distasteful anxiety to have had a daughter who attracted young men, and Ethel had given her heart to her work and seemed to be content. People spoke very well of her in the chapel and told him he should be as proud of such a daughter as she should be of such a father. This praise of her seemed to him somewhat exaggerated, but there was no doubt she was a good girl and her fits of passion seemed to be less frequent, but it was difficult to be patient with her nervousness and secretly he resented, while he benefited from, her protective plainness.

When he watched Miss Mole it was with definitely antagonistic feelings. The alert bearing of her head, her quick step, seemed to him unsuitable in a housekeeper, and arrogant in a woman who had no pretensions to good looks. If she had to be plain and thin, she should also have been meek, and he supposed it was possible to be domestically intelligent without looking as though she had some secret source of satisfaction in herself. That look had been growing on her lately and he could not but connect it with the visit and the letter of Mr. Blenkinsop. He had seen her as an unfortunate woman, undesired by any man, and he had despised her accordingly, and with the suspicion that Samuel Blenkinsop had found something in her which he had missed himself, he immediately set about searching for it and felt uneasy. He remembered, too, his discovery of Wilfrid, sitting on the floor and talking to her, not dutifully, but with enjoyment sparkling in his dark eyes, and it was increasingly evident that Ruth liked her.

Robert Corder did not understand that he was always jealous of those who gave to someone who was not himself and of those who received: what he did realise was that Miss Mole had some mysterious power to make certain people like her and that she did not like him, and as it was impossible for him to like a person first, he remained in his state of irritated interest and curiosity. Mrs. Spenser-Smith had been wise in her choice of a housekeeper: his house was well kept, his food was well cooked, and Ruth looked healthier, but Mrs. Spenser-Smith probably had no experience of Miss Mole as an inmate of her house, of this personality which gave every promise of being properly negative and then developed signs of positive character. Not once, since her arrival, had Miss Mole asked Robert Corder’s advice. The kitchen range, the gas, the hot water, might go wrong, and Miss Mole was equal to them or found someone else to manage them; she had no difficulty in keeping her accounts; she brought him no tales of dilatory or dishonest tradesmen. This peace from domestic care was what he wanted, what Mrs. Spenser-Smith had told him he ought to have and what Ethel had not been able to give him, but he would have been better pleased if he could have believed that Miss Mole was making determined efforts and overcoming her natural disabilities for his sake, instead of taking everything in her easy stride. He knew nothing of Miss Mole’s real difficulties, of the constant caution necessary to prevent Ethel’s jealousy when any two members of the family paid more attention to each other than they did to her, to give her encouragement without raising false hopes, to damp down her threatened returns to sentimentality about Wilfrid, to give her the position of mistress of the house while she did nothing to sustain it. Miss Mole had to intervene in the quarrels which sprang up so quickly between the sisters, to avoid giving evidence of the sympathy between Wilfrid and herself, to steer conversations into safe waters and, above all, to conceal the control she really exercised. It was exhausting work but it did not exist for Robert Corder. The family of the minister of Beresford Road Chapel should be, and therefore was, a happy one, and his own griefs, his consciousness of the different treatment he received in the chapel from what he received at home, were not allowed to mar the concord, but it seemed strange to him that while Mrs. Spenser-Smith should be ready, almost too ready at times, to help him and Miss Patsy Withers brought her rather touchingly simple little problems for him to solve, his own children should give him little and ask for less. There were no signs of Howard’s improvement in this or any other direction. He showed no affection and no enthusiasm, and Robert Corder was confirmed in his opinion that it was a mistake to make things too easy for a young man: his father had fought for his position and kept it; Howard accepted his advantages and did not use them. Robert Corder could speak of these dissatisfactions to no one, now that his wife had gone, and while she lived he had hardly noticed them, and there had been no Miss Mole to add to them. To discuss Miss Mole with Mrs. Spenser-Smith would be to criticize her judgment; to deplore the faults of his children might be to admit failures of his own, and when Miss Withers referred to them in terms of praise he could not buy her sympathy at the cost of her envy of a life in which work of eminent importance was combined with the amenities of the happy home he had made, yet it comforted him that she knew of the little rift in the lute for which he could not possibly be blamed. She seemed to have an intuitive distrust of Miss Mole which agreed with his own, and it was increased, no doubt, by Ethel’s story of the mattresses. It was wrong of Ethel not to have told him. It was a small matter, but it was indicative, and it was necessary for him to keep an eye on the stranger in the house. If it had not been Wilfrid’s mattress, he would have taken action, but he could not pamper the boy, even in the cause of discipline.

===Chinese===

罗伯特·科德的职事让他一天中大半光阴在外，却也偏偏叫他赶在生意人端坐办公室的时辰回得家来——生意人对家中诸事一无所知，要直到晚间回去，见一餐饭伺候在桌上、一天的活计表面俨然停当，方才知晓家里有过什么。罗伯特·科德却身不由己，对这个家的所作所为知道得清清楚楚。清早他顺着园中小径摇摇摆摆地走，会迎面碰上肉铺的小伙计，拎着一块一眼认得出的大块肉；倘若那块肉到晚上没以熟食的面目上桌，他就要纳闷它去了哪里、莫尔小姐又何必在还用不着它的前一天就把它订下；要不就是他回家时，撞见半个客厅的家具都搬进了门厅，瞥一眼莫尔小姐正拿着抹布或鸡毛掸子鼓捣着什么。他的书房倒从不曾受这般搅扰。早饭前炉火生好，屋子想必也掸过扫过了；他在家时便乐意坐在里面。可即便是那方圣殿，他也免不了听见种种声响、看见种种动静。前后门的铃一拉，他听得见；多丽丝从厨房蹬蹬蹬奔上那三级台阶的脚步声，他听得见；她若没把来客引进书房，十有八九，他会听见她那口带拉德斯托土腔的嗓门喊莫尔小姐，请她去裁断一桩她自己拿不下的事。埃塞尔那蹦蹦跳跳的步子，同莫尔小姐那轻快匀整的步子，他分辨得出；有时听见前门砰地一关，他还会踱到窗前，看看出去的究竟是管家还是女儿。出去的是女儿，他心头便掠过一丝不自在；出去的是莫尔小姐，他便登时生起一股活生生的火气。埃塞尔的模样总有哪儿不对，他又叫不出名目；他知道，尽管衣裳穿得花哨，她却不像佩茜·威瑟斯小姐那样透着十足的女性气，也不像她母亲当年那样浑然不觉自己是什么模样，而且她自然算不得漂亮。这或许是桩好事。倘若养了个招小伙子们喜欢的女儿，那才是又大又极不称心的一块心病；埃塞尔把心都扑在了工作上，看上去心满意足。礼拜堂里众人对她称道备至，还对他说：他有这样一个女儿，该多么骄傲，正如女儿有他这样一位父亲该多么骄傲。这份夸奖在他听来未免有些过甚，可她无疑是个好姑娘，性子发作的次数似乎也见少；只是她那副神经质叫人难以耐着性子对付；至于她那副替她挡事的朴素相貌，他受其庇护，却又暗自记恨。

他瞧着莫尔小姐的时候，怀的是不折不扣的敌意。她那颗头昂得警觉有神，她那双脚步轻快利落，在他看来，出于管家是不称职分，出于一个与美貌无缘的女人，便是傲慢。既然她命定又平又瘦，那就还该添一副温顺脾性；他想，一个人持家能干是一回事，总犯不着满脸挂着仿佛她自身藏了件什么暗自得意的宝贝。近来这副神情在她脸上与日俱长，他不能不把它同布伦金索普先生的那次来访、那封来信联系起来。他原把她当作一个不幸的女人、一个没有哪个男人要的女人，也据此看轻她；如今起了疑心，疑心塞缪尔·布伦金索普竟在她身上发现了自己漏看的东西，他立刻动手搜寻，心里老大不踏实。他还记得撞见威尔弗里德的那一幕：那孩子坐在地板上同她说话，不是出于本分，而是黑眼睛里闪着乐子；露丝喜欢她，也一日比一日藏不住了。

罗伯特·科德并不明白：凡见人把什么给了旁人而不是给他，凡见人从旁人手里受些什么，他都要嫉妒；他明白的只是：莫尔小姐有某种神秘的本事，能叫某些人喜欢她，而她偏偏不喜欢他；既然要他先去喜欢一个人是万万不能的，他便一直困在这份恼人的兴味与好奇里出不来。斯宾塞-史密斯太太挑管家确有眼光：他的家料理得井井有条，他的饭食做得妥妥帖帖，露丝看着也壮实了些；只是莫尔小姐住进她府上是副什么光景，斯宾塞-史密斯太太多半没有领教过——这一个性子，起先处处许诺它将恰如其分地没有个性，随后却日渐冒出个性鲜明的苗头。莫尔小姐自打进门，从没向罗伯特·科德讨过一回主意。厨房的灶、煤气、热水，出了毛病就出了毛病，莫尔小姐要么亲自收拾，要么另请高明；账目她管得清清爽爽；拖沓误事的、手脚不老实的商号，也从没上过她的舌头。这份从家事操劳里解脱出来的清净，正是他要的，正是斯宾塞-史密斯太太说他理该享有的，正是埃塞尔始终没能给他的；只是，倘若他能相信莫尔小姐是为了他的缘故才狠下力气、克服她天性中的种种短处，而不是什么都迈着轻快的步子一跨而过，他本会更满意些。莫尔小姐真正的难处，他一概不知：这家里但凡有哪两个人彼此上心多过对埃塞尔上心，就得时刻小心提防她的醋劲；既要给她鼓励，又不可叫她生出非分的指望；她对威尔弗里德那套伤感情调眼看要故态复萌，又得赶紧压下去；要让她占着当家主妇的名分——尽管她自己半点也不出力撑持这个名分。姐妹俩一触即发的口角，莫尔小姐得居中排解；她同威尔弗里德之间的那点默契，得掩得不露一丝痕迹；话头的航向，得拨进安全的水域；而顶要紧的，是她实际执掌着的这份权柄，得瞒个滴水不漏。这营生累人，可在罗伯特·科德眼里，它压根儿不存在。贝雷斯福德路礼拜堂的牧师，他的一家理应和乐，因此也就和乐；他自己的那些伤心处——他在礼拜堂里受的待遇同他在家里受的待遇何其悬殊，这一点他有自知——一概不许有损这份和气。可他纳闷的是：斯宾塞-史密斯太太帮他的忙随时都乐意，有时甚至热心得过了头；佩茜·威瑟斯小姐也把她那点简单得惹人怜爱的小难题捧来求他排解；偏偏自己亲生的儿女，给他的少，向他讨的更少。在这一方面，在任何别的方面，霍华德都瞧不见起色。他既无亲情，也无热忱；罗伯特·科德愈发认定自己的高见：把一个年轻人的路铺得太平是错的。他父亲当年是拼来的位置，也守住了；霍华德坐享现成的好处，却不去用它。这些不平，如今妻子故去，他对谁也说不得；她在世时他几乎不曾留意，那时节也还没有一个莫尔小姐来雪上加霜。同斯宾塞-史密斯太太议论莫尔小姐，无异于非议她的眼力；叹惜儿女的毛病，又近乎招认自己的失职；而威瑟斯小姐提起他们来向来满口褒奖，他总不能拿她的同情作代价，去换她的艳羡——艳羡那样一种人生：身负头等要紧的圣工，又兼有他亲手营造的和乐家庭之种种安适。然而有一点到底宽慰着他：琴上那道小裂痕她是知道的，而这道裂痕断然怨不得他。她对莫尔小姐似乎凭直觉就不信任，同他自己的疑心正相投合；而这疑心无疑又叫埃塞尔讲的那桩床垫事加深了一层。埃塞尔不曾告诉他，是她不对。事情虽小，却是个征兆；家里住着这么个外人，他不能不留意。倘若那不是威尔弗里德的床垫，他早就有所动作了；可他不能娇惯这孩子，哪怕是为了立规矩。

===Original===

Such thoughts as these underlay his life when he was in the house; outside it, they were forgotten if no one jogged his memory, and he strode about the streets, visited the sick, attended his committee meetings, with an energy and liveliness which gave him a reputation as a man of action. His actions were generally those of his committees, but his enjoyment of an authority which never presented itself to his mind as being safely diluted, his look of vitality, his handsome head, his conviction that he was of importance in the religious and civic life of Radstowe, had a suggestive value for others as well as for himself. To those who were sad or sick, his entrance was reanimating; knowing this, he was able to help them as he sincerely desired to do, and it was inevitable that he should suffer from a feeling of neglect and of misvaluation when he returned to his home where his importance and his usefulness were diminished by the dullness, or the wilfulness of its inmates.

He had no great power for concentration and no desire for peace in which to meditate or to read and, unless he had callers to interview, he had little to do in the house when his letters were written and his sermons prepared, and he had time and willingness to listen to the sounds of voices, feet, bells and doors, to conjecture or to criticise. He would hear a murmuring from the kitchen as he passed through the hall and, sometimes, a burst of laughter, and making some excuse, such as the need for another pair of boots, he would penetrate into the kitchen regions and find Miss Mole and Doris mysteriously busy at the table or the stove, when he had expected to find one or both of them idle.

===Chinese===

这些念头是他居家度日的底色；出了门，若无人从旁提醒，便忘个干净。他在街上大步流星，探望病人，出席各项委员会的会议，那股劲头与活气替他挣得了一个“实干家”的名声。他的作为大抵就是各委员会的作为；可他玩味着的那份权威——它在他心目中从不曾显出其实早已稀释得稳妥无害的底相——加上他一脸的生命力、一颗漂亮的头颅，以及他那股自信：自认在拉德斯托的宗教生活与市民生活里举足轻重——这一切对他自己，也对旁人，都有一种提气的暗示价值。伤心的、病着的，见他进门便觉回过一口气来；他深知这一点，也就能照他真心所愿去帮助他们；于是回到自己家里，难免尝到被冷落、被低估的滋味——在这户人家里，他的要紧与有用，叫住客们的呆钝或任性给削了价。

他凝神专注的本事不大，也不想要一处静地来冥想或读书；信写完了，讲道章备好了，家里又没有来客要接见时，他便闲得没什么可做，于是有的是工夫、也有的是兴致，去听人声、脚步、门铃与关门声，好猜详个究竟，或数落两句。穿过门厅时，他会听见厨房里一片嗡嗡低语，有时还迸出一阵笑；他便寻个由头——比方说还缺一双靴子——一头扎进厨房的地界，却见莫尔小姐和多丽丝正围着桌子或炉子神秘地忙着什么，而他原先料定的，是她们俩总有一个闲着，兴许两个都闲着。

===Original===

“The Christmas puddings are ready for stirring,” Hannah said to him on one of these occasions. “Will you have your stir now?”

“My stir?”

“Everybody has to stir, for luck.”

Doris turned aside with a giggle. She was embarrassed by the presence of the minister in the kitchen and by Miss Mole’s airy way with him, and Robert Corder, interpreting this sound correctly and reacting to it immediately, took the opportunity to prove his essential homeliness. He stirred manfully, circling the wooden spoon through the unwieldy mixture and an unmistakable scent, very pleasant but forbidden, rose to his nostrils.

===Chinese===

“圣诞布丁该搅起来了，”有一回，汉娜对他说。“要不要现在就把您那一下搅了？”

“您的那一下？”

“人人都得搅一下，图个吉利。”

多丽丝扭过脸去咯咯一笑。牧师大人驾临厨房，再加上莫尔小姐同他说话那股轻飘随便的劲儿，都叫她好不窘迫；罗伯特·科德却把这声笑听了个准，当下便有了反应，趁势要证明他骨子里的居家本色。他抖擞起男子汉气概搅动起来，木勺在那团庞大笨重的浆料里画着圈子；一股错不了的香气直冲他的鼻孔——香得叫人受用，却是违禁之物。

===Original===

“I hope there is no brandy in it,” he said seriously.

Miss Mole looked disappointed. “I know some people prefer beer,” she said, “but I think brandy’s better. I ought to have asked you.”

He dropped the spoon. “But Miss Mole, you must know we never have intoxicants in this house, and I happen to be the president of the Radstowe Temperance Society.”

===Chinese===

“但愿这里头没放白兰地，”他正色道。

莫尔小姐一脸失望。“我知道有些人宁可搁啤酒，”她说，“可我觉得还是白兰地好。早该先问您一声的。”

他手一松，木勺掉了。“可是莫尔小姐，您总该知道，我们这个家是从来不进醉人的东西的，何况我恰好还当着拉德斯托戒酒会（Radstowe Temperance Society）的会长。”

===Original===

“Does it count,” she asked meekly, “when it’s cooked and in a pudding? I’m so sorry. What can we do about it? I can’t possibly eat all these puddings by myself.”

“Waste would only be an aggravation of the mistake,” he said sharply, “but, another time, Miss Mole﻿ ﻿…”

He went away, very much disturbed. Surely the woman could not be as simple as she seemed, and if she was not, what was she? And this was the sort of thing which could be ignored in an ordinary household but was of real importance in one like this. Doris would spread the story; it might have a far-reaching effect on the girl’s attitude towards the drink question, and probably the brandy had been bought through that young man whose relationship to Doris had affected Robert Corder’s own attitude towards the girl, as towards someone not quite unspotted from the world of the flesh, and it would be impossible to offer Christmas pudding to any visitor to the house.

===Chinese===

“煮熟了、装进布丁里，那还算不算数呢？”她怯生生地问。“实在对不起。这可怎么好呢？这么些布丁，总不能叫我一个人统统吃光呀。”

“糟蹋了食物，只会叫这错处再添一层，”他尖刻地说，“不过，下回，莫尔小姐……”

他走开了，心绪乱得很。这女人总不至于真像她看上去那样懵懂吧？倘若不是，那她又算个什么？这等事，搁在寻常人家原可以睁一眼闭一眼，搁在这样一户人家里却是实实在在的要紧事。多丽丝会把这桩事传扬出去；对这姑娘在饮酒问题上的态度，它的影响或许深远得很；那瓶白兰地多半还是经那个小伙子买来的——他同多丽丝的那层关系，本就左右了罗伯特·科德对这姑娘的看法，把她看作一个未能完全保守自己“不沾染世俗”的人；而往后，圣诞布丁是断不能拿去款待任何来客了。

===Original===

He returned hurriedly to the kitchen.

“And the mincemeat?” he enquired.

“I’m afraid that is contaminated, too,” Miss Mole replied.

===Chinese===

他又急急赶回厨房。

“那甜果馅呢？”他问。

“恐怕那个也一样不干净了，”莫尔小姐答道。

===Original===

No, she was not simple, and he remembered the mattresses. He had an impulse to order the destruction of the savoury mess, to let the family go puddingless at Christmas, but he hesitated and the ardour of his indignation left him, and he did nothing. What he needed in his house was a little committee of people like himself which would frame and pass resolutions and give orders in which responsibility was shared. Alone, he felt that, in some way he would be baffled and his position worsened, but when the pudding appeared on Christmas Day he would quietly refuse to eat it and he hoped Miss Mole would feel ashamed. Then swiftly, with the glibness acquired by answering awkward questions at his class for young men, he arranged such tolerant, humorous comments as might be needed, on domestic accidents and the lenience which must be extended to others in affairs of conscience, but he felt very bitter towards a woman who put him to these shifts and, as the object of his suspicions and dislike, she became oddly fascinating: he liked looking at her and despising her for her lack of beauty, he enjoyed listening to her and silently sneering at her remarks: he was puzzled by the frankness which alternated with her slyness and he did not see how he was to get rid of her without less petty reasons to offer Mrs. Spenser-Smith, a practical woman who would make nothing of the little objections he could name and who would not understand that a mere personality could trouble his peace of mind. He saw himself permanently saddled with Miss Mole and, two days after the pudding incident, he found more causes for mistrustful speculation.

===Chinese===

可不是，她并不懵懂，他又记起那桩床垫的事来。他一时冲动，很想下令把那堆香喷喷的混账浆料统统毁掉，叫全家过上一个没有布丁的圣诞节；可他迟疑了一下，义愤的烈火便凉了半截，终究什么也没做。他家里缺的是一个小小的委员会，全员都同他一个模子，好拟定决议、通过决议、下达命令——责任嘛，人人有份。单枪匹马，他就觉得自己总会在什么地方着了道儿，处境反倒愈发不妙；不过等到圣诞节那天布丁端上桌，他要不动声色地拒绝吃它，他还指望莫尔小姐会为此害臊。跟着他又飞快地——使出他在青年班上应付刁钻提问练出来的那副利嘴——把万一需要的宽厚诙谐的解嘲话排布停当：论家中失手的小事啦，论良心问题上待人总得容让几分啦。可他把逼得他出这些权变的女人恨到深处；而她呢，作为他疑忌与厌恶的靶子，偏又透出一种说不出的迷人劲儿：他喜欢端详她，为她的不美而鄙薄她；喜欢听她说话，又对着她的话头暗暗讥诮；她那与狡黠交替出现的坦率叫他摸不着头脑；他也看不出怎样才能打发她走——除非他能拿些不那么琐屑的理由去向斯宾塞-史密斯太太交代。那是个讲实际的女人，他数得出的那几样小指摘，到她跟前根本不成话；她也决计不会明白，单单一副性子就能搅扰他心里的安宁。他眼看自己被莫尔小姐套牢了，永世不得脱身；布丁事件过后两天，他又找到了更多可供疑神疑鬼的由头。

===Original===

He saw her go down the garden path in what might be taken for her best apparel and, as she went up the road, she waved a hand gaily towards the next door house. It was growing dusk, but he saw her hand plainly in its light glove and the light glove offended him. She was not at the tea table and when he asked where she was Ethel reported that she had gone out. He made a dubious sound and Ethel, tactlessly loyal to Miss Mole, said quickly: “She has to go out sometimes.”

“I am quite aware of that, Ethel. Do you know where she has gone?”

“She said she was going to see Mrs. Gibson first,” Ethel said, and he allowed a sufficient pause to elapse before he made some reference to Mr. Samson. Had anyone seen him lately? Did he still live alone?

===Chinese===

他看见她走下园中小径，身上那套装束简直当得起她的压箱行头；等她走上大路，她还朝隔壁那座房子眉飞色舞地挥了挥手。暮色渐浓，可他把她那只手看得清清楚楚——浅色手套里的一只手——冒犯了他的，正是那浅色手套。吃茶的时候，茶桌旁没有她；他问她在哪儿，埃塞尔回说她出门了。他喉咙里哼出一声不置可否的音儿；埃塞尔对莫尔小姐忠心，却忠得不识时务，赶忙说：“她总也有不得不出门的时候。”

“这我知道得很清楚，埃塞尔。你可知道她上哪儿去了？”

“她说她要先去看看吉布森太太，”埃塞尔说。他容一段足足有余的沉默流了过去，才状似无意地把桑普森先生提了一提：近来可有谁见过他？他还是独自一个人住着么？

===Original===

Ethel said she had not seen, and was not interested in, the horrid old man, but Ruth said, a little shyly: “He isn’t horrid. It’s just his face and that’s not his fault. Miss Mole says she thinks he’s rather nice, like everybody else when you get to know them.”

“And how has Miss Mole discovered this?”

“Oh, she just knows things like that,” Ruth said contentedly, pretending not to notice her father’s covert sneer.

===Chinese===

埃塞尔说，那可怕的老头儿她没见过，也没兴趣；露丝却带几分羞怯地说：“他才不可怕呢。不过是那张脸罢了，那又不是他的错。莫尔小姐说她觉得他人挺好——人都一样，处熟了就好。”

“那莫尔小姐又是怎么发现的呢？”

“哦，这种事她天生就知道，”露丝心满意足地说，只装没瞧见父亲那记藏在暗里的讥诮。

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===

Hannah accepted life as she found it, she accepted imperfection, but this did not prevent her from pondering on human existence in her own fashion. Finding pleasure and excitement in little things, because there were no others, seeing those about her doing the same, though, as she believed, with less intensity, she could still wonder whether more was not expected of human beings, or whether, in getting food and shelter and what happiness came their way, they were doing all that was required of them. Robert Corder could see himself as a man with a definite mission against evil; Wilfrid, perhaps, was somewhat shamefacedly hoping to alleviate the physical sufferings of men, but the mass of people was like herself, without her special advantages, living from one day of small things to another, grateful when sorrow was avoided and pathetically thankful for peace, and in a world which knew the procession of sun and moon and stars, the miracle of spring and the pageantry of autumn, the occupations of these two-legged creatures, running about the earth, seemed insufficient. They vexed themselves about things of no apparent moment, such as mattresses; they became excited over a mild entertainment, such as having tea with Mr. Blenkinsop; they wasted time, which ought surely to have been given to some higher purpose, in trying new modes of hairdressing for the benefit of a strange sailor who would not notice them. But what were these higher purposes and who pursued them, outside the special band of thinkers and creators? And to these people did their efforts sometimes seem as unimportant as hers to Hannah Mole? She envied artists with their definite objects and work which seemed to them imperative, but envy was one of the emotions she never allowed herself for long, and it vanished when she realised that she, too, should be an artist in her own sphere, that, indeed, she was already doing almost her best to be one, and that the results of her labours, if they were good, were no more to be despised than was a painting of a Dutch interior because it did not deal with gods and goddesses.

This decision to be a conscientious artist was an inspiriting one, particularly as it exacted the expression of her own, and not Robert Corder’s, conception of what the housekeeper to a Nonconformist minister should be. It was comforting to think there would be disloyalty in pandering to his notions, for in her relations with Robert Corder she sometimes felt guilty. She who boasted that she could like anybody, had not tried to like him, yet Wilfrid had told her that a little encouragement would be for the good of the community. The artist, however, did not consider the good of the community. The best work was not done in that way, and Hannah felt perfectly free to go on disliking Robert Corder and to get from that exercise the peculiar kind of pleasure which, as she suspected, he was beginning to get out of his dislike for her. She was cleverer at disguising her feelings than he was, cleverer in every way, she believed, and her pleasure would increase in proportion to her concealment and his revelations. She must be allowed this joy, she told herself: moreover, nothing was more dangerous than going contrary to nature for the sake of an imagined future good, and, if future good must be considered, what could be better for Robert Corder and his family than the impact of an independent mind on his?

Miss Mole was feeling rather gay and conceited after her tea with Mr. Blenkinsop. She had made that serious young man laugh once and smile unwillingly several times, and, in a secluded corner of the tea shop, where the food and the tea were good, she had talked freely for the first time for many years. In her situations, she had listened more often than she had spoken, and though some of them had been lost through the sharp readiness of her tongue, that tongue had merely been restive with pent-up energy and lack of exercise, like a horse, not ill-natured, but riotous when it sees a stretch of grass. To have a listener like Mr. Blenkinsop was to have the grass and no punishment for bolting. There had been no need to pick her words﻿—and when she did not pick them they were harmless enough﻿—with a potential, if not an active, fellow-sinner, and when she left him she was afraid she had talked too much for his convenience, though not for her own comfort. Voluntarily, he had not spoken of his affairs, and when, giving him his chance, she tried to tease him about the gilded gratings of the bank and the dangerous reactions of his evening liberty, he had assured her that he was not an impulsive person. She had expressed amazement and this was one of the moments when he smiled, but he was solemn when he went on to say that due consideration was always given to his undertakings.

===Chinese===

汉娜对生活向来照它本来的样子全盘收下，不完美她也认了；但这并不妨碍她用自己的那套方式去琢磨人的存在。在小事里找乐趣、找刺激——因为没有别的东西可找；眼见身边的人也如法炮制，虽然在她看来，他们用的心思没她那么足——她仍旧免不了纳闷：世人对人类的要求，莫非还不止于此？又或者，只要弄到了糊口的食物、栖身的屋顶，再加上碰巧撞见的乐子，人就算把该办的事都办完了？罗伯特·科德可以把自己看作一个身负明确使命、与邪恶对垒的人；威尔弗里德呢，或许正有点不好意思地盼着减轻世人的皮肉之苦；而芸芸众生则同她一个样，只是没有她那几分独得的禀赋，从一件件小事凑成的一天，熬到下一天，躲过了灾殃便感激不尽，得了太平更是谢天谢地、谢得叫人心酸；可在一个见识过日月星辰列队行进、见识过春之奇迹与秋之华彩的世界上，这两足动物跑来跑去操持的种种营生，总显得不够分量。他们为一些明摆着无关宏旨的东西劳神，比如说褥子；为一点小小的消遣兴奋，比如说同布伦金索普先生吃茶；他们还把光阴——那光阴照说理应献给某种更崇高的目标——浪费在试做新式发型上，好造福一个根本不会留意他们的陌生水手。可这些更高的目标究竟是什么？除了思想者与创造者那一小撮，又有谁在追求它们？而对这些追求者来说，他们自己的心血，会不会有时候也显得同汉娜·莫尔眼里自己的功劳一样无足轻重？她羡慕艺术家，羡慕他们目标明确、自认非做不可的工作；不过羡慕是她从不容许自己久存的情感之一；待到她明白过来——她在自己的天地里也该是个艺术家，说实在的，她为当这个艺术家几乎已经竭尽全力；而她操劳的成果，只要还算好，就决不比一幅荷兰室内画更该受人小觑，谁叫它画的不是诸天神祇呢——那份羡慕便烟消云散了。

拿定主意要当一个凭良心的艺术家，这主意叫人振奋，尤其因为它要求道出的，须是她自己对“非国教牧师的女管家该当何等样人”的见解，而不是罗伯特·科德的见解。想到迎合他的观念便是失忠，倒叫人心安；因为在她同罗伯特·科德的相处里，她有时自觉有罪。她素来夸口说无论谁都能喜欢，偏偏没有试着去喜欢他；然而威尔弗里德对她讲过，稍稍鼓一鼓他，于大家的福利非浅。可艺术家不问大家的福利。最好的作品从来不是那么做出来的；于是汉娜觉得自己尽可以照旧不喜欢罗伯特·科德，并且从这门功课里汲取一种特殊的快乐——她疑心，他正开始从对她的不喜欢里汲取同样的快乐。掩饰心绪，她比他高明；样样都比他高明，她相信；她的快乐会随着她藏得越深、他露得越多而成比例地增长。这份欢喜必须容她受用，她对自己说：再说，为了臆想中的来日之善而违逆天性，天下没有比这更危险的事；倘若来日之善非考虑不可，那么，叫一副独立的心智去撞一撞他的心智，对罗伯特·科德和他一家，还有什么比这更好的呢？

同布伦金索普先生吃过那顿茶，莫尔小姐的心气儿着实轻快，也着实自得。她逗得那个一本正经的年轻人笑了一回，又勉为其难地微笑了好几回；在茶室一个僻静的角落——那儿茶点都不坏——她多年来头一回畅所欲言。在历次帮佣的日子里，她听得总比说得多；虽说有几个差事就断送在她这条三尖两刃的舌头上，可这条舌头不过是憋了一腔子力气没处使、又缺乏操练，才闹起了性子，好比一匹马，性子并不坏，只是一见大片的草地就要撒欢。有一个布伦金索普先生那样的听客，就等于草地也有了，脱缰也不受罚。何况对面坐的是个即便不算现成的同谋犯、至少也算候补同谋犯的人，她的话字字不必拣——她不拣的时候，那些话其实也无伤大雅；等到离开他，她倒怕自己说得太多，碍了他的自在——虽说于她自己的畅快毫不相干。他自己从不主动提他的事；而她为了给他机会，试着拿银行那镀金的栅格、拿他晚间自由时光的危险后遗症打趣他时，他只一本正经地向她担保：他不是个爱冲动的人。她大表惊异，他就在这样的当口笑了一笑；可接下来说到凡他经手的事无不深思熟虑，神情又肃然了。

===Original===

“Then you haven’t my excuse,” she said, “or my fun. It’s a wonderful world, Mr. Blenkinsop, when you may find an adventure round any corner.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，你没有我这套借口，”她说，“也没有我这份乐子。布伦金索普先生，这世界真妙不可言，随便哪个拐角上，都可能撞见一场奇遇。”

===Original===

“Yes, but one doesn’t,” he replied crushingly.

“Of course one can avoid them. It’s a startling thought that if I’d been of the avoiding kind, I should never have known you. I’m sorry this incident always crops up when we meet, but I suppose it’s in both our minds. I’m referring﻿—”

“I know what you’re referring to.”

“And you regret the reference and the incident.”

“Not entirely.”

“Then you’ve changed.”

===Chinese===

“是啊，可人就是撞不上，”他答得叫人哑口无言。

“人当然可以躲开奇遇。这么一想真叫人心惊：倘若我生来是那种躲事的人，我就不会认识你了。真抱歉，咱们每回见面总要翻出这桩事，不过我想，它横在咱俩心里。我是说——”

“我知道你说的是哪桩事。”

“而且你懊悔提起它，也懊悔那桩事。”

“不尽然。”

“那你可是变了。”

===Original===

“Yes, I’ve changed.”

“Ah!” Hannah said significantly. “And I don’t regret it at all, for here I am, having an outing with a single gent, and but for that, I might still be in Mrs. Widdows’ stuffy parlour. But that’s not very likely,” she added honestly. She wished she had called him a technically single gent and seen what he made of that. Possibly she had talked so much that he had decided on reserve. She had told him about her childhood in the country, about her situations and the kind of books she liked and what she would do if she had a lot of money. She felt tired by her own eloquence, but relieved, and she did not think she had given him cause to doubt her discretion. It would be a pity if she had. She liked Mr. Blenkinsop and she wanted to know whether the stories she had made about him would come true, and when she looked back at her past and the men she had met, as her employers and their relatives and friends﻿—for her life had been singularly barren of closer relationships with men and women﻿—she decided that Mr. Blenkinsop was the one to whom she would tell a secret or go for help, and when, in a quick, frightened thought, she remembered Mr. Pilgrim, it did not need a great effort of her imagination to see the solid, bulky form of Mr. Blenkinsop overshadowing the narrow-shouldered, somewhat slinking figure of the other, like a policeman keeping an eye on a suspicious character.

Mr. Pilgrim, however, had come no nearer; Mr. Blenkinsop had given her nothing new to consider except a feeling of safety, and her mind was free to receive the impression of Uncle Jim. As Hannah always had her tongue in her cheek when she created romantic stories about herself, she could make them as extravagant as she chose, and she was hardly disappointed when she saw a man, in clothes which looked too tight for him, who had no likeness to her buccaneer. It was just as well, she thought, when she remembered the difference between the Miss Mole she had fitted to him and the one with which this plain Uncle Jim, who might have been a tax collector, was shaking hands. He had a close-clipped fair moustache in a short face, a pucker of wrinkles round his eyes which gave him an amused rather than a worried expression, and though he was bronzed enough to make Robert Corder look hectic with the colour on his cheekbones, he was not immediately suggestive of hurricanes and tropic sun. He was an ordinary looking man yet, in the slowness of his movements and speech and in his calm, apparently uncritical regard, there was something to make Hannah feel that here was another person who could be relied on. He had a dwarfing effect on Robert Corder, but this was Hannah’s first opportunity of comparing the minister with a man who belonged to a wider world than that of Beresford Road Chapel, and it seemed to her that Uncle Jim in the pulpit would be less incongruous than Robert Corder on the bridge of a ship. Uncle Jim’s authority was that of the man who knew his trade and had his position as a consequence and a matter of course, while, with Robert Corder, the position came first, and the authority derived from it was a constant source of gratification and made a constant demand for recognition. Hannah thought sadly that she was rather like him herself; she had no position to give her a fair start, but she was not content to let her personality make what mark it could, without assistance; a sign, she feared, of a weak character.

===Chinese===

“是啊，我变了。”

“啊！”汉娜意味深长地说。“那我可一点儿也不后悔。瞧我，这不是正同一位单身绅士出门散心么；要不是那档子事，这会儿我说不定还闷在威多斯太太那间不透风的客厅里呢。不过那也未必，”她又老实补了一句。她懊悔方才没管他叫“名义上的单身绅士”，瞧瞧他听了作何反应。也可能，她说得太多了，他这才决意守口如瓶。她跟他讲了她在乡下度过的童年，讲了她历来的差事，讲了她爱看哪一路的书，讲了倘若发一笔大财她要干什么。她被自己的滔滔不绝讲乏了，却又觉得痛快；她不认为自己给了他怀疑她口风的把柄。倘若真给了，那才可惜。她喜欢布伦金索普先生，她想知道她编下的那些关于他的故事会不会应验；而当她回顾自己的过去、回顾她遇到过的男人们——那些不过是雇主，连同他们的亲戚朋友，因为她这一生，同男人女人亲近的缘分偏偏稀薄得很——她断定，布伦金索普先生正是那个可以托付一件秘密、或者求助一把的人；等到一个又快又惊的念头提起了皮尔格林先生，她无须怎么费想象力的力气，就看见布伦金索普先生那墩实魁伟的身形压过了另一个那副窄肩、有几分鬼祟的身影，恰似一名警察，正盯防着个形迹可疑的人物。

不过，皮尔格林先生并没有再逼近一步；布伦金索普先生除了一层安稳感，也没给她添什么新的可想；她的心思便空出来，去接吉姆舅舅给她的印象。汉娜替自己编罗曼司的时候，舌头向来是贴着腮帮子的，心里自有分寸，所以尽可由着性子把故事编到天花乱坠；待见到一个衣服绷在身上像小了一号的男人，同她幻想里的那位海盗船长毫无相似之处，她几乎谈不上失望。她倒觉得这样更好，只要记一记她配给他的那位莫尔小姐，同此刻正与他握手的这位平平无奇的吉姆舅舅——那模样，说是个收税的也有人信——中间隔着多远，就明白了。他一张短脸，蓄一部剪得贴根的浅色髭须；眼角收着一圈细纹，叫他看上去与其说是忧心，不如说含着点被逗乐的神气；他那张脸晒得棕黑，相形之下，罗伯特·科德颧骨上的红晕简直要显得像痨病的潮红；可乍一见面，他并不叫人想起飓风和热带的骄阳。他是个相貌平常的男人，然而在他举止言谈的从容迟缓里，在他那平静的、显然无所评判的目光里，有种东西让汉娜觉得：这又是一个靠得住的人。他叫罗伯特·科德相形见绌；而这也是汉娜头一回有机会，拿这位牧师同一个属于比贝雷斯福德路礼拜堂更宽阔的天地的人两相对照；在她看来，吉姆舅舅站上讲道坛，总归比罗伯特·科德站上舰桥要不那么不伦不类。吉姆舅舅的权威，是精通本行的人的权威，职位是手艺挣来的结果，得来也心安理得；罗伯特·科德呢，位子在前头，从位子派生出来的权威，既是他说不尽的快慰，又时时刻刻讨要着旁人的承认。汉娜黯然想道，在这一点上她自己倒跟他有几分相像：她没有职位给她一个体面的起点，可她又不甘心让自己的人格不借外力、能磕出多少印子算多少；她怕，这是性格软弱的征兆。

===Original===

She made another of her good resolutions and remained silent and observant. She saw Ruth looking with shining eyes at the imperturbable face of her uncle and glancing from him to Hannah, as though she wished to connect them in her happiness and to know what each thought of the other. Ethel, wearing an extra necklace in honour of the occasion, and a bright blue frock, rattled her beads and jerked in her seat at the supper table, but her glances had not the confidence of Ruth’s. She could never forget the presence of her father, and her anxiety was that conversation should go amicably between him and Uncle Jim. Robert Corder did his best as host. He invited his brother-in-law to talk of his experiences and Uncle Jim was as unresponsive as Howard had been on the night of his return. He said something about the weather and Robert Corder lifted his eyebrows patiently. He was not really eager to hear another person talk, but it was exasperating to see such a waste of opportunity. A single voyage would have supplied the minister with anecdotes for countless supper tables and sewing-meetings, with texts and illustrations for innumerable sermons, and this man, who had followed the sea since he was a boy and knew all the ports of the world, could produce nothing more illuminating than a casual reference to the weather. Opportunities did not come to those who could use them. Howard and Jim were alike in their dumb obtuseness, and what sort of minister Howard would make, his father feared to prophesy. He sank back in his chair and resigned his duties. He had done all he could.

Then Uncle Jim volunteered a remark.

===Chinese===

她又许下一回向善的决心，安安静静，只看不说。她看见露丝眼睛亮晶晶地望着舅舅那张波澜不兴的脸，目光又从舅舅身上溜到汉娜身上，仿佛要在自己的欢喜里把他两个拴在一处，还想知道彼此心里怎么掂量对方。埃塞尔为给这桩盛事添彩，额外多套了一条项链，穿了件鲜蓝的连衣裙，坐在晚饭桌旁把珠子捻得咔咔响，身子在椅子里一蹿一蹿；只是她的眼风里没有露丝那份笃定。父亲的在场，她一刻也不敢忘，她唯一操心的，是他同吉姆舅舅之间的谈话能客客气气地进行下去。罗伯特·科德把主人的本分做到了十足。他请内兄讲讲历险见闻，吉姆舅舅却同霍华德回家那晚一样，一问一个不吭。他说了句把天气，罗伯特·科德耐着性子扬了扬眉毛。其实他并不真心巴望听别人谈天，可眼看这样的天赐良机白白糟蹋，实在令人火起。单单一趟远航，就够给这位牧师添上无数张晚饭桌、无数场缝纫会上的奇谈，添上数不清的讲道经文与例证；而这个打小就跟海讨生活、认得天底下游埠的人，掏出来的顶有启发的话，不过是对天气的信口一句。机会偏不投奔会用它的人。霍华德和吉姆在那份木头木脑的闷钝上如出一辙；霍华德将来能当个怎样的牧师，他父亲不敢预言。他往椅背上一靠，交卸了职守。他能做的，都已做了。

这时，吉姆舅舅自愿贡献了一句评论。

===Original===

“What’s the matter with the gas?” he said, looking at the pendant light. “It oughtn’t to make a noise like that. I’ll have a look at it tomorrow.”

“I’ve been looking at it for more than two months and it doesn’t take the slightest notice,” Hannah said mournfully.

Ruth laughed and looked at her uncle. She had been hoping Miss Mole would say something to show she was not just an ordinary housekeeper who was afraid to speak.

Robert Corder frowned. “Then, Miss Mole,” he said sharply, “you should have called in the plumber.”

===Chinese===

“这煤气灯是怎么了？”他望着那盏吊灯说。“不该闹出这样的动静。我明天来瞧瞧。”

“我瞧它也有两个多月了，它压根儿不睬我，”汉娜愁眉苦脸地说。

露丝笑了，望了望舅舅。她一直在盼着莫尔小姐说出句什么来，好表明她不是那种只知害怕、不敢开口的寻常女管家。

罗伯特·科德把眉头一皱。“那么，莫尔小姐，”他声色俱厉地说，“你早该去请管子工了。”

===Original===

“But would he come, if I did call to him? And would he go, if once he came? Plumbers,” she said, “are like everything else. You want them badly and, when you get them, you’re sorry.”

“I think,” Robert Corder said in the bland manner of his annoyance, “you must make a point of wanting the things you should not have.”

“That was implied,” Hannah said brightly. She had broken her good resolution and Uncle Jim had waked to the fact of her existence.

“We’ll try combined tactics tomorrow,” he said, and then he created a timely diversion. “I’ve got some Chinese silks upstairs. I’ll bring them down after supper.”

===Chinese===

“可我真去请，他肯来吗？他既来了，又肯走吗？管子工嘛，”她说，“同天下万物一个道理。你要的时候想得要命，真到了手，悔之莫及。”

“我看，”罗伯特·科德拿他那种把恼火调得温吞吞的腔调说，“你准是存心专要那些你不该要的东西。”

“这层意思原在内，”汉娜眉开眼笑地说。她那向善的决心是破了，吉姆舅舅却也醒过神来，知道世上有了她这个人。

“明天咱们来个联合作战，”他说，跟着便及时岔开了话头。“我楼上带了几匹中国绸缎，吃过晚饭拿下来。”

===Original===

Hannah stayed in the dining-room and Robert Corder lingered by the fire.

“You and I, Miss Mole,” he said, “are not interested in the Chinese silks.”

“Aren’t we?” Hannah replied. “I expect they’re beautiful.”

“Oh, no doubt, no doubt, but hardly matters of concern to you and me.”

Was he trying to remind her of her age and position and hinting that she should not follow the others to the drawing-room?

===Chinese===

汉娜留在饭厅里，罗伯特·科德在炉火边流连不去。

“你我嘛，莫尔小姐，”他说，“对那些中国绸缎是不会有什么兴趣的。”

“咱们不会么？”汉娜应道。“我倒觉得它们准美得很。”

“哦，自然是美，自然是美，只是不大是你我操心的事。”

他是想提醒她掂掂自己的年纪和身份，暗示她不该跟着旁人到客厅里去么？

===Original===

“Not of concern, certainly. That’s why I’m staying here,” she said reassuringly. “I wonder if Mr. Erley can stop these jets from hissing as well as the other one from bubbling. I thought I was getting used to the noise, but, as Christmas draws nearer, it sounds like all the geese and turkeys making a fuss at the thought of being killed.”

“What a very unpleasant idea!” he said, frowning quickly.

“Yes, isn’t it?” Then she looked up at him and laughed. “I’ve spoilt your Christmas pudding and now I’ve spoilt your goose.”

Mr. Corder suddenly condescended to jest. “Or, as you might say, you have cooked it.”

Hannah laughed still more merrily. She was not going to be beaten by the ladies at the sewing-meeting and Robert Corder might have found it necessary to withdraw in good-humoured dignity, before Miss Mole took advantage of his affability, if Ruth had not appeared, to say that Uncle Jim had given her a lovely silk with little flowers all over it, and did Miss Mole think it could possibly be made into a frock in time for the Spenser-Smith’s party?

===Chinese===

“自然不相干。所以我才待在这儿，”她一副叫人放心的口吻说。“不知道厄利先生（Mr. Erley）能不能叫这几处火头别再咝咝响，就像叫另一处别再咕嘟咕嘟冒泡那样。我本来自以为快听惯了，可圣诞节一近，这声音就越听越像满圈的鹅啊火鸡啊，一想到要挨宰，就闹成一团。”

“好煞风景的一个念头！”他赶忙皱起眉头说。

“可不是嘛！”说着她抬头望他，笑出了声。“我先搅了你的圣诞布丁，这会儿又搅了你的鹅。”

科德先生忽然屈尊幽默了一回。“再不然，照你的说法——你已经把它做熟了。”

汉娜笑得愈发欢畅。缝纫会那班太太们的妙语，她是决计不肯输与的；若不是露丝这时跑了进来——说吉姆舅舅给了她一匹顶可爱的绸子，满匹都是小碎花，问莫尔小姐能不能赶得上斯宾塞-史密斯家的宴会，把它做成一件连衣裙——那么，趁莫尔小姐还要借重他的和颜悦色大做文章之前，罗伯特·科德多半会觉得有必要带着一团和气的体面，抽身退席了。

===Original===

“We’ll try,” Hannah said slowly. She was thinking of the little velveteen dress which was to have been a surprise and which would seem so humble in comparison with the flowery silk.

“I knew you’d say that!” In her excitement, Ruth turned to her father and exclaimed, “she never says she can’t do anything when she knows you want it! And do come and stop Ethel from choosing a bright pink. There are heaps of others and the drawing-room’s like a shop.”

“Just a minute, Miss Mole. I suppose you have replied to Mrs. Spenser-Smith’s invitation?”

===Chinese===

“试试看吧，”汉娜慢慢地说。她心里想的是那件小小的平绒裙衫，那本该是个出其不意的欢喜，可同这匹花绸一比，怕要显得寒伧了。

“我就知道你会这么说！”露丝一兴奋，转身朝父亲嚷道，“只要她知道你想要，她就从来不说做不了！您快去呀，埃塞尔要挑大粉红的了，快去拦住她。别样的多着呢，客厅都成绸缎铺子啦。”

“且慢，莫尔小姐。斯宾塞-史密斯太太那张请柬，想必你已回复过了？”

===Original===

“No,” Hannah said, “your daughter has done that.”

“But I think it would be courteous to send a separate little note. It’s very kind of her to ask you.”

“Yes, but I wasn’t sure that I ought to go,” Hannah said meekly.

“But certainly, certainly! You could hardly refuse and you will find it quite a homely, friendly affair. Just write to Mrs. Spenser-Smith and express your appreciation.”

===Chinese===

“没有，”汉娜说，“您女儿代劳了。”

“可我觉得，另奉一张小笺才算礼数周到。她肯下请，是瞧得起你。”

“是，可我拿不准自己该不该去，”汉娜低眉顺眼地说。

“当然该去，当然！你横竖推辞不得，去了便知，那不过是场随和家常的小聚。只管给斯宾塞-史密斯太太去信，道一声谢意。”

===Original===

“In the first, or the third, person?” Hannah enquired.

“The first, Miss Mole, would be more suitable. Write it in the form of a note.”

“Very well,” Hannah said, and she began to plan her humble little letter, but Ruth had her by the arm to lead her into the drawing-room, and was asking if she had a party frock.

“Black silk and jet trimmings. It’s the only wear for a housekeeper.”

===Chinese===

“用第一人称，还是第三人称？”汉娜虚心请教。

“第一人称更合式，莫尔小姐。写成一张短笺就是。”

“遵命，”汉娜说，肚里已开始起她那封谦卑小信的草稿；无奈露丝已经挽住她的胳膊，要领她进客厅去，一路只问她有没有赴宴的裙衫。

“黑绸裙，镶煤玉边。女管家就兴这一种行头。”

===Original===

“And Uncle Jim’s got all those lovely things!” Ruth sighed.

“If you dare to say anything to him about me, I’ll never speak to you again!” Hannah whispered vehemently, but Uncle Jim did not need a hint. He was looking at the mass of stuff he had bought and wondering how he was to get rid of it and Hannah’s modest protests were useless when he thrust a roll of silk into her arms.

Ruth’s happiness was now complete. “And won’t Mrs. Spenser-Smith be secretly vexed when she sees us all looking so grand!” she cried.

===Chinese===

“可吉姆舅舅有那么多好看的东西呢！”露丝叹道。

“你敢在他面前提我一个字，我就再也不跟你说话！”汉娜狠狠地耳语道。好在吉姆舅舅原不消人递话。他正望着自己买下的那一大堆衣料发愁，琢磨着怎么打发；等他把一卷绸子硬塞进汉娜怀里，她那些温良的推辞便全然无用武之地了。

露丝的欢喜至此功德圆满。“等斯宾塞-史密斯太太看见我们个个打扮得这么神气，还不知她暗地里怎么憋气呢！”她大声说。

## XXIV / 第二十四章

===Original===

Hannah had the homeless and the childless person’s dislike for Christmas. There were a few people from whom, in the course of her career, she had not been completely severed, and to these she wrote, but the paucity of her acquaintances and her lack of real intimacy with them were very present to her at this time of the year and almost persuaded her of some failure in herself, but it was not easy, and Robert Corder had lately emphasised the fact, to make friends outside the house in which she was a dependent, and to most of the people she had served, often wholeheartedly, sometimes with misguided zeal, becoming absorbed in their affairs as though they were her own, she remained as mere Miss Mole, whose importance vanished with her useful presence. She was accustomed to this state of things but it still amazed her. A human being, to her, was a continuous wonder, a group of human beings made a drama of which she was half creator, half spectator, and she was baffled to know how people amused themselves without this entertainment which never palled and never ended. Hannah was not one of those who considered it a waste of time to lose a train and have to wait on a station platform, who shut their eyes, or read a paper, in railway carriages: she was thrilled by the sight of strangers and by the emanation of their personalities, and it was hard to understand that they did not get the same excitement from her neighbourhood.

===Chinese===

汉娜有一种无家可归、无儿无女之人对圣诞节的嫌恶。她这一路生涯里，尚有寥寥数人不曾与她完全断绝往来，逢着年节她也给他们写信；可熟人如此之少，彼此又缺乏真正的亲密，每逢这个时节，这一点便格外真切地涌上心头，几乎要说服她：自己准是在哪一点上失败了。然而，在一个自己身为依附者的家门之外结交朋友，本就不是易事——罗伯特·科德近来还特意强调了这一层——而对她伺候过的多数人来说，她始终不过是莫尔小姐罢了；那些人她伺候时常是一副热心肠，有时是热昏了头的卖力，一头扎进人家的事务里，仿佛那便是自己的事务；可她那点重要性，随着她有用的身影一齐散得无影无踪。这样的境遇她受惯了，却仍旧令她惊异。在她眼里，一个人是一件绵延不尽的奇观，一群人便是一台戏，她半是造戏的人，半是看戏的人；少了这种永不倒胃口、永不散场的娱乐，人们究竟拿什么自遣，她实在想不明白。误了火车、只得在站台上干等，有人便当作虚掷光阴；坐进车厢便阖眼打盹，或者展开报纸：汉娜不属于这路人。陌生人的身影、陌生人人格里散逸出来的气息，都叫她心头一激；而难于索解的是，他们挨着她近旁，竟生不出同样的兴奋。

===Original===

When she considered the Corder family dispassionately, she could see that the material for her drama was not promising. The egoism of the Reverend Robert offered the most scope, for the egoism of a person whose abilities are mediocre must have a humorous element, but in Ethel, in Ruth, in the quiet Howard, in Captain Jim Erley, in his shirtsleeves, as he busied himself about the gas, how could she find so much interest? Was her own egoism enlarging the significance of these who made her little world or was she really seeing the whole world in miniature, which was all the ordinary human eye could manage? The shaking of an empire was not more agitating than the imminence of Howard’s disclosure about the ministry; the intrigues of diplomacy were not more complicated and needed no more skill than Mr. Pilgrim’s intrusion into this life shared with the Corders, and the news of a great victory could hardly gratify its engineers more deeply than Uncle Jim gratified Miss Mole when he stepped off the dining-room table and said, “You’ve done Ruth a lot of good. If I’d known you were here I might have stuck to the sea a bit longer.”

===Chinese===

她若心平气和地把科德一家打量一遍，便看得出，她这台戏的料子并不见佳。罗伯特牧师的自我主义可施展的余地最大——一个才具平庸之人的自我主义，里头必定带着几分滑稽；可是埃塞尔呢，露丝呢，文静的霍华德呢，还有那位穿着衬衫袖子、正忙着摆弄煤气灯的吉姆·厄利上尉（Captain Jim Erley）呢，她上哪儿从他们身上搜出这许多趣味去？是她自己的自我主义，把这班凑成她小小天地的人物的分量吹胀了呢，还是她看见的当真是整个世界的缩影——反正寻常人的眼睛，本来也只能管到这一步？帝国撼动，未必比霍华德眼看要吐露投身圣职一事更搅得人心不安；外交上的纵横捭阖，其错综未必胜过皮尔格林先生闯进这份与科德家同过的日子，所需的心计也未必更多；至于一场大捷的战报，哪怕对它的缔造者们，也未必慰藉得比吉姆舅舅慰藉莫尔小姐更深——他刚从餐室桌子上跳下来，说道：“你把露丝调理得见好了。早知道你在这儿，我说不定还在海上多留一阵子。”

===Original===

“Think of that!” Hannah said, concealing her pleasure under this light retort. “But you might have been drowned on your next voyage and Ruth wouldn’t have liked that. The reason she looks so happy is because you’re here.”

“No, she’s different. Not so jumpy. I’m grateful to you.”

“Well, I’m not a permanency,” Hannah said rather tartly, “and she must learn to rely on herself.”

“You’re not going to leave them, are you?”

“I don’t know.”

===Chinese===

“这倒想不到！”汉娜说，拿这句轻俏的抢白掩住满心欢喜。“不过，下一趟出海你没准儿就淹死了，露丝可不乐意那个。她如今看着这么快活，就因为你人在这儿。”

“不，她变了。不那么一惊一乍了。我感激你。”

“唔，我又不是常驻久留的，”汉娜说，口气相当发酸，“她总得学会靠自己。”

“你不打算扔下他们走吧？”

“不知道。”

===Original===

“I shouldn’t blame you,” Uncle Jim muttered indiscreetly. He put on his coat and Hannah felt that he would have said more if he had remained without it. She had lost her buccaneer, but the brother of Mrs. Corder, the brother-in-law of the Reverend Robert was still there and her desire to know what the feelings of these three had been in connection with each other, was almost a pain.

She took a step at a venture. “Is that a good photograph of Mrs. Corder, in the study?”

“It depends who’s looking at it. My brother-in-law would say no, I should say no, and so would Ruth, but we’d all mean something different. She was something different with all of us, I suppose.”

“And what would Mr. Samson say?”

“Who’s he? I’ve never heard of him.” He made a grimace. “One of the deacons?”

===Chinese===

“换了我也不会怪你，”吉姆舅舅口无遮拦地咕哝道。他穿上外衣；汉娜暗自觉得，他若不曾穿上外衣，本还会再说下去的。她那位海盗是没有了，可科德太太的兄弟、罗伯特牧师的内兄弟还坐在那里；这三个人彼此之间有过怎样的情肠，她急于探明，急得几乎成了痛楚。

她冒昧试探一步。“书房里那张科德太太的照片，拍得好么？”

“那得看是谁在看。我妹夫会说不好，我会说不好，露丝也会说不好，可我们仨的意思各不相同。我猜，她跟我们每一个人相处时，便是各不相同的一位。”

“那桑普森先生会怎么说？”

“他是谁？我从没听说过这个人。”他做了个鬼脸。“是哪一位执事么？”

===Original===

Hannah laughed and did not answer. She had learnt another thing she wished to know, but she had not finished yet. “You can’t have had time to see much of your nephew.”

“Well, we stayed up rather late last night,” he said, and he and Hannah eyed each other calculatingly; he measured her trustworthiness and she his willingness to trust.

“I’m not supposed to know about him,” she said at last. “I’m surprised Ethel hasn’t told me, but she’s rather preoccupied, perhaps.” She gave him a sidelong look out of eyes in which green was the predominating colour, but these words struck no response from Uncle Jim; he had not received Ethel’s confidences as well as Howard’s, and Hannah left this side issue. “Family disturbances are very bad for Ruth,” she said.

===Chinese===

汉娜一笑，没有接话。她又探出一桩想知道的事，可是还没探完。“你总来不及把你外甥端详个仔细吧。”

“这个嘛，昨天夜里我们坐到挺晚，”他说；他和汉娜彼此打量，各自心里拨算盘：他在掂量她可信到什么地步，她在掂量他肯信到什么地步。

“照规矩，他的事我不该知道，”她终于说道。“埃塞尔竟没告诉我，我倒奇怪；不过，她也许心事重重，顾不上吧。”她拿眼角扫他一扫，那双眼睛里绿色是主色；可这番话在吉姆舅舅那里没有敲出一星回声：埃塞尔的体己话，他得到的就不如霍华德的多。汉娜便丢开这根岔出去的线头。“家里起风波，对露丝极不好，”她说。

===Original===

“I’d like to adopt her,” Uncle Jim said suddenly, and, just as suddenly, Hannah felt a rush of enmity towards him.

“But that,” she said coldly, “could not be arranged in a few days, within which time we’re going to have trouble.”

“And I don’t see how it’s to be avoided.”

===Chinese===

“我倒想把露丝领过来养，”吉姆舅舅突然说；也就在同一瞬间，汉娜心里对他涌起一阵敌意。

“可是这件事，”她冷冷地说，“不是几天里办得成的；而这几天里头，我们就要有麻烦了。”

“我看这麻烦也躲不掉。”

===Original===

“The right words in the right quarter﻿—” Hannah said thoughtfully. She did not believe that Robert Corder had any great desire to see his son a minister with a more valuable degree than his own and a social distinction he had missed. He would, of course, be able to make Howard feel his inferiority, but it would be less easy to suggest it, allied with fatherly love, to other people, and nothing else would satisfy him. A little flattery, a little comparative disparagement of Howard, would do much to mollify him, but who was going to offer it? There was no hope of that from Uncle Jim and it would come somewhat startlingly from Miss Mole. Even Robert Corder might suspect some motive in her admiration, and Hannah sighed audibly at the difficulty, the impossibility, of acting wisely for the future.

===Chinese===

“对症的话，得说到对症的地方去——”汉娜沉吟着说。她并不相信，罗伯特·科德当真巴望儿子去当牧师——拿一个比自己更值钱的学位，享一种自己错失了一辈子的社会名望。到了那一步，他自然尽可以让霍华德自觉低劣；可是要把这层意思裹上父爱，说给旁人听，就不那么容易了，而除此以外，什么都不能叫他满足。几句奉承，几句拿霍华德作比较的贬语，大可以把安抚的功夫做到家；可是这话由谁去说呢？指望吉姆舅舅，是没有指望的；出自莫尔小姐之口，又要有几分惊人。就连罗伯特·科德，恐怕也要疑心她的颂词里别有动机。要高明地为将来布局，竟是这般难，这般不可能，汉娜不由得出声叹了一口气。

===Original===

“The right quarter,” said Uncle Jim, “seems to be this Mrs. Somebody who’s financing the boy. It’s very awkward for him. He’s had two years of her patronage and that’s what’s going to worry his father most. Throwing her money in her face! And I gather she’s a lady of some importance.”

A slow smile curved Hannah’s lips. She had a sense of power which affected her physically, with a tickling feeling of pleasure. She was in haste to use that power, her mission to the Corder family enlarged its sphere, she saw herself as an appointed agent, and, for the moment, she had finished with Uncle Jim.

“You’ll see Mrs. Spenser-Smith at the party,” she said.

“Must I go to the party?” he asked in dismay. “I haven’t got any evening clothes.”

“They won’t be missed,” Hannah assured him.

===Chinese===

“对症的地方嘛，”吉姆舅舅说，“看来是那位出钱供这孩子的某某太太。这一层叫他很难堪。他吃她的恩惠吃了两年，这最要叫他父亲心里不安生。把她的钱劈脸掷过来！我又听说，那位太太还是个顶有些身份的人物。”

一缕笑意慢慢地弯上汉娜的唇角。她觉出一种力量，这力量一直作用到身体上，带着一阵酥痒的快意。她急于行使这份力量；她对科德家的使命扩大了辖区，她把自己看作一位受命在身的代理人；至于吉姆舅舅，眼前她已经打发完毕。

“宴会上你就能见着斯宾塞-史密斯太太了，”她说。

“我非去赴会不可么？”他沮丧地问。“我连一身晚礼服也没有。”

“缺了也看不出。”汉娜给他吃定心丸。

===Original===

She dusted the table on which she meant to cut out Ruth’s silk dress, and, looking busy, she spread out the material and fetched her pins and scissors, and Uncle Jim watched these preparations critically, for a few minutes, before he rolled away. Then Hannah ran upstairs to her room, enjoying her unnecessary stealth. She sat down by the wide-opened window with a pad of note paper on her knee, and the energy she had meant to concentrate on a letter to Lilla was dissipated through the eyes which could see the coloured roofs of Radstowe, the plumes of smoke, the spires, the factory chimneys, the distant fields sweeping up to the high ground which blocked the view of her own country. For her, much as she disliked the day itself, the approach of Christmas was the approach of spring in the West country. There might be snow and frosts later, but always, at this season, there was a damp mildness in the air, a message telling her that the earth was being stirred by tiny pushing feet, pressing downwards so that spikes, eager to be green, might reach upwards, and she fancied she could smell primroses, that scent delicate as the flower’s colour, soft as its pale cup. She knew a place where she thought primroses might be blooming even now, standing up among their strong crinkled leaves like some marvellously fine work on the rough palm of its maker, but from that thought and the view of Radstowe she turned aside. The primroses grew too near her cottage and innumerable reminders of other lives lessened the urgency of the task she had set herself.

===Chinese===

她掸净那张桌子——打算就在桌上裁露丝那件绸衣裳——装出一副忙碌模样，把料子铺展开，取来大头针和剪刀；吉姆舅舅挑剔地端详了几分钟这些铺排，方才轱辘而去。随后汉娜奔上楼，回自己房里去，暗自受用这场其实大可不必的蹑手蹑脚。她在敞到最开的大窗前坐下，膝头摊着一叠便笺；那股原要聚拢在给莉拉的信上的心力，却从一双眼睛里散逸出去——这双眼望得见拉德斯托五彩的屋顶、一绺绺烟羽、一座座尖塔、工厂的烟囱，望得见远处的田亩一路漫涌上去，直到那片高地；高地把视线截住，她自己的乡土便看不见了。对她来说，圣诞节这一天本身她虽嫌弃，圣诞节的临近，却是西部乡间春天的临近。往后或许还有雪，还有霜；可每逢这个时节，空气里总有一股潮润的温和，捎来一句口信：大地正被细小而向前拱动的脚尖搅动，那些脚尖向下蹬踏，好教一丛丛急欲转绿的芽尖得以向上伸展；她恍惚闻见了樱草花的香气——那香气娇嫩，如同花瓣的颜色；轻软，如同那浅色的花盏。她知道一处地方，猜想那里的樱草花此刻说不定已经开了，正从壮实的皱叶中间立起身来，宛若巧手工匠粗糙的掌心里一件精妙入神的细活；可这个念头，连同拉德斯托的景色，她一并撇开了。樱草花离她的小屋太近，而旁人生活投来的无数桩提醒，把她给自己派下的那桩差事的紧迫冲淡了。

===Original===

This was to write her little letter of thanks to Mrs. Spenser-Smith, as Robert Corder had directed, in the first person, and to do it with a gratitude which would make Lilla flush in annoyance and conceal the letter from Ernest, and she meant to add a threat that if Lilla chose to make trouble about Howard, her loving cousin would know how to make it for her, but this proved more difficult than she had thought and she tore up her attempt. Lilla might appear in Beresford Road, confident of her ability to right anything and demanding to know what it was. It would be better to go and see her, to suggest a probable cause of offence, to drop several hints from which Lilla could pick up the one she thought least objectionable, to leave her in no doubt that Hannah had a counterstroke for any indignant thrust at the family whose welfare she had so much at heart, and to set Lilla wondering at this loyalty. Lilla would curb any amount of indignation rather than have her relationship to poor Miss Mole made known. She would have been wiser to have announced it, regretfully, as a distant connection, from the first; now she must face the accusation of snobbishness and suffer from the truth of it, the accusation of deceit and explain it away without convincing anyone.

===Chinese===

差事便是：照罗伯特·科德的指示，用第一人称，给斯宾塞-史密斯太太写一封小小的道谢信，而且要写得感激不尽，感激得叫莉拉恼得脸上飞红、把信瞒着欧内斯特；她还打算添上一句威吓：莉拉倘若存心在霍华德的事上兴风作浪，她这位亲爱的表妹自会知道怎样替她也造出一浪来。可是这件事比她料想的难，写出来的稿子叫她撕了。莉拉兴许会亲自寻到贝雷斯福德路来，自信什么事都摆布得平，还要追问到底出了什么事。倒不如登门去见她：递给她一条说得过去的开罪缘由；撒下几处话头，任莉拉从里头拣一条自认为最不扎耳的；让她确凿无疑地明白，谁要朝她这般牵肠挂肚的一家人愤然一刺，汉娜必有还手的一击；再教莉拉对着这份忠心纳罕不止。莉拉宁可把天大的义愤都咽下去，也不肯让人知道她同可怜的莫尔小姐沾亲带故。她若打一开始就懊恼地声明白己不过是一门远亲，倒还聪明些；如今她可得顶着势利眼的罪名，还受着这罪名属实之苦；顶着行骗的罪名，还得百般开脱，开脱了也没人肯信。

===Original===

Hannah had no time to spare, but she made enough of it for the expedition across the downs and an interview with Lilla which sent her chuckling home again, burdened though she was with Christmas presents for the Corders.

“As you are here, you may as well take them and save the postage,” Lilla said.

“And how am I to explain where I got them?”

“That’s your affair, Hannah. I’ve never known you at a loss for a lie,” Lilla said severely.

Hannah pecked her cheek and wished her a happy Christmas. “And you’ll see us all at your party. I didn’t mean to come and embarrass you, dear, but Mr. Corder insisted that I should. He implied that he wouldn’t enjoy it without me.”

===Chinese===

汉娜本腾不出工夫，却硬生生腾出了够用的工夫：远征一趟，翻过丘陵草原，同莉拉谈了一场，回家时一路忍着笑，身上虽然还驮着给科德一家的圣诞礼物。

“你既来了，不如就捎了去，还省一笔邮资。”莉拉说。

“那我怎么解释这些东西是哪儿来的呢？”

“那是你的事了，汉娜。我就没见你编瞎话打过磕绊。”莉拉疾言厉色地说。

汉娜在她表姐的脸颊上啄了一下，祝她圣诞快乐。“你的宴会上，我们就都来啦。我原没打算来叫你难堪的，亲爱的，是科德先生执意要我来。他的意思是，少了我，他就没什么乐趣了。”

===Original===

“You won’t get me to believe that,” Lilla said, but she was puzzled. A woman who attended to a man’s comfort could be a potent influence and men were very simple. Perhaps she had reckoned too surely on the safety of Hannah’s plain exterior. She was capable and Lilla could not deny her a peculiar kind of charm. Her arrival involved worry, but her departure took something exhilarating from the house and if she, who had disturbing doubts about the niceness of Hannah’s conscience, could feel like this, how much more likely was Robert Corder to become dependent on her gay resourcefulness. And Lilla remembered that there were years of Hannah’s life of which she did not know the history, and it suddenly occurred to her that Hannah’s anxiety for the happiness of the Corders could more naturally be interpreted as anxiety for herself, and this thought made Hannah’s playful threats more dangerous, for, bad as it was to have a cousin who was a housekeeper, it was worse to have one with a past.

===Chinese===

“这话我可不信。”莉拉说，心里却犯了嘀咕。一个会照拂男人起居舒适的女人，可以成为一种厉害的势力，而男人实在简单得很。也许她先前太笃定：汉娜那副平淡的外表，总归出不了事。这女人是能干的，而莉拉也不能否认她有一种特别的魅力。她来时捎来麻烦，她走时却从这座宅子里带走了一点叫人提神的东西；连她这个对汉娜的良心端正与否怀着恼人疑虑的人尚且如此，罗伯特·科德岂不更有可能渐渐倚赖她那活泼泼的随机应变。莉拉又记起，汉娜生平有几年岁月，她是不知道底细的；她霍然想到，汉娜对科德一家幸福的关切，未尝不能更自然地解作对她自身的关切；这一想，汉娜那些打趣式的威吓便更见凶险了，因为，有一个做女管家的表亲已经够糟，再有一个带着一段过去的表亲，就更糟了。

===Original===

Willingly carrying the parcels, to save Lilla a few pence, Hannah hurried back. There was bound to be a storm when Howard broke his news, but she believed she had reduced its violence and curtailed it. She had done a lot for Ruth, according to Uncle Jim, she had done something for Howard, and, if she could secure a reasonable existence for Ethel, her life, she told herself dramatically, would not have been lived in vain, but the last was the hardest of her undertakings and when she cast about for that middle-aged minister who might solve Ethel’s problem, she could see no one but Mr. Pilgrim, bearing olive and myrtle for Ethel and a sword for Hannah Mole.

She threw a longing glance at Mr. Samson’s windows as she passed. There was the old gentleman who might have been her salvation, but he had already told her that he lived on an annuity and had no dependents to consider except the cats and birds, and for their future he had made arrangements. “Then what is the good of my coming to see you?” Hannah had asked, and sent him off into one of his throaty chuckles.

===Chinese===

汉娜心甘情愿地提着大包小包——好替莉拉省下几个便士——匆匆往回赶。霍华德吐露消息的那一天，一场风暴是免不了的；但她相信，自己已经把风暴的烈度削低，把它的势头掐短。照吉姆舅舅的说法，她为露丝做了许多，为霍华德也做了些；倘若她还能为埃塞尔挣得一份过得像样的日子，那么她这一生，她煞有介事地对自己说，就不算白白活过。只是这是她几桩事业里最棘手的一桩；她四下搜寻那位可能替埃塞尔解开难题的中年牧师，看来看去，只看得见一位皮尔格林先生：他给埃塞尔捧来橄榄与桃金娘，给汉娜·莫尔捧来一柄剑。

路过的时候，她恋恋地瞟了一眼桑普森先生的窗子。那位老先生原是她的救星，可惜他早已告诉过她：他靠一笔年金过活，没有要照应的亲属，只除了那些猫儿鸟儿；而它们的身后，他也已经有了安排。“那我还来看你做什么呢？”汉娜当时这么问过他，一句话送他喉咙里咯咯地笑个不停。

===Original===

Undeterred by the thought of the annuity, she went to see him that evening when the family was gathered in the drawing-room. She had made him a new night cover for the canary’s cage as a Christmas offering, and she hoped to give it to him and return before her absence was discovered, but Mr. Samson detained her. He had found among the jumble of objects he had collected in his travels, a fine piece of lace for Miss Fitt, and when she had thanked him and heard how he acquired it and how much less than its value he had paid for it, when he had draped it round her shoulders and said that that, at least, was not a misfit, it was nearly ten o’clock and time for Mr. Corder’s tea, and Mr. Corder was hovering in the hall when she opened the front door.

===Chinese===

年金归年金，她并不因此却步：那天晚上，趁一家人聚在客厅里，她到隔壁看他去了。她给那金丝雀的笼子新做了一幅夜罩，权充圣诞的孝敬，打算送到手就回来，趁人还没发觉她不在；可是桑普森先生把她留住了。他在自己游历四方搜罗来的一堆杂什里，翻出一块精美的花边，是给菲特小姐的；等她道过谢，听完这花边从哪儿得来、他出的价钱比它的价值便宜了多少；等他把它披上她的肩头，还说这一件，至少不是什么“不合式”的东西——时候已近十点，到了科德先生的茶点时分；她拉开前门时，科德先生正在门厅里徘徊。

===Original===

He looked at her bare head, at the parcel in her hand, and for the first time, he was openly angry with her, too angry to notice the tautening of her body and the lift of her head. He considered it most improper for Miss Mole to leave the house without warning; nobody had known where she was; they were all extremely worried, and had searched everywhere for her.

“Not everywhere,” Hannah said, smiling a little. The trump card was up her sleeve and she was going to bring it out. “If you had called next door you would have found me with Mr. Samson.”

“Mr. Samson! I very much object, Miss Mole, to any member of my household visiting that disreputable old man.”

===Chinese===

他看看她那颗没戴帽子的头，看看她手里的包裹，头一回对她公然动了怒，怒得没有留意她的身子绷紧了、她的头扬起来了。照他看来，莫尔小姐不打一声招呼就离家出门，最不成体统不过；没有一个人知道她在哪儿；人人焦急万分，到处找过她了。

“不见得到处都找过，”汉娜说，微微含笑。王牌在她袖筒里，她就要亮出来了。“你只消到隔壁问一声，就能找到我，我在桑普森先生那里。”

“桑普森先生！莫尔小姐，我家里不论谁去看那个声名狼藉的老头子，我都极不以为然。”

===Original===

“Is he disreputable? I think he’s lonely, and he misses Mrs. Corder. She used to go and see him regularly, you know﻿—at least once a week,” Hannah said, and she went upstairs to lay away, like a magpie, her lace with her roll of Chinese silk, but she had not reached her room before her triumph left her. She had been unreasonably reprimanded and provoked, but was it worth while to hurt the man? Moreover, she had been hoping for this occasion and she was ashamed. She had rushed across the Downs to do these people a service because it was one she would enjoy, and, indulging herself again, she had done more harm than she had prevented. She had imperilled the peace and goodwill for which she had pretended to be so anxious and planted a little barb, from which the wound might fester, in the heart of a man about whom she had chosen to know nothing but the worst.

===Chinese===

“他声名狼藉么？我看他是寂寞，他想科德太太呢。她从前是按时去看他的，你知道——一星期至少一回，”汉娜说着，上楼去了，像一只喜鹊，要把她的花边同她那卷中国绸缎一并藏好；可是人还没到房里，胜利的滋味已经丢下她走了。她受了无理的申斥，受了撩拨；可是，叫这个人受一道伤，值得么？况且，她一直在巴望这么个机会，她害臊了。她奔过丘陵草原，是来给这些人效一份劳的，因为这份劳是她乐于效的；她又一次纵容了自己，结果种下的祸，比她挡下的祸还多。她口口声声那样系心的和睦与亲善，叫她亲手置于险境；她还在一个人的心里埋下一枚小小的倒钩，伤口说不定要在那里溃烂——而对于这个人，她偏偏存心只肯知道他最坏的一面。

## XXIX / 第二十九章

===Original===

After supper, one of those little rumours, which start mysteriously and surprise no one so much as the people to whom they refer, began running about Mrs. Spenser-Smith’s drawing-room. For a skilfully long time she pretended not to hear it, but the moment arrived, and it was hastened by Ernest’s clumsy kindness, when it was impossible to resist the knowledge that Mr. Pilgrim could recite. Solo performances were against her rule. She knew what sort of talent the members of the chapel possessed, its devastating effect on the gaiety of her parties and the little jealousies that could arise, but Mr. Pilgrim was a newcomer, invited, as a lonely bachelor, at Ernest’s particular desire, and though she doubted his ability and disliked singling him out for honour, she could not refuse the eager requests of those ladies, of all ages, who were thrilled by the presence of another and a new minister and begged her to let him recite, nor could she ignore Ernest’s open persuasions of the apparently reluctant Mr. Pilgrim. It was noticeable that no other male voice joined in the solicitations, and that even the thinnest-necked of the young men, to whom a minister was a natural object of veneration, put his back against the wall and settled his features into an expression he hoped they would be able to maintain.
